<a id="src-rule-message-parameter-grammar"></a>
#### The rule-message parameter grammar: paths, quoting, category, and Base Year, measured locally 2026-08-24

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command. Source basis read at a12-kernel `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`.
- `route`: at the measured revision, **`rule check` could not reach this surface at all** because it took no message, so the oracle was `rule add --dry-run`, which the tool documented as kernel-validated before and after and which therefore ran the rule error-text checker. Two dmtool-authored models share one root group with a nonrepeatable subgroup, a Number named `Amount` at **both** levels, a String named `For`, a String named `index`, and an Enumeration with one declared category; one declares Base Year 2020 and the other declares none. Thirty-one decisions vary only the message text, each with a condition that references exactly the fields its message names.
- `instrument-limit`: at that revision an error-text refusal at `rule add --dry-run` wrote **no result envelope**: stdout was empty, the verdict was a prose line on stderr carrying the Kernel's own bracketed class, and the exit code was 2. Every refusal below was therefore read from that line rather than from `diagnostics`. The [2026-08-23 reconciliation](#src-2026-08-23-reconciliation) records the later standard-envelope fix and read-only `rule check --message` route.
- `path-claim`: the parameter's entity spec is the shared path grammar, admitted in every form this fragment represents: a bare name in its own scope, a group-qualified relative path, an absolute path, a parent walk, and a parent walk with an explicit turning point. A **wrong** turning point is refused `INVALID_ENTITY` naming `..Nope`, which is the live control showing the turning point arbitrated rather than being ignored. The value suffix composes with a path.
- `path-claim`: two malformed shapes carry classes this project does not reproduce, in the same refusing direction. An absolute path with a single segment is not a parse failure but `INVALID_ROOT_GROUP` naming `/Amount`, so the absolute form's first segment is the **root group**. A bare `..` with no field is not a parse failure either but `INVALID_FIELD`, so it parses as an entity and then fails the reference check. An empty path segment *is* a parse failure, `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`.
- `quoting-claim`: the single-quote escape is load-bearing and this producer's exemption is real. A field named `For` is admitted as `$'For'$` and refused as `$For$` with `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` — a **parse** class, not a dedicated needs-quoting class, which is why this project maps its own class to none. A field named `index` is admitted **both** quoted and unquoted, which is the exemption's live separator on the same model. `$RuleGroup$` as the whole spec is refused `INVALID_PARAMETER`.
- `baseyear-claim`: the English terminal really is `BaseYear`. `$BaseYear$`, `$BaseYear+3$`, and `$BaseYear-1$` are admitted on the configured model and all refused `NO_BASE_YEAR` on the unconfigured one, so the model's Base Year is the whole gate. `$BaseYear*2$` is refused `MVK_LEXER_STANDARD_ERROR` naming the character and its position, and `$BaseYear+$` is refused `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` expecting a digit sequence, so the offset syntax is exactly a sign and digits. `$'BaseYear'$` is refused `INVALID_ENTITY`: quoting the terminal turns it into a field name, which confirms the escape mechanism from the other side.
- `baseyear-claim`: the German spelling is **not** a terminal in an English model. `$Referenzjahr$` is refused `INVALID_ENTITY` as an unknown group or field, so a consumer that reused the German spelling would resolve the wrong entity rather than interpolate the year.
- `category-claim`: all four category gates fire distinctly on one model. A declared name is admitted; an undeclared one is `INVALID_CATEGORY` naming both the category and the field; an empty name is `NO_CATEGORY`; and the suffix on a Number field is `FIELD_NO_ENUM`. The value suffix on the same Enumeration field is admitted, so the two suffixes are independent rather than one gating the other.
- `local-divergence`: the original doubled-arrow fixture used an undeclared first category and therefore observed `INVALID_CATEGORY`; the [2026-08-23 reconciliation](#src-2026-08-23-reconciliation) supplied the separating three-row matrix. A declared first category reaches the trailing-syntax `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, an undeclared one draws `INVALID_CATEGORY`, and a non-Enumeration field draws `FIELD_NO_ENUM`. The local checked parser now preserves the first suffix long enough to reproduce those gates. Combining the value suffix with an arrow, `$Status.value->Group$`, remains a parse failure at both, `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, which confirms the two suffixes are alternatives.
- `local-divergence`: a second, smaller one was found and fixed while writing this checkpoint: a parse refusal quoted the spec it had split from rather than the whole authored parameter, which would send an author looking at text they never wrote.
- `persistence`: one accepted candidate carrying **three** parameter forms in a single message — a relative path with the value suffix, an absolute path, and `$BaseYear+1$` — was persisted, read back byte-identical through `rule read`, and the model re-checked `valid:true`, `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`.
- `integrity`: configured model `499e5b59ad31d03861675081474e6122f2d4e69c0f2920b626b845f6d921155f` (Base Year 2020, the categorised Enumeration, and the persisted candidate) carries twenty-nine rows; unconfigured model `bbcfefe9fbe01add64a85d01831521b97ff50195d118efd0e5fcf1c16345542f` carries the two Base-Year-absent rows. Every retained row was re-run against the exact bytes named here after the last model edit, because an earlier state of the configured model had neither the Base Year nor the Enumeration. `rule add --dry-run` persists nothing, so these two model files are the whole retained input.
- `limit`: this **static grammar checkpoint itself** covers one locale, one nonrepeatable subgroup, and no runtime observation. Runtime rendering is separately measured at the [parameter-rendering checkpoint](#src-rule-message-parameter-rendering), while the [2026-08-23 reconciliation](#src-2026-08-23-reconciliation) adds the declared-first-category doubled-arrow row, labelled Enumeration value, minimum-fraction default control, bilingual matrix, and exact locale presentation table. Still unmeasured: a field literally named `BaseYear` beside the terminal, the offset's magnitude bound, the remaining non-field parameter forms, and a label-provider result.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral on one clause. It upgrades [SPEC-2026-08-24-01](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-24-01) from source-derived to locally measured; no new outbound entry.

<a id="src-rule-message-parameter-rendering"></a>
#### What a rule-message parameter renders, measured locally 2026-08-24

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime, recorded from the artifact header with `producer.source.state` `CLEAN`; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: the model of the [parameter-grammar checkpoint](#src-rule-message-parameter-grammar), extended with one label, one fractional Number, and six rules whose messages between them carry every parameter form this project represents, then **two** documents through `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` — one filling the Enumeration and the fractional field, one leaving both empty. Both codegen strategies produced **byte-identical** message lists on every row.
- `baseyear-claim`: the offset arithmetic is exactly the obvious one and the rendering is the plain decimal. Against a declared Base Year of 2020, `$BaseYear$` renders `2020`, `$BaseYear+3$` renders `2023`, and `$BaseYear-1$` renders `2019`, in one message. `$$` renders one `$` in the same message, so the escape survives beside real parameters.
- `category-claim`: a category access renders the **category token the field's current stored value maps to**, not the category's name and not the stored value. With unlabelled `Status` holding the first declared value `open`, `$Status->Group$` renders `A`, the `Group` token aligned with that value. `$Status.value$` in the same message renders the unlabelled fallback `open`, so the two suffixes read different things from one field. The [2026-08-23 reconciliation](#src-2026-08-23-reconciliation) adds the labelled-value separator.
- `name-claim`: an **unlabelled** field's `$Field$` renders its **short name** — `Amount` for `/Order/Amount` and `Status` for `/Order/Status` — on this route, which configures no label provider. A field carrying a declared label renders that label: `$Head/Amount$` renders `Head amount`. This narrows the canonical clause's last fallback, which says "the resolved identifier's path/index debug representation": on the kernel's own `validateFull` it is the short name. The route cannot distinguish "no provider" from "a provider returning the short name", so the claim is stated for this route rather than for the public service one.
- `value-claim`: a scale-0 Number renders bare — `$Head/Amount.value$` renders `7` — an **unfilled** String's value renders as the empty string, and an **unfilled** Number declaring two **minimum** fractional digits renders `0.00`, which is the canonical clause's stated per-format default reaching a real message on this route as well. The [2026-08-23 reconciliation](#src-2026-08-23-reconciliation) supplies the max-2/min-0 control that renders `0`.
- `empty-claim`: an **empty** Enumeration renders the empty string under **both** suffixes: `$Status->Group$` and `$Status.value$` each render nothing while `$Status$` still renders the short name in the same message. That was this project's own unmeasured default for the category access and is now the Kernel's.
- `polarity-claim`: the message's `type` channel separates the two polarities this project models. The rule whose condition carries a `FieldNotFilled` conjunct reports `OMISSION_ERROR` and lists both referenced fields in `fillToFix`; the three value-comparison rules report `VALUE_ERROR` with an empty `fillToFix`. That is a live cross-check of the omission/value distinction rather than a new rule.
- `local-divergence`: one, and it made the representation stronger. A category access had been represented as an opaque caller-supplied text run precisely because its rendering was unmeasured; it now renders through the declaration's own category mapping applied to the caller's stored token, so the policy sits in the theory instead of in the consumer. Its **empty** case then measured out to the same empty string this project had chosen, so that default stopped being a guess.
- `integrity`: model `342cb1338dec504ff6fe7a5f7db62848d228907537497eaffdf10a8723c8c8f1`; documents `f58245d871e7eef89694b06a237f199c3d4aca2b71fae543903e85d3f7b799fd` (filled) and `97f35aadf9cc7e2b7777b64b8ff06b196bf146539c4828fa8f9555132bc6cf59` (empty Enumeration and fraction); request `c79dbc7978c6c213c048bb92a4ca3e2016549d557c587224ef78f2d6d440a18e`; artifact `80c0f91d34bd697e6ecc44cbe495ff535fd8a0bb0b4d6bc0dbc1c3a4d0227f5c`. An earlier single-row run against a smaller model is superseded by these bytes and is not cited: the model gained the fractional field and two rules afterwards, so its hash no longer measures these rows.
- `limit`: one locale for *this* batch, one Enumeration with one category, no repeatable group, and no label provider. The [2026-08-23 reconciliation](#src-2026-08-23-reconciliation) separately closes the exact presentation map across ten locale tags and four presented kinds. A declared provider result including the empty one remains unreachable by this route rather than merely unmeasured. Not measured here: a stored value the category does not map (categories align one-to-one with values, so no such value is authorable), the group-index parameter, and every non-field form other than Base Year.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral on one clause. It adds the rendered rows to [SPEC-2026-08-24-01](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-24-01) and narrows the existing unlabelled-fallback sentence for this route; no new outbound entry.

<a id="src-time-datetime-stored-input"></a>
#### Stored Time and DateTime input causes, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime, `producer.source.state` `CLEAN`; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` over one dmtool-authored model holding one default-format TIME field and one default-format DATETIME field, across eleven documents that set both. Both codegen strategies agreed on every cell.
- `cause-claim`: **a Time field has exactly one formal cause.** A component of the wrong width and every out-of-range component alike report `datumFormatFalsch`, and no stored clock produces `datumFalsch` — a clock has no position in time to fall below a floor. So the formal-cause audit's prediction holds: Time reports through the Date codes and needs no code of its own, and it needs only the first of the two.
- `range-claim`: hour `24` is **not** an end-of-day spelling. `24:00:00` is refused exactly like `25:00:00`, while `23:00:00` and `23:59:59` are admitted, so the clock's upper bound is 23 and a consumer must not read hour 24 as the following midnight. `00:00:00` is admitted, so the lower bound is inclusive.
- `cause-claim`: **DateTime carries both causes and the same floor as Date.** Spelling and range failures report `datumFormatFalsch`, while `1583-10-15T00:00:00` reports `datumFalsch` and `1583-10-16T00:00:00` is admitted — the identical 1583-10-16 boundary. A space in place of the `'T'` literal is refused, so the separator is part of the format rather than whitespace tolerance.
- `local-divergence`: none. The measured lexical rule is exactly the authored Time literal's, which this project already implements, so the stored classifier reuses that decoder rather than agreeing with it by construction.
- `integrity`: model `f372df8a9f3a0ede468f3b562ba057af3c2ef0ec2ac75935eda1d265bba479a3`; requests `26bb893eef80148c6eccde00d98467bfb89eb3c0c532522404ba35b5086bfbcc` and `579becdb1f4329e1162ff5bca0cf14d80c9514aa5280b7d2c367eb5e07a700cc`; artifacts `98396929c145f1b10539da0386d604c261fb765928673ada5113a7f217fef4e3` and `31f04a2194fe2a376f17cc44e952606aad2fb896787c73eacafb1ef80412c94c`. The model was not edited between the two batches, so both rest on those bytes.
- `datetime-claim`: the date half is judged exactly as a stored Date is. An unreal `2024-02-30T00:00:00` is the *format* finding while the leap day `2024-02-29T00:00:00` is admitted, and a label with **no** separator at all is refused like the space one — so the separator is one required literal rather than optional punctuation. `23:59:59` is admitted, so the clock's upper boundary is inclusive here too.
- `limit`: each kind's default format only, one model zone, validation phase only. Not measured: a leap second, a Time or DateTime declaration carrying another vocabulary format, the interaction of a below-floor date with an out-of-range clock in one label, and what either kind stores or renders. Both halves now have Lean owners; the retained **instant** is not observable through validation output, so this project's zone account for it is internal.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral on one clause. Recorded as `SPEC-2026-08-23-11`.

<a id="src-temporal-format-vocabulary"></a>
#### The temporal format gate is a kind-independent vocabulary, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: structured `field add` through `apply`, whose terminal gate is the real kernel model check, against one dmtool-authored model. Each row declares one temporal field with one format string and is read back with `field read` to confirm the string persisted as written rather than being normalized.
- `vocabulary-claim`: **the format gate checks the string against a fixed vocabulary and never against the field's kind.** `HH:mm:ss` is admitted on DATE and on DATETIME, `yyyy-MM-dd` and `dd.MM.yyyy` on TIME, the fragment format `MM` on TIME, and the ISO datetime format on DATE — every one read back carrying exactly the declared string. So a kind/format mismatch is **authorable in every direction**, not only in the one degenerate DateTime case the canonical clause already records.
- `vocabulary-claim`: the negative half is what makes it a vocabulary rather than an absence of checking. Nine strings are refused with the same model-level `The date format is invalid`, including several that look natural for the kind that offered them: `HH:mm`, `H:mm:ss`, `HH:mm:ss.SSS`, `hh:mm:ss a`, `HHmmss`, and `HH-mm-ss` on TIME, and `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm`, `dd.MM.yyyy HH:mm:ss`, and `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.SSS` on DATETIME. **A TIME field is refused `HH:mm` and admitted `yyyy-MM-dd`**, which is the sharpest cell: the gate rejects a well-formed time pattern from its own family and accepts a date pattern from another.
- `default-claim`: each kind's *default* is single: TIME defaults to `HH:mm:ss` and DATETIME to `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`. An earlier reading of the refusals concluded that each kind therefore *admits* only its default; the cross-admission rows above refute that, and the distinction matters because the peer's own report is phrased as "appears to admit exactly one format".
- `datetime-claim`: **DateTime has exactly one declarable storage format**, `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`. The day-first `dd.MM.yyyy'T'HH:mm:ss` spelling is refused outright, verified through the identical authoring path that admits the ISO one. Read at a12-kernel `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, both strings exist in kernel source but in different roles: the ISO one is the field-type constant's DateTime default, while the day-first one lives in the **parser's** date utilities and tags DateTime-valued *expressions* — the point-in-time operation, the `DateTime(...)` constructor, and a DSL constant. So it is a real kernel format string that names no declarable format. Unlike Date, which genuinely has two declarable spellings, DateTime has one.
- `local-divergence`: one, and this measurement caught it. On the kind side there is none: this project already carries `kind` and `components` independently and its policy gate never constrains the format string against the kind, so that mismatch is representable and admitted exactly as measured. But the checked DateTime **target** vocabulary admitted the day-first spelling, so it certified declarations the kernel rejects — a false acceptance, inviting because the Date sibling really does take two spellings. Narrowed to the measured single format, with the day-first string's true role recorded at its declaration. Seven conformance modules had built their DateTime fixtures on that unauthorable format and were migrated to the ISO one; one of them used it as a *different-format* control, which now uses the degenerate clock format instead, since with one legal DateTime format a differently formatted DateTime declaration would otherwise have no authorable witness.
- `integrity`: model `3d0a37786d9737abb8c9779b901d4ba989c1c05e917d3b184ec5b8e65ad53ca4` after the admitted rows committed. Each refused row was re-run **alone** against that state, because an atomic `apply` rolls back and a rejected batch otherwise reports a name collision from a previously committed sibling rather than the format verdict under test — the first pass of this matrix read three such collisions as format refusals.
- `limit`: static admission only, one model, and one group. Not measured: whether the vocabulary is exactly the union of the four kinds' own formats or a wider fixed list, what a mismatched declaration *stores* and *renders*, and which operand positions admit each mismatch — the canonical clause already characterizes that for the degenerate DateTime and nothing here widens it.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral. Recorded as `SPEC-2026-08-23-10`.

<a id="src-partial-date-input-causes"></a>
#### Which formal cause a partially known Date draws, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime, `producer.source.state` `CLEAN`, artifact schema 1; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` over one dmtool-authored model declaring five `yyyy-MM-dd` DATE fields that differ **only** in precision — full, `DAY_OPTIONAL`, `MONTH_OPTIONAL`, `YEAR_OPTIONAL`, and one more `DAY_OPTIONAL` with the pre-1900 check enabled. Seventeen documents set every field to the same stored text, so one row reads the whole precision ladder at one spelling. Both codegen strategies agreed on every cell.
- `ladder-claim`: the precision ladder is exactly monotone and each step adds exactly one omission depth. A full value is admitted everywhere; an omitted day is admitted from `DAY_OPTIONAL` up; an omitted month **and** day from `MONTH_OPTIONAL` up; all three omitted only at `YEAR_OPTIONAL`. So the declared precision names the deepest admitted suffix rather than a set of independently enabled omissions.
- `cause-claim`: **the two causes do not split where a reimplementer would put them.** A *full-precision* declaration reports every omission marker as `datumFormatFalsch` — an omission is not a legal spelling for it at all — while a *partial* declaration reports every shape it cannot hold as `datumFalsch`. So the code is chosen by whether the declaration admits any omission, not by which rule the value broke.
- `cause-claim`: within a partial declaration the split is **reality against position in time**, and it is not "spelling against semantics". `datumFormatFalsch` covers a wrong component width and an unreal calendar date among the value's *present* components — including an impossible month standing beside a legally omitted day, which is the sharpest cell: the omission is fine, the month is not, and the month's cause wins. `datumFalsch` covers a zero pattern that is not a monotone suffix, a suffix deeper than the precision admits, and an admitted value whose earliest completion lies below the universal Gregorian floor or the enabled pre-1900 boundary.
- `boundary-claim`: the floor applies to the **earliest completion** of a partial value and yields the date cause, not the format cause. An omitted-day `1583-10-00` is refused while `1583-11-00` on the same declaration is admitted, so the boundary rather than the year is doing the work; enabling the pre-1900 check refuses `1583-11-00` and `1899-12-00` on that same field while `1900-01-00` stays admitted. The two thresholds are therefore indistinguishable from the cause alone and separable only from the declaration.
- `local-divergence`: one, found by this measurement and fixed before the capsule landed. The first implementation obtained an omitted-day value through a constructor that applies the floor internally, so a below-floor completion came back as "no value" and was reported as the *format* cause. Measured, it is the date cause. The classifier now judges reality, then position in time, then precision, in that order, and the constructor's failure can mean nothing but unreality. Thirty-five of thirty-six cells had already agreed, which is exactly why the row mattered: the disagreement was one cell wide and on the branch a reader is least likely to re-derive.
- `format-claim`: the rule is **independent of the declared spelling**. Four `dd.MM.yyyy` fields at the same four precisions reproduce every structural cell: the monotone ladder, the uniform format error from the full-precision declaration, the non-suffix refusal at the deepest precision, the unreal month beside an omitted day as a format error, and the below-floor omitted day as a date error. So the omission marker is the zero *component* rather than a position in the ISO text.
- `integrity`: two model states, because the model grew between batches. Batches one to three rest on `7e82b99a6206ae3229b8ee518572950f1e8e4989ca307bec62134a1c4155aea0`, with requests `0bce7db93cdbacf4c8d22ba9790d74f1b7bfecd06ed9f28b51614afd640d1915`, `a5195bb5a4c69da53686fc0ec0262740c460dd45423415e0bbf6144c74866545`, and `044f7b6c4e798f6c80ab3afdd141acd129c2db32af79a014584c67672aa3b276` and artifacts `afdc034cc270ab7949f8a90b62adb62a126043d3d69c7f737884b7b78c7f3c9b`, `235fc56f2ca8ad51889a1064689967d5ea166a40be5b1a4b31afe26f9d323368`, and `0829d6cfd218e9866a4f9e063ee138a1c0a5da836c26122baacf1cf843288f3a`; the pre-1900 field was declared after the first two ran, so both were re-run against those exact bytes and came back byte-identical. The `dd.MM.yyyy` batch rests on the later state `48783be9582c9f9381c483e3f273ee368994f5e494d49aaaae816d771ad5fc89`, request `73b5ef190bafecfbbfd73298b0d38536c5b87aae424c00ceaf457235db9637d5`, artifact `f389c41444b7d8fa9c62c399df95ed203eac9b8e4ad3aff3d9b8ad91e183a919`. The rendering batch rests on a third state `fd5c04e650c56d5ab1c71cac4b09cc9457576debb30688cc843190d9a45a3bc3`, which adds the two message-bearing rules, request `38a23fbcf7cc4e960f27207711dc874ed485aa1678f989bf6d698a7e7d7e2d43`, artifact `7d512f6ed3518f43bbd7b02221ce8fa408b2ac86a028323b0f0508483ac1f35c`. The de_DE comparison uses a separate single-purpose model `8ab80f98713f0929b393f83854392bf4a20dd5aa62835a6517e4e96578250461`, request `395ed34206fdcb64c83923d531680faa9355ae71f9408e63216c2ef7b4154a0f`, artifact `ba5002414dfa3946d58ccda4b30eb772760a9087112aa4473a8b1ad826ea9b37`, declaring the same `yyyy-MM-dd` precision so only the message locale differs. No batch was re-run against a later state and none is claimed for one.
- `render-claim`: **a partially known value keeps its zero markers when interpolated**, so what it renders is its stored spelling rather than either boundary. Two rules whose messages carry `$Field$` and `$Field.value$` for a day-optional and a year-optional declaration render `06/00/2024` for stored `2024-06-00`, `00/00/2024` for `2024-00-00`, and `00/00/0000` for `0000-00-00`, beside `06/15/2024` for a full value. A consumer that completed the value for display would print `06/01/2024` for the first of those, which is the earliest boundary rather than the text. The rendered **pattern** follows the **message locale**, and is neither the declared format nor fixed: one unchanged `yyyy-MM-dd` declaration renders `06/15/2024` under an en_US message and `15.06.2024` under a de_DE one, with the partial value tracking it as `06/00/2024` and `00.06.2024`. So the zero marker is attached to its *component* and moves with that component's position, which is the same conclusion the two-input-format rows reach from the other side.
- `limit`: both declared input formats, two message locales, one model zone, and validation phase only. Not measured: a partial DATE inside a repeatable group, a third locale or a locale whose pattern is not one of these two, and every interaction with `ValueAsDate` beyond admission. An earlier version of this checkpoint recorded the rendered form as unobservable through validation output; that was wrong, and the `render-claim` above is what a rule message exposes.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral on two clauses. The admission and cause rules are `SPEC-2026-08-23-08`; the rendered stored spelling is `SPEC-2026-08-23-09`.

<a id="src-date-decoded-identity-witness"></a>
#### Whether two stored Date texts can decode to one instant, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime, `producer.source.state` `CLEAN`, artifact schema 1; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` on six documents over one dmtool-authored model — a repeatable `/Order/Rows` declaring a default-format `yyyy-MM-dd` DATE, plus a root String carrying three rules whose conditions read the starred Date column: `NumberOfDifferentValues(...) == 1`, `== 2`, and `FieldValuesNotUnique(...)`. Both codegen strategies agreed on every row.
- `identity-claim`: the decoded Date identity is the **instant alone**. Read at a12-kernel `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, the runtime's decoded-Date equality compares only its wrapped `java.util.Date` and ignores the not-given flag, the not-check-relevant flag, and the value's origins; distinctness itself is hash-set membership over that equality. So distinct-count Date identity is decoded-instant identity, and it is format- and spelling-insensitive where stored-text uniqueness is not.
- `reachability-claim`: **the divergence between those two identities has no witness under this declaration.** Two distinct stored texts decoding to one instant is the only shape that separates them, and every candidate is refused before it can be stored. An unpadded `2020-2-1` beside `2020-02-01` is the formal error `datumFormatFalsch` ("Only dates in the format YYYY-MM-DD are allowed") rather than a lenient parse, and so is a day overflow `2020-02-30` and a month overflow `2020-13-01`, so the parse neither pads nor normalizes an out-of-range component. The refusal is per cell: the document carrying the overflow twice reports it at both `Order/Rows/D` and `Order/Rows[2]/D`. This is a **negative** result and it closes the question for this declaration rather than deferring it.
- `control`: both controls are live on the same model and rules, which is what makes the negative result readable. Two identical texts fire the one-distinct rule **and** the uniqueness rule together; two genuinely different valid dates fire the two-distinct rule and neither of the others. So the instrument does separate the two identities when a document lets it, and the witness rows are silent because the cell is malformed, not because the rules cannot speak.
- `suppression-claim`: a malformed cell in the starred column suppresses the aggregate rather than being skipped: on both overflow rows neither the one-distinct nor the two-distinct rule fires, only the formal error. That agrees with the already-canonical combiner suppression account and is recorded as a live cross-check, not as a new rule.
- `integrity`: model `534120cf08fa8ffd15bc081f10f062c09a060df355492faf08385bf5f7beaa6e`; documents `ae183950ba7f87d4e3f9d13c1d0eefc7c281ea0efffc0e9136685ea4ff530b5e` (unpadded beside padded), `d9e89d17cbb58f4f9f4d0f5448c31293626bd540e833a25e404d2822a99bda5a` (identical texts), `7fae8119cb5b75325ab3d6bcb2b9e977c424a114b02e26548ddab094f613721b` (two valid dates), `65fddf3fd6ee730e39a126d5674443feb3b8a5ff53a16c34c55db226c6c9ab8c` (day overflow), `467dc8c5ff81978c2150e77932fd0f459d3b76a4176efb10eb7fe9b69e002e26` (month overflow), and `79b6ba6f099ce1cd7d03617375e6a455916b2174d5a3903b6e0fc7568e6d6e0a` (day overflow twice); requests `aae86f0d8e3af671a12664e6a1e69fe0be446e2f192f703e753ecbdc6678e3c6` and `551880285af3827b5e2a48dfd44a65f760e77f339ec89d14e6d3d96c8848f4d5`; artifacts `2df610ac6dea8ee49b0b83cf2798a5e841cf78bac143f72f9dca19e24c323007` and `b18f002540cd4659b5fb344314e3471b9a9b81f98a0a7d75f9c5cb4c03277305`.
- `limit`: one DATE declaration in its default `yyyy-MM-dd` format, one repeatable level, two rows, validation phase only. A differently declared DATE format is unmeasured, as are Time, DateTime, and DateRange columns; the identity reading above is source-established for all of them, but only this format's reachability is measured. Nothing here claims what a *non-Date* distinct count does, and the not-given and not-relevant flags' exclusion from equality is source-only, because a value carrying them is never inserted into the distinctness set.
- `sync`: none. Every row confirms an already-canonical clause — the non-lenient stored-conversion clause in [`spec/05`](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md) and the combiner suppression account — so this checkpoint adds provenance and closes two local residuals without changing `spec/`.

<a id="src-temporal-aggregate-gate"></a>
#### Which temporal format gate each aggregate carrier uses, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: `rule add --dry-run` over one model declaring all five component-omitting Date formats, both complete spellings, a Base-Year variant, and two repeatable groups — one holding two differently-spelled DATE fields, one holding two matching. Twenty-two decisions across three batches, every verdict `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`.
- `design`: the whole measurement turns on **equal component sets under different format spellings**, because that is the only place a component-set reading and a format-spelling reading disagree. `yyyy-MM-dd` beside `dd.MM.yyyy`, and `yyyy-MM` beside `yyyyMM`, are those pairs. Every admission is paired with its own same-format control, so an admission is not merely the absence of a gate.
- `aggregate-claim`: **`MaxValue`, `MinValue`, and `NumberOfDifferentValues` read the component set.** All three admit both equal-set-different-spelling pairs, and `MaxValue`/`MinValue` do so at three loci alike: an explicit nonrepeatable field list, explicit starred fields, and a group operand's expansion. Sets that genuinely differ are refused `MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE`, which names both formats.
- `uniqueness-claim`: **`FieldValuesNotUnique` reads the declared format string.** It refuses both equal-set pairs, and also two differently-spelled yearless formats, against a same-format control it admits. Its code is `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED` and the message text names "the same dateformat", so the two gates are distinguishable by code as well as by verdict.
- `base-year-claim`: a declared Base Year lifts the aggregate refusal for a yearless operand **only when the remaining components already agree**: `MM` with `yyyy-MM` and `MM-dd` with a complete date are admitted, while both stay refused against a year-only operand. Supplementation therefore precedes the set comparison.
- `strictness-claim`: the aggregate gate is strictly stronger than the direct-comparison gate, measured on four pairs — `yyyy` compares with `yyyy-MM` and with a complete date but aggregates with neither. This project already states that implication as a theorem rather than a case.
- `excluded`: `Sum` is refused on temporal operands outright, `MVK_NO_NUMBER`, and takes no part in this gate. `FirstFilledValue` was not measured here.
- `instrument-note`: one row was discarded and re-run because the rule code `Y2` collided with a field of that name, refused `RK_NAME_EXISTS` before the kernel saw it. An instrument refusal carries no `valid` and must never be read as a kernel verdict.

<a id="src-omitting-date-formats"></a>
#### What a component-omitting DATE format stores, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; artifact header records `producer.source.state: CLEAN`. The sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: `apply` declared one DATE field for each of the five component-omitting formats, all admitted `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. Then `:adapter:kernelProbe` observed `validateFull` on both codegen strategies over eleven documents. **No rules were needed:** a formal input failure surfaces in `validateFull` messages on its own, so each row's message list is the classifier's verdict directly. Artifact SHA-256 `736f52be07821b936003342af3d1f4c06e46ebe84f2ce78183f2a91ad6f53e5e`, held in session scratch; the account it settles is carried by executable cases.
- `declaration-claim`: all five — `yyyy`, `yyyy-MM`, `yyyyMM`, `MM`, `MM-dd` — are legal DATE formats beside the two complete ones, and their canonical texts `2020`, `2020-06`, `202006`, `06`, `06-15` are all accepted in one clean row.
- `cause-claim`: **one cause covers every failure, and the second temporal cause never appears.** A short year `20`, a year carrying extra components `2020-06`, an out-of-range `2020-13`, a single-digit `6` for `MM`, and an unreal `02-30` for `MM-dd` all report `datumFormatFalsch`, and no row reports the position-in-time code. These formats carry no complete date whose position could fall below the floor, which is the structural difference from complete-format input.
- `separator-claim`: the separator is exact **in both directions**, which is what keeps the two year-month spellings from collapsing into one lenient parser: `yyyy-MM` refuses `202006` and `yyyyMM` refuses `2020-06`, each against the other's accepted canonical row.
- `yearless-claim`: **a yearless day is bounded by its month's greatest possible day, February included at 29.** A second request of ten rows measured the whole boundary on both sides: `04-31` refused against `04-30` admitted, `01-31` admitted, `02-29` admitted against `02-30` refused. So the day is not resolved against any particular year's February, which is the `yearlessLastDay` rule the overlap owner already states. The same request measured the remaining perturbations under one cause: a month carrying an extra component `06-15`, a zero month `00`, a short month `6-15`, a short day `06-5`, a zero day `06-00`, a missing separator `0615`, and an out-of-range `13-01`. Second artifact SHA-256 `8440fed8c1a6c8e180d1bed6c96c8f9d7f7459f96838ed1d952753ce78e48734`.
- `empty-claim`: an all-empty document is clean, so empty stored text is present and value-free on every one of these formats rather than a spelling failure.

<a id="src-filled-field-count"></a>
#### What a formally invalid operand does to `NumberOfFilledFields`, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; artifact header records `producer.source.state: CLEAN` and the same revision. The sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` on both codegen strategies over one model declaring a String `A`, a `yyyy-MM-dd` Date `B`, a Number, and a String note, carrying **three** persisted rules that compare `NumberOfFilledFields(A, B)` against `0`, `1`, and `2`. Artifact SHA-256 `8ca06c74f9828271c393ef7010de490002345f16247a2638d4d1f7c3a0469a2e`, held in session scratch rather than retained under `evidence/`, because the account it settles is now carried by executable cases.
- `design`: three comparisons rather than one, because a single rule's non-firing cannot distinguish "the count differs" from "the count is unavailable". With all three present, each row's firing names the count itself, and a row that fires **nothing** is the unavailability.
- `invalid-claim`: **a formally invalid operand makes the whole count unavailable.** With `A` filled and `B` holding the unreal date `2024-02-30`, the Kernel reported `datumFormatFalsch` for `B` and fired **none** of the three rules. Skipping the invalid operand would have counted `1` and fired the first; treating it as filled would have counted `2` and fired the second. Both accounts are therefore refuted, not merely unconfirmed.
- `empty-claim`: the three other rows are each other's controls and each fires exactly the rule naming its own count: filled-beside-empty fires the `1` rule, both-filled the `2` rule, and **both-empty the `0` rule**. So an empty cell is never counted, and an all-empty list still compares normally rather than answering unavailable — emptiness and invalidity are two states in this operator.
- `computation-claim`: the count needs **no computation-arm rule of its own**. A second request added `observe: ["compute"]` and a computation assigning the same count to a Number target. With the invalid operand present the target reports `cleared: true, errored: false` and `formalErrorsInOperands` names it, so the general operand-poison rule of [`09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md) fires before the count is evaluated. The group sibling needs two arms for a **structural** reason instead: an invalid *descendant* leaves its group operand present, so that count must decide what to do with it.
- `route-trap`: **`computations.outcomes` reports changes, not state.** The same invalid row observed with the target already *empty* produced `outcomes: []` — no entry for the target at all — because clearing an empty field changes nothing. Read that way, the row looks like a computation that never ran; only re-running it with a stale value in the target exposes the `cleared: true` outcome. So an absent outcome never establishes that a computation was suppressed, and any clearing claim on this route needs a pre-filled target.
- `limit`: the group-scope operand this count admits, and the rest of the count family, were not measured.

<a id="src-date-from-datetime"></a>
#### What `DateFromDateTime` reads, and the source gate both component extractors share, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime. The runtime artifact's own header records `producer.source.state: CLEAN` and the same revision; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: two routes, one question each. Static admission through `rule add --dry-run` over a model declaring a complete DateTime `Moment`, a degenerate time-only DateTime `Clock`, two Date fields, and a String. The runtime reading through `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` on both codegen strategies. Artifact SHA-256 `7496cf9f3db4d2e1d65a8e46b145c755396882c36f98a27e338ce88679f2ecf7`, held in the session scratch rather than retained under `evidence/`, because the account it settles is now carried by executable cases.
- `reading-claim`: **the extractor reads the source label's own date components.** Two competing rules were authored in one model, one asserting the extraction equals a Date field holding the wall date and one asserting it equals a field holding the instant's UTC date. On a `2024-06-15T00:30:00` label the **wall** rule fired and the UTC rule did not, on both strategies. A midday row fired the wall rule too, establishing that the time is dropped rather than compared. A negative control whose two Date fields match neither reading fired nothing, so a firing is informative rather than automatic.
- `zone-limit`: the model zone was **ambient, not controlled**. dmtool exposes no zone setting, and the host zone at measurement was `CEST +0200`, recorded rather than chosen. So this refutes a wall-to-UTC conversion *at that offset*; it does not separate "reads the stored label" from "treats the stored label as already in the model zone", which are the same function on this route. A retained-instant claim is not measurable here at all, because validation output carries no instant.
- `kind-claim`: **the result is a Date, established by refusals rather than by an admission.** It is admitted against a Date field, against `Today`, against another `DateFromDateTime`, and as the Date operand of `AddDays`. It is refused against `Now` and against a `TimeFromDateTime` result, both `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`. An admission alone would not distinguish a Date result from a DateTime one that happens to compare well.
- `source-claim`: **one gate serves both component extractors, and it is "complete DateTime".** The degenerate time-only DateTime is refused `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` at `DateFromDateTime`, which §5 already recorded, and so is a **plain Date field** with the same class and locus, which it did not. So the requirement is not "the format retains a date".
- `syntax-claim`: the operand is a bare path; `DateFromDateTime([Moment])` is `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` at the bracket. `Day(DateFromDateTime(Moment))` is a parse failure, the same narrower-operand-grammar limit already recorded for `ValueAsDate`. Incidental but needed to author the rest: condition equality is `==`, not `=`; the operator catalog's `keyword` column is the authority and `=` is `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`.
- `persistence`: both separator rules were persisted with `rule add` and the model left `valid: true` under `model check` before the runtime probe ran, so the probe observed a kernel-consistent model rather than a candidate.

<a id="src-date-from-datetime-computation"></a>
#### `DateFromDateTime` scalar computation reaches the full-Date target channels, measured locally 2026-08-24

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `cd43c249a88874bf5688a45060e208a0dcd20782`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the artifact records `producer.source.state: CLEAN`, and the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: source-shipped dmtool authored and persisted one computation from complete DateTime `/Appointment/ScheduledAt` to full Date `/Appointment/OnSiteDate`; `computation add --dry-run` and the persisted add both returned `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`, and `model check` remained valid. `:adapter:kernelProbe` observed `validateFull` plus `compute` over four documents on dynamic Groovy and generated Java. Request SHA-256 `e5990bd5bb43f86c837fdffa9ce7f47afbccd0b466cd6da6a5b2a9b3506d9ab3`, persisted-model SHA-256 `0bd9f2b0f1840c657b0bc61d25c155854d7f94a032c8bc1a3d2317ed2deb9c44`, and artifact SHA-256 `6d88a465bf8e67befdb57f41f92151a7c1d75d65cded4a0d83c7c92625240bdf`; all stayed in session scratch rather than under `evidence/`.
- `value-claim`: a declared `Europe/Berlin` source of `2024-06-15T00:30:00` computes stored Date `2024-06-15`, and a midday source computes the same date. The target started at `2024-06-14`, so both outcomes are visible changes rather than source-identical omissions. This closes the scalar target presentation against a UTC-day projection as well as a no-op account.
- `no-value-claim`: a present-empty source clears the prefilled target with `errored: false`; malformed source text also clears it, while `datumFormatFalsch` remains visible in both full validation and `formalErrorsInOperands`. Thus clean no-value and reached formal poison remain distinct source outcomes even though the public target action is a clear in both rows.
- `agreement`: both Kernel strategies agree on every message and computation channel, and read-only `dmtool model compute` produces the same two values and two clears on the interpreter route.
- `assurance-limit`: this is an exact local observation, not `L` or `C`: no maintained peer test owns the computation matrix, and the artifact is not retained through a typed local projection. It changes no canonical clause and requests no a12-dmkits reconciliation.

- `assurance-limit`: this is exact for a direct complete-DateTime source at the target scope or one measured ancestor scope, across one- and two-level target scopes.
