<a id="src-value-as-date-locus"></a>
#### Where a partial-Date `ValueAsDate` operand may be read, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: one model carrying a repeatable `/Order/Rows` (max 4), a `DAY_OPTIONAL` `Rows/Partial`, a nonrepeatable `DAY_OPTIONAL` `/Order/PartialTop`, and a full-precision `/Order/Limit`. Seven `rule add --dry-run` decisions vary the rule's error field, and therefore its iteration scope, against a fixed operand shape. Every verdict here is `verification: KERNEL_CONFIRMED`; a *condition* refusal from this verb does produce an envelope, so these were read structurally rather than off stderr.
- `locus-claim`: **admission is decided by the reading rule's iteration scope, not by the operand's spelling.** The persisted rule normalises a bare relative operand to its absolute path, so the identical condition text `ValueAsDate(/Order/Rows/Partial, FirstDay) > [/Order/Limit]` is admitted with error field `/Order/Rows/Partial` — rule group `/Order/Rows` — and refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` ("Missing specification of an asterisk for 'Rows'") with error field `/Order/Note`, rule group `/Order`. The outer partner may be reached by absolute bracket `[/Order/Limit]` or by parent walk `[../Limit]`; both are admitted, so the spelling of the *non*-partial operand is not the gate either.
- `wildcard-claim`: a **starred** operand is refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`, reported at the operator — "At 'ValueAsDate': It is not allowed to use an asterisk" — so no iteration scope admits one. This is a separate gate from the group-scope refusal §7 already records for the same operator, and it was measured on its own rather than carried over.
- `type-claim`: a bare partial-Date reference outside the operation is refused `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`, "At 'Field value operation.': It is not allowed to use a partly known date", quoting the field. That is the refusal `ValueAsDate` exists to bridge.
- `syntax-claim`: incidental to the above but needed to author any of it. Ordinary field references in a condition are **bracketed**; the `ValueAsDate` operand is a **bare** path, and bracketing it is `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` at the `[`. The error field must appear in the condition, and a bare operand inside `ValueAsDate` **counts** as that reference — the three admitted rows use `/Order/Rows/Partial` as their error field and never reach `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`.
- `persistence`: the admitted subject was written with `rule add`, read back structurally as `converted: true` with its condition normalised to the absolute operand path and its `errorField` intact, and left the whole model `valid: true` under `model check`.
- `limit`: **static legality only, and one carrier only.** The direct comparison was measured; the Date-addition, Date-difference, and `DateTime(...)` Date-operand carriers were not, and §7's own finding forbids transferring a locus verdict between carriers here. Nothing was measured about what a bound operand evaluates to per row.

<a id="src-rule-message-semantic-index"></a>
#### The semantic-index suffix on a rule-message parameter, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: the model of the [parameter-grammar checkpoint](#src-rule-message-parameter-grammar), extended with a repeatable `/Order/Rows` (max 4) declaring `Key` and `Payload`, plus a nonrepeatable `/Order/Wanted` to serve as a field-valued key. Eleven `rule add --dry-run` decisions vary the message parameter and the condition together; the same instrument limit applies, so each refusal is read from the stderr line and its bracketed class.
- `subject-claim`: **the gate's subject is the semantic index, not the field.** `$Rows/Key For "k1"$` is admitted against a condition of `FieldNotFilled(Rows/Payload For "k1")`, in which `Rows/Key` never appears — so a keyed parameter may name a field the condition does not reference at all, provided the condition keys that group by that key. The Kernel's own refusal text says exactly this ("A semantic index `Rows/Payload For "k1"` can be used in an error text only if it is used in the rule condition in the same way"), but only the admitted row isolates the subject, because every refusing row quotes a field *and* a key together.
- `membership-claim`: condition membership therefore belongs to the **unkeyed** form alone. The unkeyed `$Rows/Payload$` against that same keyed condition is refused `INVALID_FIELD` naming the field as referenced wrongly or unused, which is the ordinary unkeyed gate rather than an index class. The pairing is exact in both directions: a plain-read condition refuses the keyed parameter `MVK_INDEX_FOR_ERROR_TEXT_INVALID`, and so does a condition keyed by a *different* literal.
- `admission-claim`: three further shapes are admitted, each `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. A literal key matching the condition's; a **field-valued** key, `For Wanted`, where the condition keys the same group by that same field; and the **value suffix before the key**, `$Rows/Payload.value For "k1"$`.
- `grammar-claim`: the suffix precedes the key and nothing else does. `$Rows/Payload For "k1".value$` is refused `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` naming the `.`, so a suffix after the key is a parse failure rather than a differently-ordered spelling. An **unquoted** key, `For k1`, is refused `INVALID_ENTITY` naming `k1` as an invalid group or field: an unquoted key is a field reference that failed to resolve, not a token that failed to lex, which is what makes the quoted and field spellings the only two. A key that is itself keyed is refused `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX`, quoting the **inner** index rather than the whole parameter.
- `composition-claim`: the key composes with the **category** suffix. `$Status->Group For "k1"$` on a plainly-referenced Enumeration with a declared category is refused `MVK_INDEX_FOR_ERROR_TEXT_INVALID` — the index gate, reached with the category itself valid — so the key neither consumes nor displaces the suffix.
- `local-divergence`: one, and it corrected a wrong gate before it shipped. The first implementation read the refusal texts alone and gated a keyed parameter on whether the condition referenced *that field* through that key, because every refusing row named a field. The admitted `subject-claim` row shows the subject is the index, and the `membership-claim` row shows the field's own membership is not required at all. Since a semantic index needs a repeatable group and the modeled message fragment is nonrepeatable, that gate could never be posed there: the fragment now decodes a keyed parameter and refuses it as outside its own boundary, and deliberately maps that refusal to **no** Kernel class rather than to the pairing class it cannot evaluate. The measured grammar in front of the gate — well-formedness, suffix order, key spelling, nesting, category composition — is modeled exactly and separately locked.
- `integrity`: model `dec9bb424bc201ec63dd88809ab3e24b3258ba935159e535362fdceec869df44` after the index additions `25ff01889c61a2b27ff354b4d97402904bc94dd7f033f121bb6974e7858b3b2a`; decision batches `65522f1adf5ce909cec60fb5fd0664ce38d1d6efa630d070de51a5894ef05758` (seven rows) and `98504a9378a184efc569bc73402728b55c3bb64418184eed37e02c3e9e246fd9` (four rows). `rule add --dry-run` persists nothing, so every row rests on those model bytes. All eleven rows were re-run against this exact state when the checkpoint was written, rather than transcribed from the first run.
- `limit`: one repeatable level, one index field, validation phase only, and static admission only — nothing here is a **rendering** claim, so what a keyed parameter interpolates at runtime is unmeasured. Also unmeasured: whether a resolvable key field the condition never names is admitted (the measured field-valued row keys by a field the condition also carries), and which class a parameter violating the index gate *and* a category gate together draws.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral. Recorded on [SPEC-2026-08-24-01](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-24-01) with the two grammar checkpoints it extends.

<a id="src-date-range-endpoint-shapes"></a>
#### Which operand shapes a DateRange endpoint admits, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: two dmtool-authored models, both `model check` valid, each with a repeatable group (max 4) holding a `yyyy-MM-dd`+slash `DATE_RANGE` and an index field, plus a scalar range and an outside key field at the root. One declares a **String** index field, the other a **Number** one. Twenty `rule check` decisions vary the operand shape, the key form, and the locus.
- `wildcard-claim`: a **starred** endpoint operand is refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` at an iterating locus *and* at a nonrepeatable one, so the class does not depend on the locus and no wildcard form of this operand exists. A **group** operand is refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`. The unstarred row endpoint at an iterating locus and the scalar endpoint at a nonrepeatable one are the admitted controls on the same model.
- `index-claim`: a semantic-index selection **is** admitted as a DateRange operand, in both key forms and on four carriers — an endpoint comparison, an endpoint's numeric component, the overlap predicate, and stored range equality. It is admitted at a nonrepeatable locus, at an iterating one, and when the keyed group is the very group being iterated.
- `index-claim`: the key's **placement** has its own class. A field-valued key read from inside the indexed group is refused `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_CONTAINED_IN_INDEX`, against a live control on the same model where the same lookup keyed by a literal is admitted from that same iterating locus.
- `index-claim`: the literal key is a quoted token whatever the index field's kind. Against the **Number** index model, `For 2` is refused `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` on three carriers while `For "2"` is admitted, and a Number field key is admitted unquoted as a reference. So the refusal for an unquoted numeric literal is a resolution failure, not the declaration-format class an ill-fitting quoted literal draws.
- `runtime-route`: three rules are then persisted on the String-index model — a keyed overlap against the scalar range, an iterating keyed presence rule, and a **guarded non-iterating** keyed presence rule whose error field is the scalar range — and `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` on both codegen strategies over seven documents. Both strategies produced byte-identical message lists on every document.
- `runtime-claim`: the keyed read is a phase observation whose four interesting states are separated by the presence rule, which fires on empty and stays silent on unavailable. **No matching row and a matched-but-empty cell both read empty**: the presence rule fires and the overlap stays silent on each. **A duplicated key and a formally invalid matched cell both read unavailable**: the presence rule stays silent, the overlap stays silent, and only the offending cell's own class is reported — `uniqueIndex` on each participating index cell for the duplicate, `datumBereichNichtGueltig` on the range for the invalid one. So this carrier's result domain is exactly the ordinary phase observation, with no-match collapsing into empty and both unavailability shapes into UNKNOWN; the two halves are distinguished by a live control rather than inferred from the silent consumer.
- `runtime-claim`: the admitted overlap itself fires on an overlapping keyed row and stays silent on a disjoint one, and an empty *other* operand suppresses it, so the keyed operand is read through the same empty-suppression rule as a direct one.
- `gate-escape-claim`: a **keyed** negative presence operand escapes the negative-condition-in-iteration gate. `FieldNotFilled(Rows/RowRange For "k1")` with the error field inside the repeated group and no positive guard is accepted by the real consistency oracle, both at `rule add` and at `model check`, while dmtool's lint warns about it — and the lint's summary drops the `For "k1"` key, naming the operand as a plain per-row read. The gate therefore keys on the operand's **read shape**: a lookup is not an iterated read, so it needs no guard. The guarded twin of the same rule and a guarded *direct*-operand rule are both accepted too, which is what isolates the escape from an ordinary acceptance.
- `address-claim`: that escaped shape has a degenerate error address, and the discriminator attributes it. The **unguarded** keyed rule fires **once** on the no-match document and reports at `/EndpointShape[1]/Rows[-5]/RowRange` — the interpreter house form with a leading slash and row coordinate **`-5`**, the Kernel's `IIdentifier.UNKNOWN` clean-no-match reanchoring sentinel and not the `[0]` this contract uses for a wildcard. On the same document the **guarded** keyed rule and the guarded direct rule both report ordinary per-row addresses, `Rows/RowRange` and `Rows[2]/RowRange`, so the negative coordinate belongs to the unguarded keyed shape and not to the keyed lookup, the no-match, or the iterating locus. Both codegen strategies produced it identically. The accepted peer reconciliation establishes that it means **no matching row**; it is readable message-pointer state rather than a physical row address.
- `integrity`: admission models `bc7f895b98d2f4da2f2a38bc2d8c503016e8e03921a3e33c3de7f33ae689db88` (String index, before its rules) and `63e36d949e90475fb1e5fc4bb02f4676206d8ba9f99f6226e8222aff3ce7d507` (Number index). Runtime model `d030b614a7f2e65733c6a80400131e081aee4507bc81a5da7682782debe4dc66` (the String-index model with all five rules; the three-rule state was `9635bd3c6d693e4b8e03209861620e2a0c7a7403fe0c6e632b28a44bf69c212f`); documents `49994576c877b6c08d392369fdd3d9b552a2bea2c3f9446155249aa472768c9e` (match, overlapping), `99eab882bce81803dc334eed39d545443ca84496883069e74840c54a5d79e698` (match, disjoint), `6efbd2f2ccea3aa5dff7dd5033c409059eb8ce24d3279804d7fd7ee4e368c03a` (no match), `a9521a59d679dcd944897add1ae3baf3db92a63b6c9197ed5f516a0e0734eb30` (matched but empty), `e7a5e900bef731fa13e058fc7107574f5e83d74c2199f08c38a04f7c3746d508` (duplicate key), `f82ba380aa9743a98493f331c7bf87d4f75c66286a9c72d11948df708376d998` (invalid matched cell), and `3fd33d06238c3224a1e81cd0106f5a93fcfcd397774ef35ba7934650e5320b19` (empty other operand); request `c6c2aa5e876f063ff17bd83ec54eed374537876dbb03e70d48552a10000fa374`; artifacts `8501308100b0c8b68f66a2f6779fc3dc84edccee67e93b56b29423877b03360b` (three rules) and `e293df0898edc045b54abff3f432f70b21f5992aa00797a11d70ca976d281789` (five rules, carrying the address discriminator). `rule check` persists nothing, so the admission rows rest on the pre-rule model bytes.
- `limit`: one repeatable level, one index field per model, one exact profile, and validation phase only. **Computation-phase** column strictness is unmeasured here and remains owned by the general index clause, as do a field-valued key's own emptiness or invalidity and an invalid cell in a *non-selected* row of the column. No `interpretationOfYear` or fragment profile.
- `local-divergence`: none yet, because the keyed operand has no surface representation in this project at all: the DateRange source resolver takes a plain field path with no key position, so the shape is unrepresented rather than wrongly represented. The starred and group refusals match what the resolver already reports, and the measured result domain is the `CellObservation` this project already uses for a direct read, so the representation needs no new outcome class.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral on two clauses. Recorded as `SPEC-2026-08-23-07`.

<a id="src-nested-rule-locus"></a>
#### A rule locus that iterates two repeatable levels, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: one dmtool-authored model nests `Inner` (max 3) inside `Rows` (max 3) under a nonrepeatable root and declares one `yyyy-MM-dd`+slash `DATE_RANGE` at each of the three scopes. Eleven `rule check` decisions vary the rule group, the error field, and which scopes the operands sit in; one persisted rule then runs through `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` on both codegen strategies.
- `admission-claim`: a locus that iterates both levels — the error field at the leaf, the rule group at the leaf group or at its parent — admits an operand at the **root**, at the **enclosing row**, and at the **leaf**, in either authored slot. Measured on the overlap predicate, an endpoint-versus-endpoint comparison, an endpoint's numeric component, and stored range equality, so it is the locus rule rather than one carrier's.
- `refusal-claim`: the refusal direction is the same shared class. A **leaf** operand read from a rule that iterates only the enclosing row is `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, and so is a middle operand read from the nonrepeatable root, while a root operand at that middle locus is admitted. The predicate is therefore "every level the operand crosses is bound by the locus", the same one the addressed computation placement uses.
- `runtime-claim`: each operand is read at its own path inside the current leaf. With two enclosing rows carrying different ranges and each row's two leaves arranged so a borrowed row-one read would **invert** the pattern, the rule fires at leaf one of *each* row and nowhere else, and the `referenced` channel names `Rows[2]/RowRange` for the second row's firing rather than `Rows/RowRange`. Both strategies agreed on every message.
- `integrity`: model `b932706662931aff3b38d856596283ce50ee87d3f746a374d5249a7970ac406a` (with the probe rule persisted); document `8e70ba0ccdf07f8001486018edd5f629e58fc22283ba61593fac52871617383f`; request `a34287c196eab9c1a212b1a7c544751ef919cf9c06d68cf57a9cca5d075454e0`; artifact `d35cc659db8645bfa7d161133b764075f9b26af5393092f6c0dbb3d49b4c6ed4`. The eleven `rule check` rows were decided against this same model before and after the rule was added, and `rule check` persists nothing.
- `limit`: two levels, one carrier per admitted shape at runtime, and no starred operand. Two reference spellings were **not** measured because they failed to resolve at all from the root rule group and returned `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`: a relative descendant path and a relative starred descendant path from a nonrepeatable locus. That is a path-resolution boundary this batch does not characterise, and no locus claim rests on those two rows.
- `local-divergence`: none. The Lean leaf already carried the locus scope as a parameter and read every operand at its own declared scope, so the two-level shape needed cases rather than a change.
- `sync`: locally originated. It adds the two-level rows to the same locus rule the canonical clause already states, and the operand-admission predicate is the one `SPEC-2026-08-23-06` records for computation; the validation side already stated the rule in general terms. No new outbound entry.

<a id="src-computation-operand-scope"></a>
#### Which repeatable scopes a computation operand may sit in, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling worktree was empty before and after every command.
- `route`: one dmtool-authored model, `model check` valid, carries a root scope, a repeatable group `Rows` (max 5), a **sibling** repeatable group `Others` (max 3), and a **nested** repeatable group `Rows/Inner` (max 3), with a Number, a String, and a Date at the root and Numbers at each repeatable level. Thirteen `computation add --dry-run` decisions vary only where the operand sits and which target it writes.
- `admission-claim`: the gate is the computed target's own repetition scope binding every repeatable level the operand crosses. Admitted: a root operand into a `Rows` target, a root operand into a `Rows/Inner` target, and a `Rows` operand into a `Rows/Inner` target. Refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`: an `Others` operand into a `Rows` target, and a `Rows/Inner` operand into a `Rows` target. `Sum(Inner*/InnerNum)` into that same `Rows` target is admitted, which separates the refusal from a blanket ban on deeper operands and identifies the star as the flattening route.
- `kind-independence-claim`: the gate is the scope and not the operand kind. A root operand into a `Rows` target is admitted as a direct Number assignment, under `Length`, under `MonthFromDate`, under `Abs`, in a mixed outer-plus-row arithmetic pair, and under `FieldValueAsString` into a row String target.
- `integrity`: admission model `0dbe323c4232c2cfd87dbda96cbd06fd1cd1b2e190be3cb9c683ae2f4b7f486d`. A computation dry run persists nothing, so that model file is the whole retained input for the static rows; the hashes for the intermediate states this batch grew through are not retained, because each row was re-decided against the final model. Nested runtime model `ff05902c522cd12974bc8487c2be748147b634cde1edc88f03055a6b4df203fb`; documents `6429112484c6b64426d0d593a99b575ab227718a04bcceffde200ddeb1a71f03` (two enclosing values) and `1fbbf0fef52a4e159cf72a341b2fa6b503d9d936ae584f3ac208a0f808be197d` (one invalid enclosing operand); request `9bc83d2550902b1c1a125ffe4fc10406954d99ac16bbc8b0d3af28628fc4b51b`; artifact `bbb3920997e3706da75332ab9a81f184b5800e8814b0a624ad4cfbd8dca83630`. Single-level runtime model `9ee352f200b98fd327a044b4f08166fae2ba8eff0c33a08aecb9feb95efaee5c`; documents `5127974b93e6497fa6d0b38f484eb8f9872d618ea662809dfb8f35ddea9aeba5` (filled root), `0bcd8846b5d7c6fc2a1c1917d8698fc79c504668626a21cda7c209363e3e9031` (empty root, stale targets), and `ae28022ea8774f80a063d07d4cee86eac8807297b060a356a414dfc47c289860` (invalid root, stale targets); request `6498bf733f9ac6c26f473cd76e5293bf200c60ce0532530156cc7ba93193169d`; artifact `16468721d4b02a720f9f9313e646f490fff4d12cca55fb4fc1d7106c75df9d4b`.
- `runtime-route`: a second dmtool-authored model persists `/OuterRun/Rows/RowTotal := [/OuterRun/RootBase]` with a `max` of 10 on the root operand, kernel-confirmed at authoring. `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` and `compute` on both codegen strategies over three documents. Both strategies agreed on every channel of every row.
- `runtime-claim`: the outer operand is read **once at its own path** and reaches every target row. A root value of `7` computes `7` into all three instantiated rows; an empty root computes the real number `0` into every row, each firing the computation's own mismatch message against its stale stored value; and a root violating its declared maximum reports `zahlZuGross`, appears in `formalErrorsInOperands`, and **clears every row's target** — `cleared: true`, `errored: false`, no value. Deriving the operand's address from the target's path instead would read the root cell as empty in every row and compute `0` for the first two documents alike, which is exactly the account these rows exclude.
- `nested-runtime-route`: a third dmtool-authored model nests `Inner` (max 3) inside `Rows` (max 3) and persists `/NestedRun/Rows/Inner/LeafTotal := [/NestedRun/Rows/RowBase]`, kernel-confirmed at authoring, with a `max` of 50 on the middle-level operand. `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` and `compute` on both strategies over two documents of two enclosing rows by two leaves each.
- `nested-runtime-claim`: an operand one level up reaches exactly the leaves of **its own** enclosing row. Enclosing rows carrying `11` and `22` give their own two leaves `11` and `22` respectively, and an enclosing operand of `99` against its declared maximum reports `zahlZuGross`, appears in `formalErrorsInOperands`, and clears **only its own two leaves** while the sibling row's leaves still compute `22`. Both wrong accounts are excluded on these rows: borrowing the leaf's two-element path finds no cell and would compute the substituted zero everywhere, and reading the first enclosing row alone would give every leaf `11`.
- `limit`: static legality across five operand shapes, one sibling group, one nesting level. The runtime rows cover one operand shape — a direct Number copy — at one and at two levels, with no starred or deeper operand and no sibling-group row, and the route is cause-blind so each clear is observed as a clear.
- `local-divergence`: one, and it spanned eleven source families. Both runtime batches then corrected nothing: every shape, including the two-level correlation and its poison direction, matches what the widened cases already asserted. Every addressed one-source placement required the operand's scope to **equal** the target's, so every Kernel-legal form-level constant feeding a row was refused. The shared placement now carries the binding relation, and the two read sites that derived the operand's address from the target's path now derive it from the operand's own scope.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral. Recorded as `SPEC-2026-08-23-06`.

<a id="src-date-range-bound-component-runtime"></a>
#### What a computed DateRange-endpoint component evaluates to, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling worktree was empty before and after and both artifacts record `CLEAN`.
- `route`: one dmtool-authored model with no Base Year declares a repeatable group (max 5) carrying a `yyyy-MM-dd`+slash range, an `MM`+slash range, and two scale-0 Numbers. Two persisted kernel-confirmed computations assign `MonthFromDate(StartOfDateRange(RowDates))` and `QuarterFromDate(EndOfDateRange(RowMonths))` to them. `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` and `compute` on both codegen strategies over two documents. Both strategies agreed on every channel of every row.
- `value-claim`: a present exact range computes its selected endpoint's component — start month `6` from `2024-06-01/2024-07-31`, start month `1` from `2024-01-15/2024-03-15` — and the other endpoint is never substituted for it.
- `yearless-claim`: an **unconfigured yearless** range computes its retained label at runtime as well as admitting statically: end quarter `3` from `03/07`, `4` from `11/12`, and `2` from `05/05`. This is the row that separates the correct read from selecting through the exact-value-only endpoint carrier, which faults on a fragment carrier and would have poisoned every one of them.
- `empty-claim`: an unplaced range computes the real number `0` rather than clearing, which is the ordinary Number empty-as-zero rule reaching this operand. Measured for both computations on the same row.
- `invalid-operand-claim`: an inverted range is `datumBereichNichtGueltig`, and the computation reading it **clears** its target — `cleared: true`, `errored: false`, no value — while the sibling computation on the same row reading a clean operand still computes its own value. The clear is only visible when the target was filled in the source: over an empty source target the same row reports no outcome at all, which is the source-relative channel rule rather than a second runtime behaviour. The computation's own mismatch message stays silent on the cleared instance and fires on every stored-versus-computed disagreement, so cleared-versus-stored is not flagged here either.
- `integrity`: model `efc61721f1692230d13a65f8ff63823b81d41e89f6ec5e7f4492fb6a0187f2ad`; documents `321e7c8d651101ad5b0d2cfb4cf0d9748c5f3f4453f19154400192064bfea7b8` (empty targets) and `1484a583da1fc822da558a2aa80e261998608ddddc041aea3828ab7d6abc4e5d` (stale filled targets); requests `508bdd6eab043aea770f17d9855cc49c6cc47fe9c8f0fc65eb00eb168302404b` and `9a890f95423b7ba0536e5f772da2cf51cd5ca8bbf5d8a42ab4469d6c5f8b67f5`; artifacts `7022d277205cb63ac6a2bad397014d03d36734d90748a837a98c5b498b3b5591` and `d8cae5a4c7c78b46bcbe67c970294f8f94d28afe0653899d3df29b27aaed55de`.
- `limit`: one repeatable level, no Base Year, the default zone, and one profile per carrier. `DayFromDate` and `YearFromDate` values, a wrapping range, `interpretationOfYear`, a scalar-source-into-row-target placement, and the applied-document state after a clear were not exercised. The route is cause-blind, so the clear is observed as a clear and its producer edge is not published.
- `local-divergence`: none. Every value, the empty zero, and the cleared invalid-operand row match what the Lean cases already asserted, so this batch upgrades their status from internal composition to calibrated rather than correcting anything.
- `sync`: locally originated. It adds runtime rows to the same clause `SPEC-2026-08-23-05` already carries and states the endpoint operand's computed value account explicitly; recorded there rather than as a second entry.

<a id="src-date-range-bound-component"></a>
#### The numeric component of a selected DateRange endpoint, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: every decision taken with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`; dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime. The sibling stayed empty before and after every command.
- `route`: two models authored with the structured `model new` / `group add` / `field add` / `config modify` verbs, both `model check` valid. `BoundPart_DM` declares **no Base Year** and holds a repeatable group `/BoundPart/Rows` (max 5) carrying one `DATE_RANGE` per admitted profile — `dd.MM.yyyy`+dash, `MM`+slash, `yyyy`+slash, `MM-dd`+slash, `yyyy-MM`+slash, `dd.MM`+dash — beside a row Number, a scalar `dd.MM.yyyy` range, and a root Number. `BoundPartBY_DM` declares Base Year 2024 over `MM`+slash and `dd.MM`+dash row ranges. Twenty-nine `rule check` decisions plus five `computation add --dry-run` decisions.
- `exposure-claim`: the component gate is the declared profile's own component set with the Base Year supplementing the **year** and nothing else, which is `part.admittedBy hasBaseYear components` unchanged from a direct Date field. Measured across all four extractors: a full-Date profile admits all four; `yyyy` admits year alone; `yyyy-MM` admits year, month, and quarter; `MM-dd` and `dd.MM` admit day, month, and quarter; `MM` admits month and quarter. Under Base Year 2024 the `MM` and `dd.MM` declarations additionally admit `YearFromDate` while `DayFromDate` on `MM` stays refused, which is the live control showing the supplement reaches the year half alone. Every refusal is `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`.
- `gate-order-claim`: at this carrier the **locus** gate reports before the component gate. One row operand whose profile withholds the requested component draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from a non-iterating locus, while the identical operand and component draws `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` once the rule iterates the row. A starred occurrence draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`, so no wildcard form of this operand exists and an iterating rule is the only legal way to read a row endpoint's component.
- `computation-claim`: the operand is admitted in a Number **computation** operation, not only in a validation condition. A row endpoint's component computing a row Number and a scalar endpoint's component computing a root Number are both accepted by the kernel gate; a row operand computing a root Number is refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, and the self-reference control on the same target reports `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`, which is what distinguishes acceptance from an ungated preview. Four further rows fix the target half. A **scalar** endpoint computing a **row** Number is accepted, so the placement direction the addressed leaves refuse is a local narrowing rather than a Kernel rule. An unconfigured yearless row endpoint computing a row Number is accepted, while `YearFromDate` on that same declaration is refused `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`, so the component gate holds on this surface as well as in a condition. Assigning a component to a **fractional** Number target is refused `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, which fixes the derived scale at 0.
- `integrity`: models `d5c1db81d8b3293d8e810e8f67a2f725a1a76bb74ef877edb61007a97db370bd` and `17a7fdcbaf74e879afd45b2f67f52452858e9a2291cdd36c9fbd943e48778eb5`. `rule check` and a computation dry run persist nothing, so the two model files are the whole retained input. The first model gained one fractional Number field for the follow-on addressed-computation rows after this batch was first taken; the complete twenty-four-row exposure matrix and every locus row were **re-measured** against the hash above rather than assumed unaffected, and all thirty reproduced identically.
- `limit`: static legality only. No runtime observation of what an admitted component evaluates to on either surface, one repeatable level, no `interpretationOfYear` declaration, and no wrapping-range row. The computation rows are `--dry-run` previews, so they establish admission and not computed values; the derived-scale row is measured only at target scale 2 against derived scale 0, so the suppression branch of that code is unmeasured here.
- `local-divergence`: two. The Lean exposure predicate had branched on the comparable-endpoint exact-value gate, which these rows show is not the component gate; it now applies the direct-field rule uniformly. The runtime read had selected the endpoint through the exact-value-only selector, which faults on a fragment carrier and would have reported an admitted `MonthFromDate` on an `MM` range as malformed; the component read now selects from every carrier.
- `sync`: locally originated and behavioral. The canonical clause's extractor enumeration omitted Base-Year supplementation, listed fewer admitting profiles than the kernel admits, said nothing about the computation surface, and did not carry this carrier in its measured locus list. Recorded as `SPEC-2026-08-23-05`.

<a id="src-date-range-yearless-locus"></a>
#### The rule-locus gate over unconfigured yearless DateRange carriers, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: every decision taken with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`; dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime. The sibling stayed empty before and after every command.
- `route`: a second model, authored with the structured `model new` / `group add` / `field add` verbs and `model check` valid, declares **no Base Year** so a yearless declaration stays a retained label pair. It holds a repeatable group `/YearlessLocus/Rows` (max 5) with a `MM`+slash `DATE_RANGE`, plus scalar `MM`+slash and `MM-dd`+slash ranges. Ten `rule check` decisions vary only the error field.
- `locus-claim`: the locus rule reaches the unconfigured yearless carriers unchanged. A yearless endpoint pair, both overlap operators, and stored equality are each admitted with the error field inside the repeated group; the endpoint pair and both overlaps are refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from outside it. The plural operator obeys the rule on both of its sides, and its all-scalar shape is admitted at a non-iterating locus with a `MM` scalar against a `MM-dd` list, so this carrier applies no component gate in the label domain either.
- `literal-claim`: comparing an unconfigured yearless endpoint with a **complete date literal** is refused `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` from *inside* the repeated group as well, so that refusal is the declaration's own comparability rule and not a locus effect. This is the live control separating the two gates on one model.
- `integrity`: model `89cf50eefe6456b6d02fc205449b29da8fd20f51aa8d06bb0f04a88a6e321e11`. `rule check` persists nothing, so the model file is the whole retained input.
- `limit`: `rule check` static legality only, one repeatable level, one unconfigured model, and `MM` declarations on both sides. No runtime observation of what an admitted per-row yearless comparison evaluates to, no `MM-dd` row, and no Base-Year control on this model.
- `local-divergence`: none. Lean already refused the outside locus and admitted nothing extra; what this measurement authorized is the row-reading representation, which reuses the scalar yearless owner rather than adding a second account.
- `sync`: locally originated, and it adds no rule the canonical clause does not already state — the locus rule and the yearless comparability rule are both recorded, and this batch shows they compose without either one moving. No outbound entry.

<a id="src-date-range-rule-locus"></a>
#### The rule-locus gate over four DateRange condition carriers, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: every decision taken with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`; dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime. The sibling stayed empty before and after every command.
- `route`: one UTC model, `model check` valid, declares a scalar `DATE_RANGE`, two scalar `DATE` fields, and a repeatable group `/Form/Rows` (max 5) holding a `DATE_RANGE` and two `DATE` fields. Thirty-six `rule check` decisions vary only the error field, which is also the rule's iteration scope: an error field inside `/Form/Rows` places the rule inside the repeated group, `/Form/Existing` outside it.
- `locus-claim`: the same condition is admitted from inside and refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from outside, on all four carriers that read an unstarred repeatable operand — `DateRangesOverlap(RowRange, /Form/Existing)`, `[RowRange] == [/Form/Existing]`, `StartOfDateRange(RowRange) == "01.06.2020"`, and `[/Form/Existing] == DateRange(RowDate, RowDateB)`. Each refusal names the **offending operand's own** field, `RowRange` on the first three and `RowDate` on the construction, which is what isolates the carrier under test. The construction row was measured a second time for exactly that reason: its first form carried a `FieldFilled(RowRange)` guard, and that operand reported first, so the composed row shared the outcome without establishing that the construction endpoints were ever reached.
- `endpoint-claim`: the endpoint carrier behaves identically at both loci and across its shapes. `StartOfDateRange` and `EndOfDateRange` against a fixed date, an endpoint pair against a scalar peer, and both endpoints of the same repeatable row are each admitted from inside and refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from outside, under `==`, `!=`, and an order operator. The refusal message names the offending operand, so each shape is isolated rather than sharing an outcome with a neighbour.
- `overlap-claim`: the singular overlap predicate follows the same locus rule, and its whole-list gates are unchanged by the locus. A bare repeatable operand beside a scalar one is admitted from inside and refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from outside, at list length three as well as two; a **starred** occurrence is admitted from either locus, since a star reopens its own level; and a list carrying **both spellings of one field** — the bare occurrence beside the star — is admitted from inside. A sole bare operand is refused for multiplicity, `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDFN`, so the arity gate is untouched. Negating the predicate remains a parse refusal, so it has no negative form at any locus.
- `construction-pair-claim`: a construction compared with another construction obeys the same rule on all four endpoints. A row-read construction against a scalar one is admitted from inside and refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from outside, and the all-scalar pair is admitted at a non-iterating locus.
- `plural-overlap-claim`: the plural operator obeys the same locus rule on **both** of its sides. A repeatable distinguished scalar against a scalar list and a scalar against a repeatable list are each admitted from inside and refused `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from outside; a starred list operand is admitted from either locus, and a repeatable scalar against a starred list of the same field is admitted from inside.
- `polarity-claim`: the negative-condition-in-iteration gate does not reach a value comparison on the operand. From inside the repeated group, stored equality is admitted under `==` and `!=`, and an extracted endpoint under `==`, `!=`, and `<`; negating the overlap predicate is a parse refusal, `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, so that carrier has no negative form to gate. The control is live on this exact model: `FieldNotFilled(RowRange)` from the same locus is refused `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`, its positive form is admitted, and the `And`-guarded negative is admitted again.
- `gate-order-claim`: the locus gate decides **before** the error-field-referenced gate. The endpoint row refuses `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` with error field `/Form/Existing`, which that condition never references; the same error field with a scalar-only condition (`StartOfDateRange(/Form/Target) == "01.06.2020"`) instead draws `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`, and adding the reference admits it. So the missing-wildcard class is not a masked reference failure.
- `integrity`: model `86c67a377242921bd4670c6effc9070861f53d32c054bb029a452a1de3ff998c`. `rule check` persists nothing, so there is no request or artifact to pin; the model file is the whole retained input.
- `limit`: `rule check` static legality only, one repeatable level, one error field per locus, and DateRange carriers only. The construction row's inside locus uses an endpoint field as the error field, since the construction is what must reference it. No nested repeatable chain, no starred spelling of the same operand, no runtime observation of what the admitted inside-locus rule *evaluates* to, and no non-DateRange carrier in this batch.
- `local-divergence`: Lean already refused the outside-locus operand at both shared resolvers, but reported no Kernel class for it, so a consumer reading the diagnostic projection saw an uncovered refusal. The class is now owned once by `ResolveError.diagnostic?` and inherited by the entity-list checker, the direct DateRange owner, and the construction endpoint. The **admitted** inside-locus half stays unrepresented and is tracked as its own obligation, because these carriers are not embeddable in the iterating rule tree.
- `sync`: locally originated. The carrier breadth extends an account the canonical clause already stated for the construction endpoint; the gate order is new, and both are carried outbound by [`SPEC-2026-08-23-04`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-04--the-operand-locus-gate-decides-before-the-error-field-gate).

<a id="src-date-range-list-crossing"></a>
#### Direct-list DateRange `FirstFilledValue` gates and rendered crossing, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: model authored and every decision taken with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the probe artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout.
- `route`: one unconfigured UTC model declares an ISO target, a slash-separated month target, and pairs of direct nonrepeatable sources in `dd.MM.yyyy`+dash, `yyyy-MM-dd`+slash, and `MM` with the empty separator. Eight `computation add --dry-run` decisions cover the gate matrix; two persisted computations then feed `:adapter:kernelProbe` observing `validateFull` and `compute` over three documents on both codegen strategies, which agreed on every row.
- `source-gate-claim`: the list's sources are gated by **format identity**, not by component set. A list mixing `dd.MM.yyyy` with `yyyy-MM-dd` is refused `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED` even though both expose the same components, and a three-source list whose odd member matches the target reports the same code, so this gate decides before the target is consulted.
- `target-gate-claim`: the one shared source format is gated by the **target's component set**. Dotted-plus-dotted into an ISO target is admitted, as is an empty-separator month pair into a slash-separated month target; a month pair into an ISO target and a dotted pair into a month target are both refused `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE_RANGE`.
- `runtime-claim`: the target's own spelling renders the stored value on an admitted crossing. A dotted source `01.06.2024-30.06.2024` stores `2024-06-01/2024-06-30` on the ISO target, an empty-separator `0609` stores `06/09` on the slash month target, and a document filling only the second source stores `2024-07-02/2024-07-31`, so the lazy scan reaches it. An all-empty document produces no outcome entry at all.
- `local-divergence`: Lean required source format identity **against the target**, which conflated the two gates and refused every admitted crossing while rendering under the source profile. Both are corrected: the shared source format is now compared with the target by component set, and the target's own format renders. The empty-document clearing behaviour is unchanged and remains calibrated elsewhere; this row shows only that no outcome is reported.
- `integrity`: model `d6272963cb8123576589a371a77d62fe84b49a6ca268d341e4a25e7f43a4b9e6`; request `2ca043699f39d4343dff993e8cd8a6db48226cf9e43eb3cc69bb99612a1ae187`; artifact `90150d28c053d955a36c496d0fa2dc8c4d507e1f1fe6ac92efd9f4a59480e8c8`. The dry-run decisions were taken before the two computations were persisted, against the same declarations.
- `limit`: two-source and one three-source list, two component sets, one unconfigured model, and direct nonrepeatable sources only. No `yyyy`, `yyyy-MM`, or `MM-dd` crossing on the list shape, no Base-Year model, no formal or clearing branch beyond the all-empty row, and no list longer than three.
- `sync`: locally originated, and narrower than it first appeared. The canonical clause already stated both gates — sources by common declaration profile, target by component set — so what this measurement adds is their **order** and the two yearless spellings on the list shape, carried outbound by [`SPEC-2026-08-23-03`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-03--the-daterange-computations-source-and-target-gates-are-ordered-and-both-spellings-participate). The local correction was to Lean, which conflated the two gates and rendered under the source profile even though its own clause said otherwise.

<a id="src-date-range-interpretation-first-filled"></a>
#### Direct-list DateRange `FirstFilledValue` across year interpretations, measured locally 2026-08-24

- `revision`: the model was extended, both computations were admitted, and the runtime probe executed with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `3a4025bbfdbd925d7f4c23e519f15ce0e2461b21`, which the artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout.

- `claim`: the declared year interpretation enters neither the direct list's source-profile gate nor its target component-set gate. Both directions are admitted even though the source and target resolve the same wrapping label under different endpoint years.
- `runtime-claim`: a wrapping source stores `01.11-28.02` into the oppositely interpreted target in both directions, and the ordered control similarly stores `01.03-31.10`. The [interpretation-comparison checkpoint](#src-date-range-interpretation-comparison) independently pins that the two wrapping source values are unequal after endpoint resolution. Target-owned rendering is established separately by the [list-crossing checkpoint](#src-date-range-list-crossing), whose crossed spellings distinguish it from source-token copying; this same-spelling row does not.
- `integrity`: model `8ab30399fc592f80c39caa543918195fba81a3651ad046d3e167952b74aa7777`; request `ce7d9d5b1dedfa7ed8dc69e6f82753e6b27846df388a86a2b6b2fce7a3c771d7`; artifact `7adbbd8a52eb60ef896ff9fbd7b81fc91c2f8c66cb5515a947105016a482d2b5`.
- `limit`: two direct nonrepeatable sources, one component class, one Base Year, UTC, and no formal source. Group, filtered, repeatable, another interpretation-bearing profile, a dependent read of the computed target, and an unconfigured model remain unmeasured on this route. The direct-star carrier is measured separately by the [interpretation star checkpoint](#src-date-range-interpretation-first-filled-star).
- `sync`: locally originated and consistent with the direct-list gate already accepted as [`SPEC-2026-08-23-03`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-03--the-direct-list-firstfilledvalue-shape-gates-sources-and-target-differently): its common source profile is the lexical `(format, separator)` pair, while the target gate reads the component set. This checkpoint separates the independently declared `interpretationOfYear` key from that accepted lexical gate rather than adding another gate, so it creates no outbound ledger entry.

<a id="src-date-range-interpretation-first-filled-star"></a>
#### Direct-star DateRange `FirstFilledValue` across year interpretations, measured locally 2026-08-24

- `revision`: the model, both computations, and every structured read were authored through the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `3a4025bbfdbd925d7f4c23e519f15ce0e2461b21`, which each probe artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout.
- `route`: one Base-Year-2020 UTC model declares a direct single-level repeatable `Entries` group with `dd.MM`+dash `FROM` and `TO` sources plus opposite root targets. The real Kernel gate admits `FirstFilledValue(Entries*/ToRange)` into the `FROM` target and `FirstFilledValue(Entries*/FromRange)` into the `TO` target. Structured computation readback and whole-model checking retain both operations as `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`; `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` and `compute` over five documents on dynamic Groovy and static Java.
- `claim`: `interpretationOfYear` enters neither the direct-star carrier gate nor target assignment. Both directions are admitted because source and target expose the same component set. Lean retains the source-resolved exact endpoints before target presentation; this Kernel row observes only the resulting yearless target text and does not claim the Kernel's internal carrier. Target-owned presentation is separated from source-token copying by the [all-eight profile checkpoint](#src-date-range-2026-08-19-reconciliation), not by this same-spelling model.
- `runtime-claim`: wrapping first-row input stores `01.11-28.02` in both targets, and ordered first-row input stores `01.03-31.10`. An empty first row reaches the wrapping second row. A row whose `FROM` source is ordered and `TO` source wraps stores the ordered label only in the `TO` target and the wrapping label only in the `FROM` target, separating the two source identities. An all-empty two-row group clears both seeded targets. Every row has no validation messages or formal operand errors, and both Kernel strategies agree on every channel.
- `integrity`: model `8c4cb6dd18b3ec837e922ef81093b45c35f049364c27715d211e9ab8bddacdb0`; request `ec3020f11b8f17c4a1c37c023ad0cc7840c38b65b09cc902554bffc17f5152a5`; two byte-identical artifacts `4dd4e7e89f50c19963c1f9b9c6477928ba468f27c1eef8e216f327e1567a0639`.
- `limit`: one component class, one Base Year, UTC, one direct single-level star per computation, at most two instantiated rows, and no formal source. Another interpretation-bearing profile, a group or filtered carrier, another computation family, and a dependent target read remain unmeasured here. The unconfigured route is measured separately by the [unconfigured interpretation checkpoint](#src-date-range-unconfigured-interpretation-first-filled-star).
- `sync`: locally originated and consistent with the direct-star component-set gate accepted as [`SPEC-2026-08-19-03`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-19-03--date-range-firstfilledvalue-admits-matching-fragment-profiles) and widened across spellings by [`SPEC-2026-08-23-03`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-03--the-direct-list-firstfilledvalue-shape-gates-sources-and-target-differently). The checkpoint separates `interpretationOfYear` from that accepted carrier gate and adds no new gate, so it creates no outbound ledger entry.

#### Unconfigured interpreted DateRange declaration-order discriminator, corrected 2026-08-24

- `original-revision`: every declaration, computation, structured read, and runtime probe used the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `3a4025bbfdbd925d7f4c23e519f15ce0e2461b21`, which the artifacts record as their producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout.
