Both strategies agreed, but the model did not vary declaration order.
- `original-integrity`: the first batch used model `0acfc83dd41c92e9070d51b4f4e76fb9ac7d2b55b161682b79b50e2f50bf9b7f`, request `536d4a26134368f79a4af456df9c86567924e3f0154e016cbd94b726f7a9a69e`, and two byte-identical artifacts `1bcbe08a3b454e80be06a10bf19fd04f6c51221c3dd8a5a887b4299f5450f65a`. The completing batch used model `f13ff5781fc8fe3d6ed7e6b21485be28ad716ff7e9a13cc27e2ca4e9230d103f`, request `c3227ea35abdcc4976479a3a9c2ed96e278ccccf153530650d1ef20aa95d0b6b`, and two byte-identical artifacts `b696697be796e523060f3a204bc090717a2c24d1288b5c7971050bb98103d827`. The mixed-presence batch used model `3c2bde84679e459a984f5bf8ac54c395664e552bc93a2dc015f198ecec662b04`, request `5924fb2b8151a6f6f7e5fc55c1556cea9d45fb44cb709920d7f29d7fdcd3c2e0`, and two byte-identical artifacts `c506a11766a3a84c93d6ee43c5120a80c5d66be555f5c6bee5ad52697b42e3db`.
- `reviewed-revision`: exact a12-dmkits `cd43c249a88874bf5688a45060e208a0dcd20782`, clean before and after the targeted differential run. [`DateRangeSharedFormatOrderDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/DateRangeSharedFormatOrderDiffTest.kt) retains the order-only control on both Kernel strategies and the independent interpreter.
- `corrected-outcome`: placing the interpreted `dd.MM` declaration block first admits interpreted `MM` source `11/02` and computes `01.11-01.02`; placing the otherwise identical `MM` block first reports `datumBereichNichtGueltig` for that source. For standard `MM` source `03/10`, dotted-first computes `01.03-31.10` while month-first computes `03/10`. Both Kernel strategies agree within each model. The interpreter produces the month-first, declaration-local account under both orders.
- `disposition`: the original values remain valid observations of the dotted-first model, but the claimed field-local validity and presentation rules are withdrawn. The order-only control establishes model declaration-order dependence. Source inspection points to shared generated formats and first-definition retention, but no runtime denominator establishes every format equivalence class or consumer, so that mechanism is not generalized into the canonical clauses.
- `local-state`: the FlatModel retains field declarations but not the Kernel's generated shared-format equivalence state. Lean therefore removed the dotted-carrier specialization, restored the field-local fail-closed unconfigured wrap boundary, and excludes that branch from its Kernel-correspondence claim rather than selecting one observed order as universal.
- `limit`: the corrected runtime control covers one dotted-versus-month equivalence pair, formal validation of one wrapping source, direct-star computation of one wrapping and one ordered source, two declaration orders, UTC, and no dependent read. Other admitted DateRange profiles, equivalence classes, direct lists, groups, filters, and consumers remain open.
- `sync`: the peer superseded [`SPEC-2026-08-24-02`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-24-02--an-unconfigured-year-interpretation-admits-wrapping-dateranges-and-firstfilledvalue-uses-the-dotted-carrier) as written and recorded the broader divergence as `IG141`. This is inbound reviewed provenance and creates no new outbound entry.

<a id="src-date-range-unconfigured-bound"></a>
#### Unconfigured yearless bound extraction and its component-set consumer gates, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: models authored and every condition decided with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the probe artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout.
- `route`: two unconfigured UTC models declare the yearless and year-bearing DateRange profiles side by side. `rule check` and `rule add` decide the bound conditions below, each `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`, covering all four extractors against every declared component set, both endpoint positions, both lexical spellings of each yearless set, the full-Date and `Today` comparisons, and bound-versus-bound.
- `extraction-claim`: `StartOfDateRange` and `EndOfDateRange` are **admitted** on a yearless profile with no Base Year. The refusals sit on the consumers, not on the extraction, so an unconfigured yearless bound is a legal value that only some consumers accept.
- `component-claim`: an extracted bound carries exactly its source declaration's component set, and each numeric extractor is gated by it. `MonthFromDate` is admitted on `MM` and on `yyyy-MM` and refused on `yyyy`; `DayFromDate` is admitted on `MM-dd` and on `yyyy-MM-dd` and refused on `MM` and on `yyyy-MM`; `YearFromDate` is admitted on `yyyy` and refused on `MM`. Every refusal is `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` at the extractor.
- `comparison-claim`: comparing a bound with a full Date is refused for every profile that does not expose the full set — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` against both a `dd.MM.yyyy` literal and `Today`, for `MM`, `MM-dd`, and `yyyy` — while the exact profiles are admitted. Bound-versus-bound comparison instead follows the established direct temporal admission rule: `MM` against `MM` and `MM` against `MM-dd` are admitted, `MM` against `yyyy` is refused. Year presence, not identical component sets, is what that rule reads, which is why a component-set mismatch is admitted here and refused for whole-range equality.
- `local-divergence`: Lean refused the extraction itself for every unconfigured yearless profile, so its gate sat one step too early. The extraction, the numeric-component consumer, and the endpoint-pair comparison are now implemented, reusing `DateNumericPart.admittedBy` and `TemporalComparisonOp.admitsFormats` over the new `DateRangeInputFormat.components` projection, with a bound value that carries the retained labels instead of an exact `DateValue` and `YearlessInterval.ofMonthPair` supplying the positional completion. The `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` refusal of a fixed full-Date peer is now reported at the comparison as well, with a proved law stating that the gate and the exact owner's policy precondition are the same condition.
- `runtime-claim`: `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes four documents on both codegen strategies, which agreed on every row. A `MM` range `06/09` exposes month 6 at its start and 9 at its finish; a `MM-dd` range `06-01/09-30` exposes day 1 at its start, day 30 and month 9 at its finish; and the two alternate spellings agree with their siblings. `QuarterFromDate` on a month-only start reads the month's quarter.
- `completion-claim`: bound-versus-bound comparison completes each fragment by its endpoint position before comparing. A `MM` start against a `MM-dd` start fires when the day is 1 and stays silent when the day is 2, on otherwise identical documents, so a month-only start completes to the earliest day rather than comparing the month alone. Changing the month silences it too. An all-empty document fires no bound rule at all.
- `finish-completion-claim`: the finish position completes to the greatest day its month can ever have. A `MM` finish equals a `MM-dd` finish on September 30 and not on September 29, and on February it equals day **29** and not day 28, with no year available to decide leapness. Ordering works on the completed labels too: a June 1 start is strictly before a June 2 start. Its request is `b159a1016115f99b75ac8c5973ba8bc58d64ffde504bbf430459a95ffd7d446b` and artifact `c86efccefe450fe38d02c7ca96a132bd272b3a3ae276936b736ee14d7de44315`, decided against model `7c3d4f708118496d2acddd5825fdaeff1270a4c40dee8bd3e1a21e743ae6c577`.
- `pair-admission-claim`: the endpoint pair follows the ordinary direct temporal admission rule rather than requiring identical component sets. `MM` against `MM-dd` is admitted under equality, inequality, and ordering; `yyyy-MM-dd` against `yyyy-MM` and against `yyyy` are admitted; and `MM` against `yyyy` is refused `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`.
- `integrity`: the conditions were decided against two models, the unconfigured comparison model `b33c2b880a05a18ce1eea1bdf7fd3c93bff7782859b0934a3ffac977f54df52f` and the bound model `d47829e10b22ffa26691cd13f241e13189ee993db197c160d236d6ce20341e38`; the runtime rows used request `3ee656dcb60de44caad054e3561885d7e2782bc0bb1cda98486aac01493c8934` and artifact `7218dadb07f566ddf97638558ade34bc6359ce142dddf942637f606f9a4d3c96`. The static extractor batch was decided before the final four rules were added, against the same declarations.
- `limit`: one unconfigured model, one time zone, and direct nonrepeatable fields. No formally invalid or empty operand row, so those projections stay internal; no repeatable, starred, or group source; no exact-versus-exact endpoint-pair runtime row; and no configured-model control in this batch beyond the ones already at the [bound checkpoint](#src-date-range-bound-base-year). The positional completion is measured between the month and month-day classes only, at both endpoint positions, with the February pair separating day 29 from day 28.
- `sync`: locally originated. The canonical clause never stated a Base-Year precondition for extraction, so this corrects only this project's own gate placement; what the clause gains is new behavioral content — the component-set consumer gates, the full-Date comparison refusal, and the positional completion before a bound-versus-bound comparison — carried outbound by [`SPEC-2026-08-23-02`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-02--an-extracted-daterange-bound-carries-its-declarations-component-set).

<a id="src-date-range-interpretation-comparison"></a>
#### Equality across declared year interpretations, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: model authored and every condition decided with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the probe artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout.
- `route`: one Base-Year-2020 UTC model declares `dd.MM`+dash three times, twice with `FROM` and once with `TO`. Three `rule check` conditions decide admission, and `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` over five documents on both codegen strategies, which agreed on every row.
- `admission-claim`: a declared interpretation does not enter the comparability gate. All three equality conditions are admitted — `FROM` against `TO`, `FROM` against `FROM`, and the inequality direction — even though both overlap operators refuse the same declarations. The interpretation is not part of the component set.
- `runtime-claim`: identical wrapping stored text under `FROM` and under `TO` is **unequal**: `01.11-28.02` on both fields fires `!=` and not `==`, because the two sides resolve to `2020-11-01..2021-02-28` and `2019-11-01..2020-02-28`. Identical ordered text `01.03-31.10` on the same two fields is equal. One pair of declarations therefore separates resolved-identity comparison from stored-text comparison, and confirms through a third channel that the interpretation is inert on an ordered range.
- `empty-claim`: an empty counterpart leaves both directions unfired, and an all-empty document fires nothing, on the same declarations.
- `integrity`: model `8cc70b23d1825dce3487b1d720574bc94b4e347e1845564700934a0a53842bd7`; request `77254d257c752db6c3e8eb1fbc2678c05c27c48c34f7aab6dbc1075d40c7aae0`; artifact `4d84316b3ae282d08a7a9337926d1fe9717fc3e21a3c09d0539af7dd9a7e8b3a`.
- `limit`: one component class, one Base Year, one wrapping and one ordered value, and equality only. No interpretation on another profile, no bound row under an interpretation beyond the [interpretation-overlap checkpoint](#src-date-range-interpretation-overlap-refusal), and no unconfigured model. Computation-target execution is measured separately by the [interpretation first-filled checkpoint](#src-date-range-interpretation-first-filled).
- `sync`: locally originated and consistent with the canonical account, which already states both the resolved-endpoint comparison and the wrap placement this row composes. It creates no new outbound entry; the overlap refusal it contrasts with is carried by [`SPEC-2026-08-23-01`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-01--an-interpretationofyear-declaration-is-refused-as-an-overlap-operand).

<a id="src-date-range-stored-comparison"></a>
#### Stored-versus-stored DateRange comparability and cross-spelling identity, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: model authored and every condition decided with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the probe artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty before and after every command.
- `route`: one Base-Year-2020 UTC model declares all eight admitted `(format, separator)` pairs, with a second instance of each profile that has only one lexical spelling, so every same-component pair is expressible without self-comparison. Two `dmtool batch` runs decide 24 comparison conditions through `rule check`; four persisted rules then feed `:adapter:kernelProbe` over four documents on both codegen strategies, which agreed on every row.
- `admission-claim`: the equality gate reads the declared **component set** and nothing else. Nine admitted conditions cover every same-component pair, including all three lexical crossings in both authored orders: `yyyy-MM-dd` against `dd.MM.yyyy`, `MM`+slash against `MM`+empty separator, and `MM-dd` against `dd.MM`. Fifteen refused conditions cover every cross-class pair, each `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE_RANGE`, in both authored orders for three of them and for `!=` on one. `dd.MM.yyyy` against `dd.MM` is the separating control: it shares a separator and differs only in components.
- `class-claim`: the eight declarations therefore fall into five component classes — full Date (`dd.MM.yyyy`, `yyyy-MM-dd`), year (`yyyy`), year-month (`yyyy-MM`), month (`MM` with slash or empty separator), and month-day (`MM-dd`, `dd.MM`) — and comparability is exactly equality of that class.
- `runtime-claim`: an admitted crossing compares retained identity rather than stored text. `2020-11-01/2021-02-28` equals `01.11.2020-28.02.2021`, `06/09` equals `0609`, and `06-01/09-30` equals `01.06-30.09`, all in one document; a stored-text comparison would refuse all three. Changing one endpoint on each crossing leaves equality unfired and fires inequality on the full-Date pair, so the relation discriminates.
- `empty-claim`: an empty operand on either side leaves **both** directions unfired: neither `==` nor `!=` fires when one counterpart is absent, and an all-empty document fires nothing. Absence is therefore neither equal nor unequal, matching the general not-evaluated rule for empty comparison operands.
- `integrity`: model `7c20cdd15c51401cd3e9d6210a29cb8ef3228c04b98848628c34364896a6219f`; grid batches `89db80ec6fe760584577ea70de971f29144738ac80a239fcb798008eb7ef657f` and `23bfde31ac02ca64f8022c93819b73d15526c937741f8436f85ae14f88e56f36`; request `ad2def222262a259debb6fdb381b53aa374940b290d37ced6a6b1fbda3c32e2b`; artifact `bd1e45907bee3ec662511350f6099917005b34655839402eafdfe2e6d40b5a06`. The grid was re-decided against the final model after the four rules were persisted and returned identical verdicts.
- `unconfigured-claim`: a second model declaring the same profiles with **no Base Year** decides nine representative conditions identically — every same-component pair admitted, every cross-component pair `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE_RANGE` — so the gate is Base-Year-independent. Four further documents observed on both strategies show each class comparing its own retained identity there: unconfigured `06/09` equals `0609`, `06-01/09-30` equals `01.06-30.09`, `02-01/02-29` equals `01.02-29.02` with no year to make February 29 leap-dependent, one differing label fires only inequality, and an empty counterpart fires nothing. Its model is `b33c2b880a05a18ce1eea1bdf7fd3c93bff7782859b0934a3ffac977f54df52f`, grid `b0351dc3327c872782306c98798f8865767c620152f9b56bacdd2f31422ade7f`, request `803124a85895882e69f5e85318ad4d5964bd91ebfcc67ca91cce94319fa5063d`, artifact `e6be15b8c700ea303057b2ea57d5b49abb33981a6f2b498e9d256690ecf35c40`.
- `limit`: equality and inequality only, one time zone, and direct nonrepeatable stored fields on both sides. No starred, group, semantic-index, or repeatable operand; no construction on either side, which keeps its own checkpoint; and no formally invalid operand row. The unconfigured grid decides nine of the twenty-four pairs rather than all of them.
- `sync`: locally originated and consistent with the already accepted [`SPEC-2026-08-19-01`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-19-01--date-range-equality-requires-matching-component-sets-across-both-operands), whose principle it instantiates for stored operands. The canonical clause gains the stored instance, the three lexical crossings, and the order and operator symmetry; it creates no new outbound entry, because the peer's account already states that the gate reads component sets rather than lexical profiles and no reconciliation follows from confirming it.

<a id="src-date-range-interpretation-overlap-refusal"></a>
#### `interpretationOfYear` as an overlap operand and its placed-year calendar check, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: both models authored and every condition decided with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the probe artifact also records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty before and after every command.
- `route`: one Base-Year-2020 UTC model declares `dd.MM`+dash three times (no interpretation, `FROM`, `TO`), `yyyy-MM-dd`+slash three times (no interpretation twice, `TO` once), and carries six persisted bound-equality rules plus one overlap control; a second model declares two `dd.MM`+dash fields and one `FROM` field with no Base Year. `rule check` decides 15 overlap conditions and 5 bound/equality conditions; `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` over five documents on both codegen strategies, which agreed on every row.
- `admission-claim`: an `interpretationOfYear`-bearing declaration is **refused as an operand of either overlap operator**, reported as `MVK_INVALID_DATE_RANGE_FORMAT` against the composite declared format (`'TT.MM-TT.MM (von ist Referenzjahr)'`). Ten refused conditions cover both sides of `DateRangesOverlap`, both the scalar and the list side of `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps`, the yearless `dd.MM` and the year-bearing exact `yyyy-MM-dd` profile, and the unconfigured model. Five admitted controls isolate the key as the cause: the same operators, the same profiles, and the same models admit every interpretation-free counterpart, including the unconfigured yearless pair.
- `scope-claim`: the refusal is confined to the two overlap operators. `StartOfDateRange`, `EndOfDateRange`, and `==` are admitted on the same interpretation-bearing declarations, under both the yearless and the exact profile, so the gate is not a blanket refusal of the declaration.
- `endpoint-claim`: the endpoint years are now locally measured rather than peer-pinned. Six persisted bound-equality rules over `01.11-28.02` fire exactly as `FROM` start `01.11.2020`, `FROM` finish `28.02.2021`, `TO` start `01.11.2019`, `TO` finish `28.02.2020`, while the two crossed controls stay silent. An ordered `01.11-30.11` fires only the `FROM` start row, so neither reading moves an endpoint of a non-wrapping range even when it starts in the anchor month.
- `leap-claim`: each endpoint's calendar reality is checked in the year it is placed in. `01.11-29.02` under Base Year 2020 reports `datumBereichFormatFalsch` on the `FROM` field, whose finish would be a February 29 that 2021 does not have, and is valid on the `TO` field, whose finish lands in leap year 2020 with its `TO` start row firing. The two fields carry the identical stored text in one document.
- `empty-claim`: an empty interpretation-bearing field fires no bound-equality row, so an absent value terminates before endpoint placement rather than being completed to a default year.
- `integrity`: model `b6ce50aa6b923eb8efcaacce95c6d3f19fac00971d639e948f9c341a0240e75e`; unconfigured model `aca8f877012ca714f7edd212e3435ff9adb7bb2297ca4a09bd07720683eafea1`; request `ffdc8f8ea00c5666a9b9d42b4a381ea89bb84a95142ee89ede28a885bbbe6fa9`; artifact `a03386414f72605b671357d8f37f45d8f20f8743296b1660c4b461be41abaef2`. The artifact pins only the model path, so the model was re-observed unchanged after the last declaration was added and both runs produced byte-identical rows.
- `corroboration`: the CLI's own `field add` schema states the direction and the Base-Year dependence of the key in the same terms. That is peer documentation rather than a measurement, and it is recorded only because it agrees with the rows above.
- `limit`: one Base Year, one time zone, one wrapping stored value plus one leap variant, and the formal and bound channels only. No comparison, computation-target, or `FirstFilledValue` row under an interpretation; no interpretation on `MM`, `MM-dd`, `MM` with the empty separator, `yyyy`, or `yyyy-MM` beyond those pairs accepting the declaration; and no measurement of what a wrapping value means on the admitted comparison route.
- `sync`: locally originated. The overlap refusal and the placed-year calendar check are both new to the canonical clause and carried outbound by [`SPEC-2026-08-23-01`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-23-01--an-interpretationofyear-declaration-is-refused-as-an-overlap-operand). The endpoint years themselves are no longer an outbound item: they only upgrade this project's own provenance from peer-pinned to measured.

<a id="src-date-range-year-interpretation"></a>
#### Wrapping yearless ranges and `interpretationOfYear`, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: model authored with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `07d578ce6584692e164a958b64b19ad915331b05`; the probe ran at clean `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, recorded as the artifact's producer with state `CLEAN`. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime; the sibling stayed empty throughout. This is the first local measurement of this mechanism, reachable only because the [instrument handback](#src-dmtool-2026-08-22-instrument-handback) shipped the authoring key.
- `route`: one Base-Year-2020 model declares `dd.MM`+dash three times, without an interpretation and with `FROM` and `TO`. `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` over six documents on both codegen strategies; every row agreed.
- `formal-claim`: the interpretation decides whether a wrapping stored range is formally valid at all. With no interpretation, `01.11-28.02` reports `datumBereichNichtGueltig`; with `FROM` or `TO`, the identical stored text reports nothing. An ordered range is unaffected either way. This is a formal-validity flip on the declaration, not a comparison outcome.
- `declaration-claim`: the key is accepted on **every** admitted `(format, separator)` pair, including the year-bearing ones where it cannot apply, so declaration admission does not gate it. This checkpoint measures only configured year placement. The corrected [unconfigured interpretation checkpoint](#src-date-range-unconfigured-interpretation-first-filled-star) shows that validity there also depends on model declaration order, so no configured result transfers to that branch.
- `endpoint-provenance`: the endpoint years were peer-pinned when this checkpoint was written and are now locally measured at the [interpretation-overlap checkpoint](#src-date-range-interpretation-overlap-refusal). `FROM` keeps the start in the Base Year and moves the finish into the following year; `TO` keeps the finish and moves the start into the preceding year, retained by the peer's `WrappingYearlessRangeDiffTest` and recorded at the [2026-08-19 checkpoint](#src-date-range-2026-08-19-reconciliation). A local comparison row could not reach them here, because `dd.MM` against `dd.MM.yyyy` is refused by the component-set gate before any comparison runs.
- `local-state`: Lean models configured `FROM`/`TO` and retains the field-local fail-closed boundary for an unconfigured wrap. The corrected [unconfigured interpretation checkpoint](#src-date-range-unconfigured-interpretation-first-filled-star) excludes that branch from correspondence until model-wide generated-format state has a measured denominator.
- `integrity`: model `daec574be76e3d1ef807abe9dd678f7dc5e78f9897906113b0b418c238654aa8`; request `fc1241965032cf3d17accdc61b69cae759fe7670b098565228bb6a71bfb8efdf`; artifact `f97752d4ab98e6ce53a3a2a60630d93975e4aab4305382391dfcf87bd5d26cb3`.
- `limit`: one profile (`dd.MM`+dash), one Base Year, one wrapping value, and the formal channel only. Later checkpoints separately cover overlap, bounds, comparison, configured computation targets, and the unconfigured direct-star declaration-order discriminator.
- `sync`: locally originated. The canonical clause already states the `FROM`/`TO` endpoint rule and the admitted value set; what it gains is the formal-validity flip, which is recorded in the same clause. It creates no new ledger entry, because the endpoint mechanism it depends on arrived inbound and is already reviewed.

<a id="src-date-range-yearless-group-carrier"></a>
#### Yearless DateRange group carriers, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: model authored with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `07d578ce6584692e164a958b64b19ad915331b05`; the probe ran at clean `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, recorded as the artifact's producer with state `CLEAN`. The sibling stayed empty throughout. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime.
- `route`: one unconfigured model declares a repeatable group of two yearless fields and a fixed group of one `MM` plus one `MM-dd` field. `rule add --dry-run` decides four conditions and `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes five documents on both codegen strategies, which agreed on every row.
- `admission-claim`: the singular operator refuses **every** group carrier with `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`: a starred group, a fixed group, and a fixed group beside a starred one are all refused even though each expansion is entirely DateRange. The plural operator instead admits a group carrier on its list side, including a yearless group in an unconfigured model.
- `runtime-claim`: an admitted group carrier contributes every yearless declaration in its recursive expansion at every instantiated row. A scalar `01/06` fires against a row's first member `04/09` and, on a different document, against the second member `05-01/07-31` while the first is disjoint; it fires against a second row's member when the first row misses entirely; and a disjoint expansion does not fire. An empty scalar terminates before the list is read, so the scalar-first rule is unchanged by a group carrier. Every firing is VALUE, since no filter is attached.
- `integrity`: model `269ded4f19ad41f7ff2e325a47565863d972495625738fd2cb4769ed5588e97d`; request `61f43a06247ef4e161d5db860a4a8e1aaaad0348fec6788fba2e17e7fcd151df`; artifact `4c4f37059d754466810d272dcf9d68071f23fae719ca994bda0a05c68732439b`.
- `limit`: one repeatable level and one fixed group, both with two-member expansions. No nested group, no mixed-kind expansion, no empty expansion, and no filtered group carrier. The singular refusal rows carry a Kernel diagnostic; the plural admission rows do not exercise a mixed year class inside one expansion.
- `sync`: locally originated and consistent with the existing account, which already states the plural group carrier's recursive expansion and the singular group refusal. This measurement supplies the Kernel rows behind both in the unconfigured yearless domain and creates no new ledger entry.

<a id="src-date-range-starred-yearless-overlap"></a>
#### Starred yearless DateRange overlap, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: model authored with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `07d578ce6584692e164a958b64b19ad915331b05`; the probe ran at clean `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the artifact records as its producer with state `CLEAN`. The sibling stayed empty throughout. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime.
- `route`: one dmtool-authored unconfigured model declares a repeatable group with maximum four rows carrying an `MM`+`/` field and an exact ISO field. `rule add --dry-run` decides four conditions, and `:adapter:kernelProbe` observes `validateFull` over seven documents on both codegen strategies. Every row agreed across strategies.
- `admission-claim`: a plain starred yearless operand is admitted with no Base Year, both as the sole operand and beside a direct operand in the same group, and so is a starred exact operand. The plural operator still refuses a starred *scalar* with `MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_DATE_RANGE_COMPARISON`, which confirms the existing local account of that shape.
- `runtime-claim`: the flattened star list compares yearless labels under the ordinary any-pair rule. With the sole star operand and error field outside the group, `01/06` beside `04/09` fires, `01/03` beside `06/09` does not, the touching pair `01/06` beside `06/09` fires, and `01/02` beside `02/02` fires, so February is reached with no year. One filled row alone does not fire, and a filled row beside an empty row does not fire, so an empty row is a skipped slot rather than a missing operand.
- `runtime-claim`: the per-row scalar-plus-star shape shows the documented any-pair consequence in this domain. With the error field inside the group, a list-internal overlapping pair fires at **every** iterated row including rows whose own cell is empty, because the star operand alone already contains the overlapping pair; with no internal pair each filled row still fires through its own self-pair. The group is iterated to its declared maximum, so a two-row document reports four addresses in the first case and two in the second.
- `integrity`: model `3e2c1bb3ca28281c21d73dd39fc13741a05e3a1708b97b2c0e57db6c6c0bc245`; request `23791cdd201fad4b4dc755d75d9909b12da1428014220636e1ccf232e23ab971`; artifact `01872e06fb70c65be5834d3db7c3803005f599656035d139b7a0283bb428767e`.
- `filter-claim`: a filtered star is admitted on this route too, under `Having` with either an absolute or a relative per-row operand, and a firing that reached the filter-bearing operand is **OMISSION** rather than VALUE. With a filter keeping both rows, an overlapping pair fires omission; dropping one side of the pair, keeping only a disjoint pair, or dropping every row each yields no firing. The unfiltered star beside it keeps firing VALUE on the same documents, which separates the polarity rule from the selection rule.
- `filter-integrity`: the filtered rows use the same model; request `f5e38b803504e3f983930557ef36002531d8627e5b9d4a9854d20a7e31feee45`, artifact `3f10cf746b034ceec1758e6adca160a8f9c867f5f77d44dd685ebffa7d3762a9`.
- `limit`: plain and filtered stars only. No group carrier, no nested or multi-level repetition, and one repeatable level throughout. The per-row scalar-plus-star rows are measured but not modeled: that shape needs the rule's own iteration context, which this capsule does not supply, so only the sole-star operand is implemented.
- `sync`: locally originated, and consistent with the existing any-pair account rather than correcting it. The clause already states any-pair over one growing set and closed intervals; this measurement extends its denominator into the unconfigured yearless domain and creates no new ledger entry.

<a id="src-date-range-plural-year-class"></a>
#### Plural DateRange overlap year class and fragment operands, measured locally 2026-08-23

- `revision`: models authored with the source-matched launcher at clean a12-dmkits `07d578ce6584692e164a958b64b19ad915331b05`; both `:adapter:kernelProbe` runs executed at clean `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, which the artifacts record as their producer with state `CLEAN`. The earlier revision is an ancestor of the later one and the sibling stayed empty throughout. dmtool `0.13.0`, kernel `30.8.1` built and runtime.
- `route`: `rule add --dry-run` decides nine `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` conditions against an unconfigured model and a Base Year 2024 copy, each declaring the exact ISO profile beside `MM`, `MM-dd`, and `yyyy`. Two probe requests then observe `validateFull` on both codegen strategies for six configured and three unconfigured documents. Every row agreed across strategies.
- `syntax-claim`: the authored form is `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps(scalar In list)` with no parentheses around the list. A comma in place of `In`, and a parenthesized list, are both `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`. The operator catalog's exact-id entry carries this in `constraints` and `validExample`; the substring-search view does not.
- `admission-claim`: the plural operator applies the same uniform-year gate as the singular one, over scalar and list together. Unconfigured, an exact scalar beside a yearless list operand is refused `MVK_DATE_WITH_AND_WITHOUT_YEAR` in either direction, while `yyyy-MM-dd` beside `yyyy` or `yyyy-MM` is admitted in either direction. Under Base Year 2024 every one of the same conditions is admitted, including the mixed pairs and a `yyyy` scalar against an `MM` list operand.
- `admission-claim`: an unconfigured all-yearless plural pair, `MM` scalar against an `MM-dd` list operand, is admitted, so the gate is a year-class gate rather than an exactness gate on this operator too.
- `runtime-claim`: a Base-Year-completed fragment participates on either side and the exact operand keeps its own year. `2024-06-01/2024-06-30` against `06/06` fires with the fragment as scalar or as list operand; the same range in 2023 does not fire, and a disjoint month does not fire.
- `runtime-claim`: a `yyyy` scalar decides by its own year, firing against `06/06` at `2024/2024` and not at `2023/2023`. A filled fragment scalar whose only list operand is empty does not fire, so the scalar-first termination rule is unchanged by a fragment scalar.
- `runtime-claim`: the unconfigured all-yearless plural route compares yearless labels exactly as the singular route does. `01/06` overlaps `06-15/06-20`, is disjoint from `07-01/07-05`, and `01/02` overlaps `02-29/03-05`, so February reaches day 29 where no year decides leapness.
- `integrity`: models `c676d9c1a4efb885836d0ecaa4f4f42bd68bd21a6d77222781ce248457564e2a` and `dd5fec5778cef62e84ae72483dba3d27327d426aa00a3903e4abe14faaea25a2`; requests `6dbc759007855b33f3b2ba87a52f30fb0bdb027c9f6432f5eb4712602d71036c` and `7ef6125b2a00524debcbfac0692a63c12722ebe5b9205c6aabc23036a31b2983`; artifacts `23a3a8bb2629d80892f1666f131d0dad4fd0196c1032b6614d6f750d941f4325` and `57e3a3a7f65fe8a57d3e8a93eda147388a2a95623c2de9c254499b121790392a`.
- `limit`: one-element authored lists throughout, so no row separates scalar-versus-list from any-pair, and none exercises the matched-container polarity rule. Direct stored operands only, one filled value per field, ordered ranges only. No starred, filtered, group, or repetition list operand; no empty or malformed operand beyond the single empty-list row.
- `local-state`: both halves are implemented. The configured half lives with the plural owner and the unconfigured all-yearless half with the yearless owner, which now exposes a scalar-versus-list entry point beside its any-pair one; the two share one generic first-match scan and its no-UNKNOWN law.
- `sync`: locally originated and consistent with the clause the singular gate already carries, which states the gate over any overlap operand list rather than per operator. [`SPEC-2026-08-22-01`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-22-01--date-range-overlap-admits-by-year-class-and-a-base-year-lifts-the-gate) already carries that correction and is extended by this measurement rather than superseded, so it creates no second entry.

<a id="src-dmtool-2026-08-22-instrument-handback"></a>
#### Reviewed 2026-08-22 instrument handback and its two Kernel measurements

- `revision`: a two-batch inbound letter answering both 2026-08-22 feedback notes. Every cited revision was confirmed reachable and an ancestor of a12-dmkits `07d578ce6584692e164a958b64b19ad915331b05`, where this project re-verified the authoring surface with a source-matched launcher and a clean sibling worktree.
- `revision-retraction`: Batch 1's header revision `5d4cd47db` is wrong for its own first row and the letter retracts it. The CLI authoring surface landed four commits later at `fc26c38b1`; the modelkit layer and the empty-token measurement are reproducible from `6d9a97a7b`, diagnostic multiplicity from `566edd6f1`, and the envelope changes from `60b770867`. A checkout at `5d4cd47db` would return `RK_UNKNOWN_ARG` for the authoring row, so pin each claim to its own revision rather than to the letter's header.
- `diagnostic-route`: `rulekit.validate.laws.DiagnosticMultiplicityLawsTest`, published in the `diagnostics` contract of `dmtool schema result`; the peer's note is KF192.
- `diagnostic-claim`: a static diagnostic list counts neither defects nor gates, in either direction. One condition carrying both a comparison defect and an unreferenced error field reports the comparison code alone; the same condition with a *missing* error field reports two codes and suppresses the comparison. Two controls fire each code alone, so the single-code row is suppression rather than absence, and the two-code row travels the whole path intact, which places the suppression in the Kernel rather than in the tool.
- `diagnostic-limit`: two multi-defect pairs on one fixture. That establishes that suppression and code duplication both occur, which is enough to stop a consumer counting; it is not a general law about which gate wins, and no wider sweep was run. The peer's own precedence hypothesis is explicitly quarantined as unrun and is not recorded as behavior.
- `local-consequence`: this project's own [mixed-year measurement](#src-date-range-mixed-year-overlap) survives the claim. Its first pass did use a non-operand error field, so eight rows carried a second live objection; every retained row was re-run with the error field referenced by the condition, and the retained claims assert a refusal plus the observed code rather than exclusivity of the objection.
- `interpretation-route`: `field add`, `field modify`, and `typedef add` accept `interpretationOfYear` inside the `dateRange` block from `fc26c38b1`; `field read` and `typedef read` echo it in **both** the JSON contract and `--text`, and `model diff` reports a change to it. `field read` registers no bespoke compact view, so its `data` goes through the generic indented walker that emits every key it meets.
- `interpretation-claim`: the admitted set is exactly `FROM` and `TO`, confirmed locally at `07d578ce6`: both are accepted and persist, `STANDARD` and an empty token are each refused at the CLI boundary with `RK_INVALID_VALUE` naming the admitted set, an omitted key is accepted, and the resulting model stays Kernel-valid. This lifts the [2026-08-19 admission limit](#src-date-range-2026-08-19-reconciliation) for the value set itself, though not for that revision's all-pair static grid.
- `interpretation-mechanism`: two same-named Kernel enums explain why the wrong spellings tempt. `md-model-api` `IDateRangeType.InterpretationOfYear` is `{FROM, TO}` and only *comments* the subsequent/previous semantics, while a separate `kernel-conversion-java` `InterpretationOfYear` is `{STANDARD, FROM, TO}`. `STANDARD` is therefore expressed by omitting the key, never by passing the token.
- `empty-token-route`: `BoundModel.ofJson`, with Kernel `checkConsistency` behind `ModelLoader`, reproducible from `6d9a97a7b`.
- `empty-token-claim`: a present but empty `interpretationOfYear` is refused at deserialization rather than read as an absent key, so empty and absent stay distinct for this declaration exactly as they do for a cell value. The tempting mechanism points the other way, because the host JSON binder coerces an empty token to null for enums by default.
- `empty-token-limit`: four tokens on one declaration profile, `dd.MM` with dash at Base Year 2026. Not swept across the other seven admitted pairs. This project did not independently reproduce it, because authoring such a model requires bypassing the structured-verb boundary that the CLI gate now closes.
- `instrument-claim`: four requested capabilities shipped and are confirmed usable from here. A `-` argument reads the JSON spec from stdin on the nine authoring inputs, which removes the temp-file-per-field pattern; `model info` emits `fileName` and a computed `fileNameMatchesId`; `schema result` now states that an accepted preview's missing `valid` is *asymmetric* rather than merely absent; and unreadable-input failures moved from stderr to a coded stdout envelope with the exit code unchanged.
- `instrument-limit`: the id-versus-basename finding shipped as reported state rather than the warning this project proposed, because the peer's own demo gate showed a warning firing on copy-to-scratch-name working copies, which is the normal authoring workflow rather than the exceptional mistake. The comparison must also tolerate a double extension, and A12 Tools' handling of `.dm.json` is measured by neither project.
- `envelope-consequence`: a harness keyed on exit status is unaffected, but one grepping stderr for an unreadable model or spec now reads an empty stream. Two prior defects on that path are disclosed and fixed: `apply` and `batch` returned `RK_INTERNAL_ERROR` with exit 70 and a stack trace for a merely absent ops file, and an unreadable *model* was attributed to the *spec* argument on `rule add`, `computation add`, `field add`, and `apply`. Only "could not obtain the bytes" is coded; a file that opened and would not parse keeps its reader's own plain text.
- `withdrawn-finding`: this project reported a `field read --text` divergence that omitted the key. It is **withdrawn as a measurement error of ours**: the 11-line text output was piped through `head -10`, which cut the one line carrying `interpretationOfYear`. The peer could not reproduce it at `07d578ce6`, and neither of the two causes it offered applied, because the field read was the same one whose JSON was pasted. The peer pinned the renderer both directions anyway at `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`, mutation-proven against a renderer taught to skip that key, so the acceptance criterion is locked despite the report being false.
- `overview-omission`: `model describe` and `model report` omit date-family config in **both** channels by design, verified locally: a DATE_RANGE field is reported with `path`, `name`, and `kind` alone, with no format, separator, or interpretation even in JSON. They are structural overviews and `field read`/`typedef read` are the per-field config surfaces, so an orientation step cannot confirm a declaration and "the JSON carries it" is true of `field read` and false of `model describe`.
- `sync`: entirely inbound from committed a12-dmkits revisions, so it creates no outbound ledger entry and none of it returns to its origin as a request. It closes the `interpretationOfYear` instrument request carried by the 2026-08-22 feedback notes; the locally originated [`SPEC-2026-08-22-01`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-22-01--date-range-overlap-admits-by-year-class-and-a-base-year-lifts-the-gate) was filed after this letter and remains pending.

<a id="src-2026-08-23-reconciliation"></a>
#### Reviewed 2026-08-23 a12-dmkits reconciliation and correction batch

- `basis`: inbound a12-dmkits correction letter against base `108d047b787f0e049dcd2f4902f24d7523347a00`. Every short revision in the letter was resolved from the sibling repository rather than extended by inference, and the review was read-only.
- `accepted-revisions`: aggregate compatibility `e56b03591f5c50a9f1d8761f38d7daf1139a6c56`; `DateFromDateTime` `cfdb2c80f79b0da32d9ff2df27fcbb4b1be160d5`; filled-field count `b8d6ce19957c6c4e342f12a53f9b8ccd183918b7`; component-omitting Date input `6b7d065864a1b43437615411eee689b1902a2369`; rule-message authoring and rendering `a552f7d5db2f2f0ed1606bcb0e6b59ef526ca087`; locale presentation `2a1caf46632645db55154948b6745084fffe5ce1`; overlap gates `de5c60cf536ffafc058aa1bdfe02e76fdeb36247`; DateRange endpoint gates `8362ed763416f4cf6c91b7f1347e543f519223eb`; computation gates `18839bfbd2633e94e52281e7a2f038f4e8fcb2ec`; rule-locus and keyed-range gates `f4e607547d850d64485ab795b141772c0b3b1e17`; temporal input family `5afb569a0cf8a5b83de61b8e27e8e871af2acf92`; and the aggregate/keyed READ follow-ups `feb5613f90fe22d039fe7c64104f13f617bbefe5` and `3ec7db80eb7ab698a54b88c2cad754ae5eed2bbb`.
- `instrument-revisions`: standard error-text envelopes landed at `033b8fc2a2fa7ade0290bbfbf091da055b8c18b0`; read-only `rule check --message <locale>=<text>` and declared-locale validation landed at `e27ade2f048bbea8405526ddb823ce84bd427e22`.
- `corrections`: a year-only selected DateRange endpoint compares with a full Date because the direct gate reads year presence; a sibling computation operand selects parallel iteration and is admitted only for matching index-field names, with different names and no index producing distinct classes; clean semantic-index no-match coordinate `-5` is `IIdentifier.UNKNOWN`, readable as no matching row; a doubled category arrow reaches the first suffix's kind and membership gates before trailing syntax; Enumeration `.value` renders the declared value label with stored-token fallback; and an unfilled Number default reads minimum fractional digits.
- `extensions`: the rule error-text checker examines only model-declared locales; the exact locale presentation table reaches Date, Number, DateTime, and DateRange but not Time; the rule operand-locus gate precedes whole-list arity; `DateFromDateTime` is locked under an explicitly declared `Europe/Berlin` model zone; and the aggregate component-set gate admits equal components across DATE and DATE_FRAGMENT kinds.
- `limits`: the letter's mechanism section is explicitly hypothetical and supplies no canonical claim. The dmtool front end deliberately rejects an undeclared `--message` locale even though the Kernel silently leaves undeclared-locale message text unchecked; this is an instrument policy, not a Kernel semantic correction.
- `sync`: all eighteen pending entries are accepted at their own cited revisions. The peer opens no return entry, so this batch updates existing receipts and canonical clauses but creates no outbound ledger request.

<a id="src-date-range-2026-08-19-reconciliation"></a>
#### Reviewed 2026-08-19 DateRange reconciliation and correction batch

- `revision`: all routes below were inspected in clean a12-dmkits at exact reviewed revision `52ce932dc9b32aefe8c1e424d7e56d7749709540`; the sibling worktree remained clean.
- `declaration-route`: [`FieldVerbTest`](../../a12-rulekit/cli/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/cli/field/FieldVerbTest.java) retains the complete 8×4 DateRange `(format, separator)` grid; [`IncompleteDateFormatAdmissionLawsTest`](../../a12-rulekit/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/IncompleteDateFormatAdmissionLawsTest.java) retains the separate seven-format DATE whitelist.
- `declaration-claim`: exactly eight DateRange pairs are admitted: dotted full Date with dash; ISO, `yyyy`, `yyyy-MM`, `MM`, and `MM-dd` with slash; `MM` with an empty separator; and `dd.MM` with dash. The other 24 grid cells are refused.
- `declaration-mechanism`: the peer records that the Kernel keys admission on the concatenation `format + separator + format`, so `MM` with an empty separator is admitted because `MMMM` is a whitelisted combined pattern while every other format's doubled form is refused. A local pair table agrees with that mechanism on the whole measured grid but is a projection of it, so widening the format set is not a matter of adding table rows.
- `split-mechanism`: `DateUtil.splitStringBySeparator` halves a stored token when the declared separator is empty, which is why `MM` with an empty separator is the one declaration reaching that branch. Splitting such a token on the empty string instead would shred it per character and report a well-formed month pair as malformed.
- `split-limit`: the peer's six empty-separator rows (`0609`, `0606`, `0906`, `06`, `06x9`, `1309`) assert Kernel/interpreter parity without pinning either engine's codes, so they establish agreement rather than the malformed and inverted precedence itself.
- `interpretation-scope`: the same wrapping suite declares `interpretationOfYear` on `MM` with slash and `MM-dd` with slash as well as `dd.MM` with dash, and its interpretation-omitted rows are the ones this project consumes: a wrapping range without an interpretation stays refused under every yearless profile.
- `comparability-route`: [`DateRangeComparabilityLawsTest`](../../a12-rulekit/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/DateRangeComparabilityLawsTest.java) checks ten rows through the real Kernel; [`DateRangeFirstFilledProfileDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/DateRangeFirstFilledProfileDiffTest.kt) retains target-owned lexical rendering through both Kernel strategies and the interpreter.
- `comparability-claim`: DateRange comparison and computation-target assignment require matching date-component sets rather than identical lexical profiles. Full dotted and ISO profiles may cross, with output rendered under the target declaration; different component sets yield `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE_RANGE`.
- `construction-route`: [`ConstructedRangeFragmentDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/ConstructedRangeFragmentDiffTest.kt) and [`ConstructedRangeFragmentTest`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonTest/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/ConstructedRangeFragmentTest.kt) retain finish-position completion for `MM`, `yyyy`, and `yyyy-MM`, changed-start and changed-finish controls, `MM-dd`, and semantic-index endpoints.
- `formal-route`: [`DateRangeFormalDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/DateRangeFormalDiffTest.kt) and [`DateRangeSplitFormalTest`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonTest/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/DateRangeSplitFormalTest.kt) retain separator absence, malformed shape, empty endpoints, and the legal empty-separator midpoint split.
- `interpretation-route`: [`WrappingYearlessRangeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/WrappingYearlessRangeDiffTest.kt) retains explicit expected endpoint-year rows for `FROM` and `TO` through the dynamic Kernel route and the interpreter.
- `interpretation-limit`: the same test retains wrapping, nonwrapping, `MM`, year-bearing, and leap-day Kernel/interpreter parity rows without fixed expected outcomes. It does not exercise generated Java, so those rows do not independently establish the no-shift or leap-day claims or static-strategy preservation.
- `interpretation-admission-limit`: the inbound handback reports `FROM` and `TO` admission across all eight DateRange declaration pairs and refusal of `STANDARD`, `PREVIOUS`, and `SUBSEQUENT`, but the cited maintained test retains neither that all-pair static grid nor the three refusal rows.

a12-dmkits revision `8094f6643ef3ad01d40dd6d3675356196374e104` answers this project's unstarred-group reference request and is the basis for [`spec/01`](../spec/01-data-model.md)'s unstarred-group clause. `KF183` and [`UnstarredGroupReferenceDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/UnstarredGroupReferenceDiffTest.kt) own it: an unstarred group operand expands **recursively** into a fired message's `referenced` set with **concrete** coordinates, identically in the presence, entity-list, and count positions, tri-checked dynamic Groovy against generated Java against the interpreter on complete sorted sets, with the starred form re-read on the same model as the wildcarding contrast. Two scope limits travel with it. The measured model is **nonrepeatable throughout**, so a subtree field below a repeatable level deeper than the rule binds has no witness; and the kernel pointer carries a coordinate per group part, including non-repeatable parts at `[1]`, which this project's normalized field-instance pointer deliberately does not represent — the retained distinctions are set membership and concrete-versus-wildcard, which do agree. The same revision closes the two computed-target scale entries by measuring the complete 24-cell operator × position × operand-kind grid plus twelve suppression rows rather than narrowing the earlier claim, and `KF181` now states that grid.

- `claim`: an unstarred repeatable group reports `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` under `Sum` and `NumberOfFilledFields`; the starred form and a nonrepeatable unstarred group are admitted.
- `claim`: expansion kind is carrier-specific: `Sum` reports `MVK_NO_NUMBER`, extrema report `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE`, and `NumberOfDifferentValues` reports `MVK_STRING_ENUM_AND_NON_STRING_ENUM`.
- `scope`: the structural arity, star, and duplicate gates belong to the shared entity-list checker; expansion-kind diagnostics do not transfer between carriers.
- `limit`: the HModel profile has no indirect-overlap witness.
- `limit`: this revision did not measure a star on a nonrepeatable group or `FirstFilledValue`; later checkpoints own those rows.

#### Validator-cardinality and cleared-value checkpoints

a12-dmkits `73bc4a04b6a7f81e619af261f9263115f4b67106` supplies the kernel oracle behind [`spec/06`](../spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md) §A.3's per-**cell** custom-validation cardinality, which until then rested on a single-cell differential plus a peer-internal cache test and therefore could not separate per-cell from per-value. `CustomFieldTypeContextDiffTest.theSameValueInTwoCellsReachesTheValidatorOncePerCell` puts one value in two in-cap rows of a repeatable group with a counting factory and measures two invocations with identical context, two rejections at distinct pointers, and agreement across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter, with the positive control that a value-shaped cache key turns it red at `expected: <2> but was: <1>`. The peer's own prose had drifted to a per-value wording in five places while its implementation was per-cell, which is why the clause now names the discriminator instead of only the rule.

a12-dmkits `06b84701969979a2e5a2b63de7445a6a1d521473` measures what a **cleared** computed instance carries, now stated in [`09-computations.md` §3.3](../spec/09-computations.md#33-what-compute-reports): the value accessor reads present and empty for every clear rather than echoing the erased input, so clearedness is a channel-membership fact and a consumer reading that accessor aliases a clear with a computed empty string. `ComputeResultChannelsLawsTest` owns it, and the scope is now **both codegen strategies** at a12-dmkits `a154cd2adbd78c70e2ae101050b5bb3f8f709668`. The route it took is worth keeping, because it is the cheap half of the discharge rule working in both directions: the first version of that case read the shared dynamic engine alone while the producer's own contract said "for every clear", so this project scoped the local clause to the anchor and returned the mismatch instead of adopting the wider sentence; the peer then measured the second arm rather than narrowing its wording, and both strategies report present-and-empty at the same fixture. The interpreter is not part of this case, so the fact is kernel-strategy-wide and not tri-checked.

<a id="src-boolean-fixed-group-value-count-computation"></a>
#### Boolean fixed-group value-count computation

- `revision`: clean a12-dmkits `cd43c249a88874bf5688a45060e208a0dcd20782` with source-shipped dmtool `0.13.0`.
- `route`: structured dmtool authoring persisted `NumberOfValueInFields(True In TrueFlags)` over a fixed group containing one Boolean and one Confirm declaration and `NumberOfValueInFields(False In FalseFlags)` over a fixed group containing two Boolean declarations; computation readback preserved both exact group operands, the Kernel consistency gate accepted the model, source-shipped `:adapter:kernelProbe` observed `compute` and `validateFull` on dynamic Groovy and generated Java, and read-only `dmtool model compute` exercised the a12-dmkits interpreter over the same four documents.
- `kernel`: 30.8.1 built and runtime; both Kernel strategies return `2`, `1`, and `0` for two matches, one match, and empty input in both computations. With an earlier match followed by malformed Confirm or Boolean content, both targets are absent from computation outcomes and the exact operand formal errors remain reported.
- `separator`: the two-match and one-match rows distinguish group expansion from an empty operand stream or a group path treated as a scalar field. The malformed-after-match row distinguishes formal poison from an ignored group; the empty row is the zero control.
- `peer-divergence`: a12-dmkits authoring, persistence, readback, and Kernel checking are correct, but its interpreter returns value `0` for both computations on all four documents, including the malformed row. The exact source locus is [`numberOfValueInFields`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/eval/ExprEval.kt): it calls `expandGroupStars`, whose stated contract leaves unstarred fixed groups untouched, then the fallback reads the group path as though it denoted a scalar cell. The measured fixed-group result isolates that call from the neighboring typed representation and Kernel route, so this is an interpreter operand-expansion defect rather than a dmtool command-envelope defect.

#### Group-message checkpoint

a12-dmkits `7df9f5570a1b7f7b4e5bfa5118a6c3521c8db320` then closes the message residual this project raised against that batch, in four further tri-checked cases of the same test, which the peer's amendment now pins correctly and whose case counts identify each revision on their own (7, 11, 17, 19, 23, verified here against the file at each). A firing over a starred group operand emits exactly one message at the error field's concrete first row, count and address independent of the duplicate's location, on both rule placements and both error-field loci, every firing VALUE. **The separating structure is the reason to trust the count**, and the first two rows would not have carried it: with the error path resolving above the repeatable group, one evaluation over the operand and a per-row evaluation whose two messages collapse onto a single address predict the identical observation. The third row moves the error field inside the rule's own group, which gives the second account two distinct addresses to report at, and it still yields one message; the fourth places the duplicate wholly inside the operand's second row and the anchor stays at the first. The peer also reports that its prior lock could not have answered this: its tri-check read `any { it.code() == CODE }`, under which one message and two are indistinguishable, so all nineteen earlier rows were structurally blind to the question rather than merely silent on it. Three claim limits, of which the peer's own text states none: every row authors the **starred** form, so the unstarred group operand has no message row; `referenced` membership is untouched; and the reached-`Having` OMISSION contrast is untouched, so VALUE is measured but not separated from its alternative. The peer flagged its "this matches your starred field form" sentence as a comparison against our prose rather than a measurement, and it is stronger than it thought: our field-form account is independently kernel-locked on its side by `FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.starredDuplicateEmitsOneFirstRowMessageWithItsWildcardReferenceInBothPlacements`, so the two forms agree as two measurements rather than as a measurement checked against text. That is agreement, not a shared mechanism, and the clauses stay separate.

#### Authored-path wildcard checkpoint

a12-dmkits `4105b256da528154d1aba851bdc18f96947b3cae`, `d510684807738aac614e0d596ba6871b4715832a`, and `f9f92aaa788221d335538c3f50666da92414b23e` carry the **authored-path wildcard gate**, which is the third gate in the split and the reason `spec/07` now says a group operand and its written-out expansion are two different models. **Three provenance limits travel with it and the first is unresolved.** The cited lock `adapter.MixedScopeStarPreservationTest` **does not exist** at `ebe65be9`, and no file in that tree carries its `Sections*/Answers` fixture, so the three rows themselves were not locatable here; the nearest name present is `MixedScopeFieldFillReadTest`, which is not it. The three revisions do resolve and their subjects are consistent with the claim, the last two being "keep a group operand a group instead of replaying its expansion" and "read a group operand under the two arms that had none". The clause was therefore recorded as the peer's measurement with its row-level evidence unconfirmed, and that marking is now lifted: the rows are asserted again under a live lock, named at the end of this section. The peer's own converter disclosure is the corroborating half, since it reports five symptoms of having replayed the expansion, including emitting an unstarred nested repeatable level and so producing a condition the kernel rejects from a model it had just read as valid.

#### Additional carrier-sweep provenance

Three further rows in that sweep are **deliberately not absorbed**, recorded so a later reader does not mistake the omission for an oversight. The `$` outer-marker gate (`MVK_INVALID_OUTER_ITERATION`, universal and filter-only, correcting the peer's own earlier reading that had taken it for a statement about aggregate operands) and the computation-target reference class (`MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`, whose consequence is that no legal compute spelling of a group-scope-from-a-deep-row discriminator exists, making validation the only carrier that can measure one) are both real static-legality facts that belong to their own clauses rather than to the group-operand paragraph. The peer's three excluded spellings are correctly excluded by its own analysis: each measures a spelling rather than a gate, and each was caught by its own field control failing alongside it. The 2026-08-15 §17 iteration/polarity account was **initially declined here**, because its fixture varied index-keying, index-field identity, and the error field's locus together; the peer then ran the separating witness rather than handing the question back, and the result **corrected this project's own clause**.

**Index-keying is not a gate on the unstarred repeatable group operand; the error field's locus is.** The peer measured it *within one model* built twice and differing in exactly one builder call, `indexField("Sku")` present or absent, so nothing else moves: the two 24-cell matrices over four field-fill quantifiers, both spellings, and three error-field loci are identical cell for cell, and the two inside-the-group loci agree whether or not the error field *is* the index field. [`spec/07`](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md) previously stated the rule as index-keying, with a non-indexed group needing the `*`; that predicted `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` where the kernel accepts, and it is corrected. The provenance is the peer's own: its `KERNEL-FINDINGS` and `KERNEL-SEMANTICS` carried the un-separated conjunct *"only when the group is INDEX-keyed ... and the rule's error field sits INSIDE it"*, which is what reached this project, and one of its own tests over a never-indexed repeatable group had shown locus selection for weeks without being read against the claim. Locked upstream by `UnstarredRepeatableGroupIterationLawsTest`, whose first case asserts the two matrices are non-degenerate before asserting they are equal, so the equality cannot pass vacuously.

**The neighbouring `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` gate is syntactic, and this project's clause had characterized it semantically.** The peer refuted the obvious reading in both directions: `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` is grammatically negative and admitted, `NumberOfFilledFields(G) < 1` carries no negation and is refused, and since that count cannot fall below zero the refused `< 1` and the admitted `<= 0` denote one predicate. So the analysis normalizes nothing and does not decide the condition's truth set. `spec/07` now states it as a kernel-owned refusal with those discriminating cells rather than as a property of what the condition means, which is what the peer recommends and is the only form that is not wrong on `< 1` versus `<= 0`. The peer explicitly declines to reconstruct the syntactic rule, holding the shape of the accepted set as a hypothesis over nine cells.

**The two gates composed on a nested repeatable then produced a row neither predicts**, locked by `NestedGroupOperandGateLawsTest`: an operand starring an outer level while leaving an inner repeatable level bare is refused in all six of its cells, which a consumer composing the gates separately admits, because each gate alone has no objection. The polarity-adjacent refusal tracks the **iterated** level rather than the operand, and a filter on a group operand is refused outright, so it cannot move either gate on this carrier. `FirstFilledValue`'s expansion **kind class** closed in the same batch, locked by `FirstFilledValueFamilyLawsTest`, and its temporal half then went through a retraction worth recording in full, because the peer's first answer was **confounded in the same way this project had just caught elsewhere**. That row compared a DATE declared `yyyy-MM-dd` against a DATE_FRAGMENT declared `yyyy-MM`, with a control of two DATEs both at `yyyy-MM-dd`, so kind and declared format moved together and nothing in the pair could say which the kernel refused. It read here as a genuine per-carrier disagreement with `FieldValuesNotUnique`'s format-keyed gate, and `spec/07` briefly carried a marker saying so. **Separated at `6e482c67`, the carriers agree**: `FirstFilledValue`'s temporal gate reads the **declared format**, so a DATE declared `yyyy` beside a DATE_FRAGMENT declared `yyyy` is admitted across two kinds while two DATE fields at differing formats are refused. The fixture carries both separating pairs and the marker is withdrawn. What survives is the axis lesson rather than the carrier one: a compatible-families table phrased in **kinds** is wrong on any operator whose gate keys on format, and both projects had phrased theirs that way.

The format rule then gained a witness independent of its closest pair, at `96d0cbedceaa31c89fccf1d978fbd171307de45c` and `e9b3d725bb52bcf6c459b8241710262424def16c`. `DATE` beside `DATE_FRAGMENT` leaves open the objection that the kernel treats those two as one type; `TIME` beside `DATE_TIME` does not, and they can share a declared format exactly because a DateTime may be declared time-only. That pair is admitted at one shared format while two `DATE_TIME` fields at differing formats are refused, and `DATE_RANGE` behaves identically once its allowlisted (format, separator) pair is read as the format, so the rule now covers all five temporal kinds rather than the two that happened to be measured.

**The peer's own account of how its wrong draft arose is worth more than the rows and is recorded here rather than in a clause.** Its first version of the second witness concluded the opposite, that `TIME` and `DATE_TIME` each admit exactly one format, that the discriminating rows were therefore unauthorable, and that this project should narrow its clause to vacuous-outside-the-DATE-pair. That rested on probing six and five candidates and generalizing from one acceptance each, while the true set sat enumerated in the peer's own findings from fourteen weeks earlier. Sweeping for the same premise found it in two live places, a published diagnostic corrective that an agent would read as a prohibition and a silent evaluator defect that reported a false format error and suppressed every rule referencing a time-only DateTime field, on a document the kernel validates cleanly. That defect survived behind a completeness argument, a sibling sweep declaring "the two DATE sites are the whole class" while excluding `TIME`/`DATE_TIME` on an unmeasured cardinality. The kernel rows that fell out of the fix are in [`spec/05`](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md), locked upstream by `TimeOnlyDateTimeSurfaceLawsTest` at `b803a5572c74b171219205bb2d04e4888cb0a44b`, and their vacuity control is the part to copy: a table of nothing but refusals is satisfied equally by a field that is simply dead.

**The `MixedScopeStarPreservationTest` citation recorded above as unresolved is now closed:** the peer confirms that test was deleted by `f9f92aaa`, the very revision the section cited, and its rows are asserted directly again by `NestedGroupOperandGateLawsTest.aGroupOperandAndItsWrittenOutExpansionAreNotInterchangeableSpellings`, verified here to carry the group-operand admission, the `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` on the written-out expansion with the inner level unstarred, and the both-levels-starred control. The authored-path wildcard clause therefore no longer carries an `unconfirmed` marking.
