No outbound follow-up remains.

### Inbound partial-relevance, index-identity, and row-domain correction batch

a12-dmkits clean revision `752054077be62be441b119af14efc97c6baf13d9` contains the maintained routes behind the 2026-08-08 consolidated inbound correction to §§8, 9, and 12. The partial-relevance extent split begins at `3ce95a05979c1f6c0b5afe83e83607232bb21dbd` and `0dbc7fc8a0c5e65c3ec33f3f867fdb614f5dd7e1`; [`PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest.kt), [`PartialNestedAggregateDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialNestedAggregateDiffTest.kt), [`PartialNestedWildcardScopeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialNestedWildcardScopeDiffTest.kt), [`PartialMultiStarScopeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialMultiStarScopeDiffTest.kt), [`PartialExtentGateShapeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialExtentGateShapeDiffTest.kt), [`PartialValidationValueListDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialValidationValueListDiffTest.kt), and [`PartialValueListCellRelevanceDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialValueListCellRelevanceDiffTest.kt) separately retain the all-rows universal gate, the starred value-list existential gate, `SumOfProducts` complete-cross-product gate, first-star binding, per-iteration-row scope, and concrete cell filtering. [`PartialRuleGateScopeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialRuleGateScopeDiffTest.kt), [`PartialAtLeastOneScanDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialAtLeastOneScanDiffTest.kt), [`PartialParallelJoinKeyDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/PartialParallelJoinKeyDiffTest.kt), and [`ParallelJoinDuplicateKeyDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/ParallelJoinDuplicateKeyDiffTest.kt) retain iteration-bound error gating, relevance-scoped duplicate compromise, relevant-index join construction and validity, unmatched `-5`, and duplicate exclusion. The original relevance-scoped duplicate landing is `cb75eb3cdfa8363d3b96ce0acd89592d4d8965c8`.

The same current peer revision carries the wider index and row-domain discriminators rather than inferring them from the partial-validation cases. [`NumericIndexKeyDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/NumericIndexKeyDiffTest.kt), [`IndexIdentityCustomTypeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/IndexIdentityCustomTypeDiffTest.kt), [`FirstFilledValueEntitySpecDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/FirstFilledValueEntitySpecDiffTest.kt), and [`SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/SemanticIndexComputeNoMatchDiffTest.kt) retain admission-before-kind-owned identity, collision, selection/join, three-valued presence, authored-pointer identity, descendant-coordinate selection, and computation-overlay payload reads. [`RepeatableRowContentDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.kt), [`ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeTest`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonTest/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeTest.kt), and [`RepetitionNotUniqueKeyOrderDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/RepetitionNotUniqueKeyOrderDiffTest.kt) retain V1-to-V2 predecessor padding, recursive implicit validation child row 1 versus concrete-row consumers, and deepest-key-parent RNU iteration independent of authored key order. These are reviewed inbound corrections already committed in a12-dmkits; they update this repository's canonical clauses and provenance without creating an outbound ledger entry. The partial-extent probes do not establish declared-versus-present completeness, three-or-more-level relevance gates, or skip-versus-UNKNOWN for a non-relevant value-list cell, so those claims remain absent.

## Question-to-locus index

The clause map gets a reader into the right subsystem; this table gets a working agent to the decisive mechanism. Search the question or symbol before starting a new audit. “Decides” names the claim class that the locus can actually establish; cross-layer behavior still follows the composition rule above.

| Semantic question | Decisive Kernel locus | Decides / caveat |
|---|---|---|
| Which DocumentV2 rows and cells physically exist? | `DocumentV2`, `GroupInstanceV2`, `FieldInstanceV2`, `RepetitionsV2` | Immutable physical topology and present instances; not checked semantic validity |
| How is immutable input projected into validation? | `DocumentAbstractRtService`, `ValidationData`, `CheckCommand.formalePruefung`, `FormalChecker` | Model/config selection and eager formal checking; later rule/computation behavior is separate |
| When does a checked cell become validation UNKNOWN or computation poison? | `ValidationCache`, `CalculationCache`, field format definitions, generated read sites | Phase-local read behavior; a generated program or template is needed for reachability/order |
| How are required and unique-index findings staged? | `AutogeneratedRulesService`, `ValidationCommand.processInternalVV`, `MainValidatorController.preliminaryError`, `IndexFieldCache` | Generated finding construction and invalid marking; not authored rule execution |
| What are the three-valued Boolean tables? | `DreiWertBool`, `ValidierungsErgebnis`, condition templates | Local truth/result algebra; template call order is required for short-circuit claims |
| How are empty scalar values compared? | `BedingungsOperatorHelper`, `VkBigDecimal.NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL`, type format definitions | Local empty substitution and comparison behavior; authoring legality comes from checkers |
| How are Number operations authored and lowered? | numeric operation checkers, `CheckOpUtils`, `CompositeOperation` backing bean | Static admission, tree shape, operand order, and emitted operation code; runtime helpers decide numeric results |
| What precision, rounding, comparison, and storage rules apply to Number? | `VkBigDecimal`, `NumberCombiner`, `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche`, `FormatDefinitionZahl` | Runtime arithmetic/comparison/storage mechanisms; reachability and target handling remain separate |
| Which numeric result becomes a target error, clear, or stored value? | `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert`, numeric format checks, `DocumentComputationResultImpl` | Target checking and public result classification across layers; inspect all three |
| How do `RangeAsNumber` and `FieldValueAsNumber` select and convert text? | their parser/checker classes, `RuntimeController`, host UTF-16 digit conversion | Selection/fallback and host digit behavior; declaration legality must be checked separately |
| How are Date/Time/DateTime values decoded and checked? | temporal format definitions, `ValidationDateParser`, Date/Time parser checkers | Field-kind/component admission and decoded values; model-zone instant resolution is separate |
| Which model zone is legal and selected? | `NoMetaModelChecks.checkTimeZone`, `MetaDataValidierungIntern.getTimeZone` | Static zone-id legality and configured zone selection |
| How is a fresh local temporal label resolved? | `DateUtil`, especially strict construction/clearing helpers | Fresh label resolution; do not infer calendar-addition behavior from it |
| How do temporal additions and differences behave? | `BedingungsOperatorHelper`, `GregorianCalendar` call sites, generated temporal operation code | Calendar mutation and completed-period/day behavior; exact emitted operand order needs backing bean/template review |
| How are Date/Time construction arguments ordered? | construction checkers, parse-tree constructors, `CodeGenCreator.createDatumParameter`, operation `.st` files | General authoring/lowering and generated argument order |
| How are String line breaks, patterns, lengths, and legal characters checked? | `FormatDefinitionString`, `PatternUtils`, `LegalCharTester`, String parser/checkers | Local formal and target policy; host matcher behavior remains an injected boundary in Lean |
| What is Enumeration value identity versus category identity? | Enumeration model API, `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`, runtime Enumeration projection | Stored token, category position, and comparability; display labels are not identity |
| How do value-list quantifiers scan operands? | value-list checkers/backing beans, `CheckEntityListenUtils`, generated condition code, `ValidationCache` | Static list shape and generated scan order; maintained differentials decide poison/order seams |
| Which repeatable rows are iterated? | `EntityIterator`, `KontextIterator`, `EbenenIterator`, `GroupFillCache`, iteration `.st` files | Actual-row extent, environment traversal, and loop nesting; do not infer rows from filled cells |
| How are filtered stars and outer correlation applied? | filter visitors, path/checker classes, iterator construction, generated rule code | Static filter discovery and runtime traversal; concrete generated shape may be needed for ordering |
| How is repetition-not-unique evaluated? | RNU checkers, `IterationNotValidVisitor`, generated uniqueness rule path | Key relation, cluster construction, and validation emission; `@From` legality is a separate path check |
| How does ordinary semantic-index lookup work? | semantic-index checkers, `SemanticIndexLevelVisitor`, ordinary indexed-read runtime path | Key/column construction and ordinary match-versus-column timing |
| How does parallel-index suppression/clearing work? | `CalculatedFieldIterationLimitCreator`, `CalculationCache.getIterationValuesForGroups`, `markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated`, `CodeGenCalculatedField.st` | Parallel route participation, suppressed iteration, and post-loop invalid marking; it is not the ordinary semantic-index route |
| How are same-target computations combined? | `CalculationDependencies`, `CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder`, `CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st` | Order-preserving flattening and one first-selected table; selected empty/poison/failure ends the scan |
| What orders distinct computation targets? | `CodeGenCalcBuilder`, `CalculationDependencies`, `TopologicalSortHelper`, `CodeGenCalc.st` | Codegen-time dependency order, deterministic independent tie-break, and cycle rejection; runtime adds no scheduler |
| When is an invalid computed dependency observed? | `CalculationCache` value/presence reads plus generated condition/expression control flow | Read-driven poison only when the access is reached; a graph edge alone does not pre-skip |
| Which stored computed inputs are hidden and which completed values are visible? | `CalculationCommand` stripping and `CalculationCache` calculated-value overlay | Working-view stripping and completed producer visibility |
| How are computation result channels built? | `DocumentComputationResultImpl.init` | Exact pointer partition, source-relative change, cleared/with-errors/without-errors, and `noErrorOccurred` |
| In what order is a computation result applied? | `DocumentComputationResultImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)` | Cleared → with-errors → with-changes on a caller-supplied document; no validation call |
| When is generated computation validation run? | `ValidationModelConverter`, `ComputationAlternativesJoiner`, validation service orchestration | Later validation owns all relevant alternatives; computation execution does not invoke it |
| When is a partial rule skipped? | `rulesDir/CodeGenRuleDefinition.st` in Java/Groovy/JavaScript-VK | Rule-level early return before iteration/condition; `CodeGenRules.st` is only the enclosing pass-through |
| How are validation messages rendered? | message model/API, `FehlerHandler`, `CheckCommand.ersetzeFeldParameter`, errortext grammar | Locale resource lookup and token replacement; computation pointer partition is a later separate mechanism |
| How is a custom condition invoked? | `ApplicationCondition.st`, `MainValidatorController.applikationsBedingung`, `CustomConditionWrapper`, `ICustomCondition` | Generated invocation and host callback boundary; registration/failure policy requires the service/model path |

## Focused cross-layer packets

### Immutable document and checked-input construction

[`DocumentV2`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-document-v2/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/document/apiV2/immutable/DocumentV2.java), `GroupInstanceV2`, `FieldInstanceV2`, and `RepetitionsV2` own physical topology and present instances independently of the model. `DocumentAbstractRtService` selects the exact model and passes a separate [`DocumentProcessingConfig`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/api/DocumentProcessingConfig.java). `ValidationData` projects the immutable document into runtime checking; `CheckCommand.formalePruefung`, `FormalChecker.checkIndices`, and `ValidationCache` establish address-formal ordering and the later value cache. The cache omits ordinary empty cells, so it cannot substitute for the immutable input when placement, group content, requiredness, display, or application is observed.

Generated required/index findings are later stages. `AutogeneratedRulesService`, `ValidationCommand.processInternalVV`, and `MainValidatorController.preliminaryError` own generated mandatory/uniqueness checks and exact invalid marking. Partial validation rebuilds a relevance-selected view rather than filtering a full result after evaluation. Computation’s eager operand prepass is another selected view; it does not redefine the checked document.

Scalar formal-read construction has two reachability-sensitive routes. `IBooleanType`/`IConfirmType` mark their alternative display tokens `@NotInD`; `TypeConverter.convertBoolean`/`convertConfirm` consult them only when `considerNotInD` is true, while A12's supported `ExternalDocumentModelService` construction takes the default false route. `FormatDefinitionJaNein` and `FormatDefinitionJa` therefore receive the fixed lowercase tokens and own the case-sensitive formal codes; neighboring ingress conversion does not change that validation contract. For Number, `ValidationData.getValue` returns a stored `String` unchanged but sends a non-String basic value through `FormatDefinitionZahl.convertFromBasicType`, which strips decimal trailing zeros, reapplies the declared minimum fractional scale, and renders plain text. Every evaluation consumer, including `RuntimeController.feldWertAlsString`, reads that selected text. `IndexFieldCache.normalizeValue` separately converts Number keys for numeric semantic-index identity, and `CheckSemanticIndexImpl` format-checks each literal before code generation.

Stored temporal conversion is non-lenient. `FieldValueConverter` delegates Date, Time, DateTime, and DateRange conversion through the declared formats; `ValidationDateParser` disables leniency. `DocumentJsonAndXmlDeserializationInfoImpl` retains a nonempty value that fails conversion as its exact stored text rather than dropping the placement. These are in-memory input semantics; JSON mapper syntax and permissiveness remain outside the evaluation account.

### Ordered validation and partial rule execution

`rulesDir/CodeGenRuleDefinition.st` in Java, Groovy, and JavaScript-VK owns the filter-bearing partial-rule early return before iteration and condition evaluation. The enclosing `CodeGenRules.st` mainly orders preliminary checking and delegates rule bodies; it is not the source for that skip.

`CodeGenParseTreeHelper.FilterConditionVisitor` discovers filters statically. `ValidationCommand.processTVInternal`, relevance caches, `EbenenIterator`, and the relevant runtime controller methods establish actual-row iteration, per-cell relevance, aggregate extent, and exact error placement. Use a generated program or maintained differential when a claim composes those layers.

### Computation definition, execution, and result

`CalculationUtils.expandCommonPrecondition` distributes a common guard. `CalculationDependencies` groups computations per target and builds the dependency order. `CodeGeneratorParameters`, `CodeGeneratorService`, and `CodeGeneratorParametersWrapper` preserve the supplied computation-information list into grouping; the producer of that original list is not fully traced, so the safe claim is supplied order, not generic “document order.”

`CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder` flattens all rows for one target into one generated method. [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st) makes false guards fall through and makes a selected, poisoned, or failing row end the scan. The continuation after a selected operation is unconditional, including when storage produces no value. Generated validation is separate: `ValidationModelConverter` and `ComputationAlternativesJoiner` retain every relevant mismatch rather than reusing first selection.

`CodeGenCalcBuilder`, `CalculationDependencies`, `TopologicalSortHelper`, and [`CodeGenCalc.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalc.st) establish codegen-time dependency ordering, deterministic independent tie-breaking, and cycle rejection. Runtime adds no scheduler.

`CalculationCommand` owns stripping, eager formal-operand checking, and computation orchestration. `CalculationCache` owns read-driven poison and the calculated-value overlay. Value/presence reads throw only when reached; generated `And`/`Or` can hide an invalid suffix. Calculation-field poison marks the target invalid without adding a message.

The inverse String-to-Number route crosses the same overlay rather than reparsing stale source state. `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkString, …)` stores a selected nonempty checked String through `CalculationCache.addBerechnetenFeldWert` and leaves clean no-value unstored; `RuntimeController.feldWertAlsZahl` obtains its token through `feldWert` and therefore the cache's overridden `getWertXML`, where a completed value shadows the source and an invalid computed target throws before conversion. The numeric conversion's established empty-as-zero rule then applies only when that reached cache read is cleanly empty.

[`DocumentComputationResultImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/a12internal/service/DocumentComputationResultImpl.java) owns the five public projections, exact pointer-based message partition, source-relative change, `noErrorOccurred`, and V2 application order. A value-less local target error has no computed instance to absorb its message, so it remains in the residual channel; inherited poison emits no message. `applyTo` processes cleared, with-errors, and with-changes against a caller-supplied document; validation remains a separate call.

A second unchanged-source probe at that revision admits `/Probe/Rows/Minimum := Min([A], [B], [C])` and the corresponding `Max`, with source scales 0, 2, and 1 and the target at their derived maximum scale 2. A separate real-kernel `computation add --dry-run` discriminator at the same clean revision gives the third operand the uniquely largest scale: source scales 0, 1, and 3. A third unchanged-source probe at that revision closes the nonempty-list lower bound: the real-kernel `model check` gate admits repeatable `Min([A])` and `Max([A])` into a scale-2 target and rejects a scale-1 target with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`. The separate kernel-free `model compute` route confirms the a12-dmkits interpreter's single-source value identity, empty-as-zero, formal-poison clearing, and ordinary target rejection. That runtime result is interpreter evidence only; it is not a dynamic-Groovy or generated-Java observation.

The immediate-literal addressed extension was measured at the same clean a12-dmkits revision with source-shipped `dmtool` 0.10.1 through its structured workflow: `model new`, one atomic `apply`, `model describe`/`field read`, and a caller-tagged `batch` of `computation add --dry-run` preflights, without inspecting or editing raw model JSON. The real kernel accepts repeatable `Min([A], 1.250)`, `Min(1.250, [A])`, `Max([A], -1.250)`, and `Max([A], [B], -1.250)` into a scale-3 target; it rejects `Min([A], 1.250, 2)` with `MVK_TOO_MANY_CONST_FOR_CALC_OP` and the first call into a scale-2 target with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` naming expression scale 3. Persisting the first accepted case to a separate structured output, reading it back, and running `model check` reconfirmed the exact operation with `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The kernel-free `model compute` route corroborated field/literal order, missing Number zero, negative selection, and formal-invalid clearing, while `model eval` located the malformed, above-maximum, and excess-scale source rows exactly. Because each `model compute` item still repeats only the declaration path and omits repetition coordinates, those runtime values remain exploratory interpreter corroboration rather than row-specific retained evidence; exact addressed execution is locked internally in Lean.

The first operation-valued addressed extension was measured through that same structured route and exact clean revision. The real kernel accepts repeatable `Min(Abs([A]), [B])` and `Min([B], Abs([A]))` into a scale-2 target, accepts `Min(Abs([A]), [B], 1.250)` into a scale-3 target, rejects the first call at target scale 1 with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, and rejects `Min(Abs(1.250), [B])` with `MVK_NO_CONST`. Persisting and reading back the first accepted call preserved its exact authored structure, and `model check` returned `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The kernel-free runtime route corroborated negative magnitude, positive identity, missing-Number zero, poison in either field position, post-selection target rejection, and a negative direct-field winner; `model eval` independently located both malformed inputs. The unchanged missing-coordinate limit keeps those runtime rows exploratory, while the static authoring and rejection matrix is real-kernel evidence.

The sibling operand-local rounding probe used that unchanged structured model and exact revision. The real kernel accepts both positions of `RoundDown([A], 1)` beside `[B]`, plus the corresponding nested `RoundUp` and `RoundAccounting`, into a scale-1 target. It accepts a scale-3 literal sibling, rejects the scale-1 expression at target scale 2 with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, rejects `RoundDown(1.250, 1)` with `MVK_NO_CONST`, and rejects places 15 with `MVK_INVALID_DIGIT_FOR_ROUNDING`. Persisted read-back preserved `Min(RoundDown([A], 1), [B])`, and `model check` returned `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The kernel-free floor route corroborated signed ties, missing zero, poison in either position, target rejection, and a negative direct-field winner but retained the same missing-coordinate limit; exact addresses, all three mode distinctions, first-poison cause, and consumer identity remain internal Lean locks.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `27c1e583eae27dfba4beabe885f7d1b25f11a460`, dmtool `0.11.1` measures the first arithmetic-valued addressed extremum operand through the repaired structured route. Real-kernel dry-runs accept `Min([A] + [B], [C])`, `Min([C], [A] + [B])`, and `Min([B] + [A], [C])` at derived scale 2, the matching `Max([A] + [B], [C])`, plus `Min([A] + [B], [C], 1.250)` at scale 3 and the independently admitted literal-bearing child `Min([A] + 1.250, [C], 2)`. The scale-2 expression into a scale-1 target is rejected with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`; self-reference through `[Target] + [A]` is rejected with `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`. Persisted read-back preserves `Min([A] + [B], [C])`, and `model check` returns `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. Row-addressed `DM_INTERPRETER` output separates clean addition selection, a direct-field winner, empty left/right/both, poison in either inner operand, and post-selection target rejection. The result remains cause-blind, so exact inner first-poison ordering is locked internally rather than inferred from `cleared`.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `112169fa4ce71d9b45c0b276e0406e275f70354d`, dmtool `0.11.1` extends the same structured matrix to operand-local subtraction. Real-kernel dry-runs accept `Min([A] - [B], [C])` in both outer positions, reversed inner order, the matching `Max`, a scale-3 literal sibling, and both a literal-bearing and literal-only subtraction child. A scale-2 subtraction expression into a scale-1 target is rejected with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, while self-reference through `[Target] - [A]` is rejected with `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`. Persisted read-back preserves `Min([A] - [B], [C])`, and `model check` returns `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. Row-addressed `DM_INTERPRETER` output separates exact subtraction, a direct-field winner, empty left/right/both, poison class in either inner operand, and post-selection target rejection. Independent Lean execution reproduces those addressed values while additionally retaining the inner first-poison cause that the public `cleared` result does not expose.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `b852a2867bc33d621cb4bee0cc692ffa8db5f042`, dmtool `0.11.1` with built/runtime kernel `30.8.1` closes operand-local multiplication through the same structured route. Real-kernel dry-runs accept `Min([A] * [B], [C])` in both outer positions, reversed inner order, and the matching `Max` into the summed scale 3 for a scale-2 and a scale-1 source, plus a scale-4 literal sibling at a scale-4 target, a scale-0 inner source at scale 2, and the separately admitted literal-bearing `Min([A] * 1.5, [C])` and literal-only `Min(1.5 * 2, [C])` children. The same expression is rejected at both a scale-2 and a scale-4 target with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` naming derived scale 3, so the product's derived scale is exact rather than a lower bound. A scale-matched `Min([Target] * [Z], [C])` is rejected with `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`; an unmatched-scale self-reference never reaches that gate. Persisted read-back preserves `Min([A] * [B], [C])` with an empty `errorCodesToSuppress`, and `model check` returns `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`, so exact summed scale needs no warning suppression. Row-addressed `DM_INTERPRETER` output separates the product winning, a direct-field winner, empty left/right/both as zero, a formally invalid operand as cleared, and post-selection target rejection at a capped target, with `model eval` locating the malformed input at its exact instance pointer. The result stays cause-blind, so inner first-poison ordering remains internally locked.

<a id="src-numeric-extremum-call-boundary"></a>
#### Numeric extremum call boundary

- `revision`: a12-dmkits `700095dfb964af69c8a9f7208673415cf91f7e4f`, reachable from clean current main `89aa03957034de620562eb23a095d878f6547dca`.
- `route`: maintained [`OpListMathConstantLawsTest`](../../a12-rulekit/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/OpListMathConstantLawsTest.java) invokes the real kernel consistency oracle through the existing typed DSL and exact string control.
- `claim`: each `Min`/`Max` call independently admits at most one immediate or bracketed numeric literal; a nested same- or different-selector call is one complete operand and does not donate its literal to the outer budget; flattening the two calls changes admission.
- `limit`: the maintained row establishes static admission at one nonrepeatable root and the peer's retained tree shape. It does not measure repeatable addressed execution, nested selected values, scale projection, first poison, result addresses, partial execution, division, power, or surrounding arithmetic.

On 2026-08-04, dmtool `0.11.1` with built/runtime kernel `30.8.1` closed the literal-bearing arithmetic child and uncovered the capability-aware target gate. Every static verdict below was reproduced identically by a launcher self-reporting `source: 5c0d5f0395a45d4e0199662de097ecc7fb97b843 (clean)`, so the account is anchored to an exact revision. The original run used an unlabelled working-tree build; a12-dmkits `9d10e682` then added the built-from revision and `CLEAN`/`DIRTY` state to `--version` and `manifest.version`, which is what makes this anchor citable at all. Real-kernel dry-runs admit an immediate literal at either inner position of the arithmetic child, and the child's derived scale consumes the literal's **authored** scale: `Min([A] * 1.5, [C])` is accepted at declared scale 3 while `Min([A] * 1.50, [C])` requires 4 and is rejected at 3 naming derived scale 4. The child's literal does not consume the outer list's budget — `Min([A] * 1.5, [C], 2)` is accepted while a second outer constant is rejected with `MVK_TOO_MANY_CONST_FOR_CALC_OP` naming `At 'Min'` — and a constant-only child `Min(1.5 * 2, [C])` plus `Min(1.5 * 2, [C], 3)` are both admitted. Persisted read-back preserves `Min([A] * 1.50, [C])` with its authored trailing zero and an empty `errorCodesToSuppress`, and `model check` returns `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. Row-addressed `DM_INTERPRETER` output shows an empty field times a literal remaining `0`, a malformed source still clearing, and the replaced field's own target rejection disappearing from the row because the literal removed that dependency. The same route then measured the target gate itself: `[A] * 100` and `Min([A] * 100)` derive scale 0 through integer-literal trailing-zero stripping and are accepted at declared scales 0, 2, and 4, while capable `Min([A] * 1.50)` is rejected at declared scale 0, field-bearing `Min([A] * 100, [C])` is accepted only at its exact derived 2, and `[A] * [B]` and `Min([A], [C])` require exact equality. That predicate is the correction carried by [`SPEC-2026-08-04-01`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-08-04-01--the-computed-target-scale-gate-is-the-comparison-predicate-not-equality).

At the same launcher revision, the custom-field-type predicates were measured only as far as this route reaches. Their authored keyword is `Valid`/`Invalid`, not the catalog ids `ValidType`/`InvalidType`. Real-kernel `rule check` admits `Valid(Iban, "IBAN")`, `Invalid(Iban, "IBAN")`, the same call inside a larger condition, and — decisively — `Valid(Iban, "NotARegisteredTypeName")`, confirming that the type name is not validated at model-check time; a Number operand is rejected with `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM_OR_EXT_ENUM`. The runtime half is **not** reachable here: both predicates delegate to the kernel's client-SPI `IPredefinedType`, a12-dmkits' operator catalog documents that a kernel runtime with no validator for the named type fires **both** polarities so they are not complements, and the kernel-free interpreter instead declines the construct — `model eval --strict-custom` refuses with `unknown predefined type "IBAN"` naming each affected rule. The interpreter is therefore honestly silent rather than contradicting the catalog, and this project has no route to the kernel runtime branch.

At launcher revision `5c0d5f0395a45d4e0199662de097ecc7fb97b843` (clean), dmtool `0.11.1` with kernel `30.8.1` measures the complete `FieldValuesNotUnique` clause, which `spec/07` canonizes but no Lean owner implements. Real-kernel `rule check` admits the plain field list over two and three Number fields of differing scales (`0` and `2`), the starred single-field form `FieldValuesNotUnique(/Probe/Rows*/Key)`, and a mixed direct-plus-starred list; it rejects a single operand with `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`, a mixed Number/String list with `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`, and an operand list that omits the error field with the ordinary `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`. On the persisted rule, `DM_INTERPRETER` `rule eval` then separates every runtime facet the clause names: scale-0 `5` against scale-2 `5.00` **fires**, so equality is typed across declared scales; distinct values pass; two empty operands pass, confirming that empties are skipped rather than compared; one empty operand passes; and a malformed operand yields `suppressed` with `suppressedBy` naming the field and its formal error code. The runtime half is interpreter triangulation, not kernel runtime evidence. Two stored-Enumeration operands are likewise admitted while any Enumeration/String mix is refused in either order, so the single-declared-kind rule holds across kinds rather than being String-specific. The firing-locus account that this probe left open — the authoring skill claimed per-row iteration while `model eval` fired exactly once with a declaration-shaped pointer — is **resolved inbound**, and the once-per-scope reading was correct; see the aggregate-message paragraph below.

a12-dmkits revision `b5aba1ec60ab9ec6b99fd831f91e12e65e3eb0d6` is the reviewed inbound revision behind the 2026-08-04 aggregate-message reconciliation, and its root fix is `bbbbf48ea8480a3f0bfc5274251c148cc7958d54`. Starred `FieldValuesNotUnique` is aggregate-style: for `Count = [7,5,5]` both dynamic Groovy and generated Java emit exactly **one** message at the error field's concrete first-row address `/Order[1]/Items[1]/Count`, with the wildcard reference `/Order[1]/Items[0]/Count`, invariant under repeated-group versus parent placement. A reached `Having` retypes that firing to OMISSION even when every retained value is filled, an unfiltered firing is VALUE, `Having` field operands join the reference set, and `CurrentRepetition` contributes none. [`FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.starredDuplicateEmitsOneFirstRowMessageWithItsWildcardReferenceInBothPlacements`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest.kt) covers both placements, both filter shapes, cardinality, polarity, error pointer, and reference content across both kernel strategies; [`StarOnlyIterationScopeDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/StarOnlyIterationScopeDiffTest.kt) is the independent starred-`Sum` control proving the wildcard reference is an aggregate law rather than a uniqueness special case, and [`GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/GroupStarValueAggregateDiffTest.kt) plus [`OuterCorrelationDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/OuterCorrelationDiffTest.kt) decide recursive group-star expansion and the concrete-outer/wildcard-inner split. That reference channel is a **structural projection of the checked condition**, not a read trace. The peer revision is inbound reviewed provenance, so its accepted canonical corrections create no outbound sync-ledger request. Its measured fixture instantiates three of `repeatability` 50 rows, which is what establishes that an uninstantiated declared tail does not escalate the unfiltered VALUE polarity.

At launcher revision `b5aba1ec60ab9ec6b99fd831f91e12e65e3eb0d6` (clean), dmtool `0.11.1` with kernel `30.8.1` independently separates the polarity branch this project implements. `DM_INTERPRETER` `model eval` over four rows keyed `7, 9, 7, empty` emits exactly one unfiltered message typed `VALUE_ERROR` with the wildcard reference `/Probe[1]/Rows[0]/Key`, so neither a skipped empty cell nor the uninstantiated tail of a `repeatability`-8 group escalates it; a sibling rule filtered to the first three rows, whose retained `7, 9, 7` are all filled, emits one `OMISSION_ERROR`; and a filter retaining `9, 7, empty` does not fire, so the filter changes the compared set. Real-kernel `rule check` admits both filtered rules, so the filtered starred form is statically legal. The polarity and reference halves are interpreter triangulation against the inbound differential above rather than independent kernel-runtime evidence. The same launcher's `batch --observations` reproduces the admission matrix — two Strings, two stored Enumerations, and two differing-scale Numbers accepted; either kind mix rejected `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`; a single direct operand rejected `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`; the starred slot and its filtered form accepted — as a byte-identical artifact across repeated runs, with `rule check` supplying the real-kernel verdicts.

a12-dmkits revision `dd0bcadf33a5df081fa8ac32ac49ba439bc35d3d` is the reviewed inbound revision that answers the three 2026-08-04 outbound entries, all now `accepted`. It reproduced every reported verdict for the `FieldValuesNotUnique` admission matrix and the computed-target scale gate against the real kernel rather than accepting them as reported, and added two controls this project had not required: `NumberOfDifferentValues` over the same String/Enumeration mix is **accepted**, which is what establishes that the two operators' kind gates genuinely differ rather than that the kernel refuses the mix everywhere; and for the scale gate, two derived-scale-4 operations differing only by a field reference flip at one declared scale, which shows the gate reads capability rather than the scales. Every scale rejection is `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` alone, asserted as a complete code list. The peer's own computation prose was the error and is corrected; this project's clauses were confirmed accurate. Owners: `KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md` §9 and §11, `KF173`, `KF174`, `FieldValuesNotUniqueAdmissionLawsTest`, `ComputedTargetScaleLawsTest`, and the operator catalog, whose new uniqueness constraints, invalid example, CI-executed rejection probe, and `NumberOfDifferentValues` contrast were verified present in the local launcher.

The same revision settles the custom-field-type `Valid`/`Invalid` observable that [`spec/05`](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md) already described. Across both kernel strategies, an unregistered type name on a **filled** String field makes both polarities fire as ordinary rule messages typed `VALUE_ERROR` at the concrete error address, with nothing raised; an **empty** field fires neither and raises nothing; and the two distinguishable registration states — no factory supplied, and a factory present that returns nothing for the name — are indistinguishable in every cell. `UnregisteredPredefinedTypeObservableDiffTest` pins that matrix with the non-complement property asserted separately, and `PredefinedTypeValidityDiffTest` now asserts the nature of the observable rather than the presence of a code. The **causal mechanism is unresolved on both sides** and must not be modeled: kernel runtime source reads as though the double firing were impossible, since the two operator methods share one value-specified gate and then differ only by one comparison against *Valid* while the validator lookup raises on an empty resolution, and the peer confirmed the Groovy lowering reaches those methods and found no covering `catch` or fall-back factory. The named settling probe is to dump the generated program and follow the actual call. This project's local account is therefore deliberately observable-only. Launcher provenance for the local verification: built at `dd0bcadf` from a dirty tree, with the two commits that followed being documentation only.

The same forms' **static** gate has a separate provenance. Kernel `30.8.1` parser check `CheckPredefinedTypeValidityConditionImpl` at pinned kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66` requires a field operand, no wildcards, a value validation, a String/Enumeration/extensible-Enumeration declared type, and a nonempty unquoted type name; it never consults the host registry. a12-dmkits' own `MVK-LEDGER.md` records the two resulting rejections independently as kernel-delegated laws — `MVK_NO_EMPTY` for the empty name and `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM_OR_EXT_ENUM` for the operand kind — so this is a second-implementation corroboration rather than a local-only reading, and it needs no outbound request. It is **not** a kernel measurement: no `rule check` probe has exercised either rejection from this project, because the local launcher and its sibling checkout were both dirty when the clause was written. The Lean elaboration therefore implements only the nonempty-name gate; [`SG7`](SEMANTICS-GAPS.md#sg7--string-pattern-and-custom-field-completion) owns the unmodelled operand gates.

a12-dmkits revision `ddaf2e13` is the reviewed inbound revision answering the two 2026-08-05 outbound entries, and it carries one correction this project did not ask for and had wrong. **A formally unavailable operand of `FieldValuesNotUnique` is skipped exactly like an empty cell and does not suppress the rule.** Measured across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter at full `field | type | referenced` message identity, on Number and on Date operands: a duplicate before or after an unconvertible cell still fires `VALUE_ERROR`, an unconvertible direct operand before the duplicate still fires, and — the separator — **two equal unconvertible values produce no message at all**, because neither enters the comparison. That last pair is what distinguishes skipping from suppressing; the peer notes its own published suppression claim had never been separated from evidence either, since the only case behind it has no duplicate once the unconvertible value leaves the comparison, so "does not fire" reads the same under both accounts and the lock asserted only a boolean. The interpreter defect this exposed was the more serious of the two fixed at `ddaf2e13`, because it made a clean-room engine **fail to fire rules the kernel fires**. Also corrected: the positional filter flag follows a value that was *actually compared* rather than one that is merely specified, so a present-but-unconvertible cell in a filtered operand does not escalate the polarity.

The same revision refutes the scope of this project's operand-admission entry. It established BOOLEAN and DATE_RANGE as kind refusals but did not measure CONFIRM; the clean follow-up below adds CONFIRM to that class. TIME, DATETIME, DATE_FRAGMENT, and CUSTOM are admitted. Among temporal operands the gate requires **one identical declared format string** and keys on the format rather than the kind, with a DATE declared `yyyy` beside a DATE_FRAGMENT declared `yyyy` accepted across kinds while two DATEs differing only in format are refused. The mixing gate reads a comparability category: `{String, Custom}`, `{Enumeration}`, `{Number}`, `{Date, Time, DateTime, DateFragment}`. Custom beside String is therefore accepted and "one declared kind" was never the rule. Gate order is observable as a pre-emption with the pre-empted code asserted **absent**, which is what places Time and DateTime inside the temporal category. Two incidental model-level facts came with it: several plausible declarations are refused by the kernel before any operator gate is reached (`HH:mm` TIME, `dd.MM.yyyy HH:mm:ss` DATETIME, `" - "`-separated DATE_RANGE, `MM.yyyy` DATE_FRAGMENT all draw "The date format is invalid"), and no differing-format TIME or DATETIME witness was known as of `ddaf2e13`, so their same-format rows could not separate format from kind. Finally, the peer refuted a rival mechanism rather than leaving it standing: this project's source reading that the comparison domain is selected by whether the first operand is a Number field predicts an order-dependent stored-text comparison, and cross-scale equality was measured to hold in both operand orders, so **no order-selected comparison mode is observable**. That reading survives only as an implementation note. Upstream surfaces: `KERNEL-SEMANTICS` §9 rewritten, `KF173` amended and `KF175` added, `IF247` corrected and `IF249` added, a 25-case admission-law tier, a 15-row ordered-scan differential, and an operator-catalog corrective whose trigger probe and advertised repair both execute in CI.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `cd41ea94b470a190f7d766ea6d7adf26b6ba74cf`, dmtool `0.12.1` with built/runtime kernel `30.8.1` rechecks the Number and token `FieldValuesNotUnique` admission gates in a 24-case matrix. Two Numbers of scales 0 and 2, two Strings, and two stored Enumerations are admitted; a direct singleton reports `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`, including BOOLEAN and CONFIRM singletons that prove arity precedes kind; Number beside String, Enumeration, or Date and String beside Enumeration, Number, or Date report `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`; and BOOLEAN or CONFIRM report `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED` once arity is satisfied, including a same-kind pair. A three-operand category mismatch beside BOOLEAN reports the kind code with the mixing code absent whether BOOLEAN precedes or follows the mismatching operand. Accepted Number and String representatives were persisted, read back structurally with their exact `FieldValuesNotUnique` conditions, and survived `model check`, all `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The CONFIRM rows correct the previous "only BOOLEAN and DATE_RANGE" scope; they make no runtime claim.

A follow-up probe at the same launcher revision `5c0d5f0395a45d4e0199662de097ecc7fb97b843` (clean) establishes that a repeatable Number computation needs **no** field reference at all. `Min(1.5 * 2)`, `Min(1.5 * 2, 3)`, the literal-only `Min(1.25)` and `Max(1.25)`, and even a bare `1.5 * 2` are accepted into a scale-2 target, while `Min(1.25, 2)` is rejected with `MVK_TOO_MANY_CONST_FOR_CALC_OP` because the enclosing call still admits only one immediate constant; the constant-only list derives scale 1 while retaining multiplicative-constant capability, so it is padded up to declared scales 2 and 4 and rejected at 0. Persisted read-back plus `model check` return `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. Row-addressed `DM_INTERPRETER` output then computes the fieldless `Min(1.5 * 2)` once per instantiated row — three rows, three `3.00` values, including an entirely empty row — so repetition comes from the computed target's own declaration rather than from any source reference.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `27c1e583eae27dfba4beabe885f7d1b25f11a460`, an unchanged source-shipped probe over an unindexed repeatable group admits `/Probe/Rows/Sum := [A] + [B]`, `/Probe/Rows/Difference := [A] - [B]`, and `/Probe/Rows/Product := [A] * [B]` where `A` has scale 1 and `B` scale 2. Addition and subtraction target maximum scale 2; multiplication targets summed scale 3. Re-targeting the product to scale 1 is rejected with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`. The `DM_INTERPRETER` route reports exact row-addressed `4.75`, `2.25`, and `4.375` for `3.5` and `1.25`; an empty operand contributes zero in its authored position, both empty operands produce zero, and either malformed operand clears all three targets. The addressed capsule delegates each node to the scalar precision-50 arithmetic owner; this direct-field packet adds no independent precision-rounding separator.

At the same clean a12-dmkits revision, an unindexed repeatable `/Probe/Rows/Quotient := [A] / [B]` into a scale-2 target is rejected without suppression: the kernel reports `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` with derived right-hand scale `?`. The same generated model is kernel-valid when its computation declares the documented `errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES"]`. The public interpreter then reports `10 / 4` as padded `2.50`, empty divided by `5` as `0.00`, both zero-divisor shapes and either formal-poison position as cleared, `10 / 3` as errored `3.333333333333333` through the warning-suppressed no-fit store, and fitting `10 / 2` as target-rejected `5.00` under an inclusive maximum of `3`. The public route omits row coordinates and collapses domain invalidity with clearing; the exact addressed Lean matrix keeps domain invalidity, inherited poison, target errors, source-relative clearing, and valueless-error pointers distinct. Maintained `ComputedNumberDigitOverflowDiffTest` separately locks the suppressed scalar quotient across both dynamic kernel strategies, generated Java, and the interpreter.

At the same clean a12-dmkits revision, an unindexed repeatable `/Probe/Rows/Result := [Base] ^ [Exponent]` with a scale-2 base, scale-0 exponent field, and scale-2 target is rejected without suppression with `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` and accepted when the computation suppresses that code. Changing only the exponent field to scale 1 remains rejected with `MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`, proving that result-scale suppression does not bypass exponent admission. The public interpreter reports `1.50 ^ 2 = 2.25`, missing-base and missing-exponent rows as `0.00` and `1.00`, both missing as `1.00`, `0 ^ -1` and `2 ^ 1001` as cleared, exponent endpoints `±1000` as `1.00`, `1.23 ^ 2` as no-fit errored `1.5129`, `2 ^ 2` as target-rejected `4.00` under maximum `3`, formal invalidity in either operand as cleared, `2 ^ -1` as `0.50`, and `0 ^ 0` as `1.00`. The public route omits row coordinates and collapses domain invalidity with formal poison; the exact addressed Lean matrix retains those causes, target-error classes, source-relative clearing, valueless-error pointers, and exact rows. Maintained `DecimalPrecisionParityDiffTest` and `PowerDomainFailureComputationDiffTest` separately lock staged positive and reciprocal-first negative power, runtime bounds, domain projection, both kernel strategies, generated Java where applicable, and the interpreter.

The dmtool-derived numeric observations above were reassessed after the output-contract fixes at clean a12-dmkits revision `27c1e583eae27dfba4beabe885f7d1b25f11a460`, dmtool `0.11.1`, with built/runtime kernel `30.8.1`. Structured current-version dry-runs reproduced the String, Enumeration/category, range, root `Abs`, all root Round modes, direct and literal/wrapper extrema, direct addition/subtraction/multiplication, unsuppressed division/power, and exponent-scale static boundaries with `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`; accepted representatives survived persisted structured read-back and `model check`. Current `model compute` envelopes identify `DM_INTERPRETER`, distinguish declarations from instances, and carry canonical repeatable instance pointers; row-addressed runs reproduced the conversion, root unary, direct extrema, multiplication, operand-local extrema, and addition value/outcome matrices. Computation first-cause ordering remains outside that cause-blind runtime projection and stays internally locked. At that revision the structured computation add/modify schema had no `errorCodesToSuppress` input, so dmtool could not reconstruct the legal suppressed division/power models; those runtime claims therefore continued to ride the maintained a12-dmkits differentials named above.

a12-dmkits revision `112169fa4ce71d9b45c0b276e0406e275f70354d` closes that structured-authoring gap. On a fresh dmtool-authored model, an unsuppressed scale-2 division still receives kernel-confirmed `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, while the same division and a scale-0-field-exponent power pass dry-run and persisted `model check` with `errorCodesToSuppress: ["MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES"]`. Structured read-back retains the exact list for both computations; modify dry-run, explain, and model report expose it; an unsupported `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE` is a write-safe `RK_INVALID_VALUE` rejection. `DM_INTERPRETER` then computes the clean representatives `10 / 4 = 2.50` and `10 ^ 2 = 100.00`. This reopens dmtool for purpose-specific suppressed-computation probes; the maintained a12-dmkits differentials remain the owner of the broader division/power runtime matrices and kernel-strategy agreement.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `93771ee24aeb135a88ce9662ee3d88f39e804f45`, dmtool `0.13.0` with built/runtime kernel `30.8.1` measures the direct Number target-reference overlap through one structured eight-row computation dry-run matrix. The target is always the direct left operand: `[/Probe/Target0] * 2` and `[/Probe/Target0] + [/Probe/Source0]` report only `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`; replacing the right operand by the authored-scale-1 literal `1.5` reports only `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`; suppressing only that scale code exposes `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` again. The unrelated `[/Probe/Source0] * 1.5` retains `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, while `[/Probe/Source0] * 2` is accepted. The accepted control was persisted through the structured computation writer, read back exactly, and the whole model remained `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. Every matrix row carries the complete diagnostic-code list, and the sibling stayed clean at the same revision before and after. Subtraction, another operand position, nested or specialized target reads, and precedence against invalid authoring were not measured.

At the same clean a12-dmkits revision and tool/kernel versions, one caller-tagged three-row String-computation observation batch measures ordinary target self-reference. The direct copy `[/Probe/Target]` and root `RangeAsString(/Probe/Target, 1, 1)` each report exactly one `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`; the other-field copy `[/Probe/Source]` is accepted. That control was persisted, read back structurally as `[Source]`, and survived `model check`, all `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The sibling stayed clean before and after. Concatenated, nested, malformed-range, repeatable, and specialized String target reads were not measured.

At the same clean a12-dmkits revision and tool/kernel versions, three ordinary Enumeration-computation observation batches measure plain target self-reference. With closed `A`/`B` domains on every field, direct `[/Probe/Target]` reports exactly one `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`, while `[/Probe/Source]` is accepted. The compatible `FirstFilledValue(/Probe/Target, /Probe/SourceA)` pair reports that same singleton code, while `FirstFilledValue(/Probe/SourceA, /Probe/SourceB)` is accepted. A five-row widening matrix reports the same singleton code for the target second in a pair and first, middle, or last in a triple; the three-source control is accepted. Each accepted control was persisted separately, read back structurally as `[Source]`, `FirstFilledValue(SourceA, SourceB)`, or `FirstFilledValue(SourceA, SourceB, SourceC)`, and its model remained `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`; the sibling stayed clean before and after. The retained direct, initial first-filled, and widening artifacts have SHA-256 `f33164ceecd19570f7f07e8bd77f494ed03f04dbcd615f3d3bcf1fc0c4b95e97`, `674d9ad60e1d91105f0d1e2d08bb8d9028ceba6b72fcd63a4e9077a2d0c3a314`, and `b262097df86cd90baf47767a5a94fac7bd11a16e9475ae5040ac395fe52106ba`. dmtool `0.13.0` labelled each kernel-rejected mutation child a batch failure because its summary fold read `ok` alone, so these claims use the child envelope's independent `outcome:rejected`, `valid:false`, `verification:KERNEL_CONFIRMED`, and exact kernel diagnostic plus each accepted control, not the aggregate summary.

A fourth caller-tagged five-row batch at the same clean revision measures compatible category-decorated target reads. `[/Probe/Target -> Choice]` and both `FirstFilledValue(/Probe/Target -> Choice, /Probe/SourceA -> Choice)` operand orders report the complete singleton code `MVK_ERROR_SEMANTIC_INDEX_OR_CATEGORY_FOR_ERRORFIELD`; the ordinary `/Probe/SourceA -> Choice` source and the two-source different-field `FirstFilledValue` are accepted, all `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The `FirstFilledValue` control was persisted, read back exactly with both category projections, and survived whole-model checking. The ordinary accepted category control was also persisted and the model remained valid, but dmtool `0.13.0` read-back returned `[SourceA]` with `converted:true`, dropping `-> Choice`; that control establishes kernel acceptance through its dry-run envelope but is not used as an exact persistence claim. The retained artifact has SHA-256 `02ffb70e71d5357e69ff1598522a0c34fca8eb11ad43e29ae2cb4fb1cd6c4783`. Mixed category/direct lists, category lists longer than two, incompatible categories, stars, `Having`, semantic indices, preconditions, repeatable computation, and scheduling target reads were not measured.

At clean a12-dmkits revision `4ba31a496faed41107ec95e4207cfc6256872053`, dmtool `0.13.0` with built/runtime kernel `30.8.1` closes the operand-scale axis of the computed-Number gate order. On a fresh model carrying a scale-0 target beside scale-0 and scale-1 sources, one caller-tagged five-row batch reports `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` alone for `[/Probe/Target0] + [/Probe/Source1]`, `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` alone for that same operation under `errorCodesToSuppress`, the same scale code for the unrelated differing-scale mismatch carrying no self-read, and the self-reference code for the matched-scale self-read; the different-field control is accepted. Every verdict is `KERNEL_CONFIRMED` and every rejected row's list is a complete singleton. The accepted control was persisted, read back structurally as `[Source0] + 2`, and left the whole model `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`. The retained artifact has SHA-256 `2ac1313991b91af2ee3941a324816533c0da9e4bc7b642d0fa4adb438ad7f94d`, and a re-capture on an unchanged model reproduces byte-identically; the only row that moves after persistence is the accepted control's own dmtool-level `RK_NAME_EXISTS` name collision, which is a naming artifact of that write rather than a verdict change. The sibling stayed clean before and after. Wider expression shapes, other operand kinds, precondition slots, and any causal account of the ordering were not measured.

The computed-target self-read classes are decided in `CalculationService.testRuleStructure`, which asks `AnalyseService.usesSemanticIndexOrCategoriesForErrorField` about the **whole parsed condition** and reports terminally, before delegating to the canonical-form check that owns the plain class. That control flow is the locus of the cross-slot pre-emption and of the projected class covering an index key as well as an indexed read. It establishes nothing about where the derived-scale gate sits, and no source claim about that position is made here or upstream: the scale-before-self-reference order rests on the measured suppression row alone, because the Kernel's own escape hatch cannot legalize a computation that still reads its target, so what surfaces underneath the suppressed gate is what that gate was reported ahead of. Read the locus to learn why the two self-read classes cannot be decided slot by slot; the measured verdicts remain the evidence.

Repeatable computation additionally uses `CalculatedFieldIterationLimitCreator`, `CalculationCache.getIterationValuesForGroups`, `markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated`, `GroupFillCache.getExistingFieldInstances`, and `CodeGenCalculatedField.st`. These establish static route participation, suppression by invalid index columns, exact target-instance overlays, and locus-dependent coarser invalid marking. Hash-backed internal iteration order is not a public result-order contract.

### Temporal construction and calendar profiles

Date/Time component authoring is distributed across `CheckBaseDateConstructionImpl`, `CheckDatumKonstruktImpl`, `CheckDatumExtractOpImpl`, `CheckDatumAngabeImpl`, the grammar/parse-tree constructors, and `CodeGenCreator.createDatumParameter`. The generated operation templates preserve source and component order; runtime `RuntimeController` supplies field conversion, `BaseYear`, `Today`, `Now`, construction, and component projection. a12-dmkits revision `2f97e4de6ff7f87c6d96b26b021763fda8a1f416` locks positive Date-constant positional decoding and the BMP-versus-supplementary UTF-16 boundary across both kernel strategies and JVM/Node. Revision `f29dc2ad8a74acce6a9f8222262fabf8178a2bbf` adds maintained mixed constant/field admissions and pins the exact Day, Month, complete-Year, Century, width, empty, and uniform-error-code rejection matrix, completing [`SPEC-2026-07-27-11`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#spec-2026-07-27-11--date-component-constants-use-position-specific-width-and-range-gates). Revisions `e75cc6a176f78c9b4b5704bb3a398d4297845644` and `40683600584bd62f741641fa727bdde025114aec` add the serializable Number text-bound declarations and close the Number, pattern-backed String, and exact-`yyyy` Date field matrix across maintained static, dual-kernel, interpreter, JVM, and Node routes; the client-SPI extensible-String arm remains source-characterized because it has no DM-JSON property. Revision `4f211673ad72fc29fd96c07b10ff0f391d90a41b` closes all four `Time(...)` prefix arities, and `ec9ece23abccf9fdf94e569afd9b9da57892c548` closes Date/Time extractor provenance plus `TimeFromDateTime` wall-clock projection. Revision `e6b1d1a8f154c68c80cdbc08148fa651db183735` closes the complete partial-Date suffix and unknown-year behavior.

Computed Time closes through one narrower route. `CheckTimeKonstruktImpl` and `TimeConstructionOperationImpl` assign complete `HH:mm:ss`; the Java, Groovy, and JavaScript [`TimeConstructionOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/TimeConstructionOperation.st) dialects all call `RuntimeController.constructTime` with hour/minute/second in authored order. `DateUtil.createTime` validates the clock on 1970-01-01 in the model zone as a runtime transport value. [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st) then routes the resulting `VkDate` to `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert`; that handler renders through the target `FormatDefinitionDatum`, applies only the basic target check, and stores the exact `HH:mm:ss` string. The parser rejects any other Time field format, so a real constructed clock has no reachable target-local rejection. The transport date and zone must not become Time identity. a12-dmkits revision `0621f088c00eb157fbea2eaae265941f3e84e853` locks full construction and `TimeFromDateTime` under UTC and Berlin through both kernel strategies plus interpreter/JVM/Node application. It also establishes one source-relative result exception without claiming a cause: every clean computed Time enters the changed subset even when its clock equals the source and therefore overwrites a different destination clock.

Computed Date has a separate static target gate. Every generated computation contributes an implicit comparison that reads its target. a12-dmkits revision `456dca65dbd680b6ef33d20a66651f33d565b4d7` measures `FULL` as admitted, every partial precision as rejected with `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`, and attempted suppression as rejected with `MVK_INVALID_SUPPRESSED_WARNING`; its separate full-target case retains the exact pre-1900 attempted Date as ERRORED. Legal partial stored inputs and `ValueAsDate` remain governed by the value-side precision rules above and must not be generalized into partial computation-target admission.

`DateUtil` owns strict fresh local-label construction under the model zone. `CheckDatumAddOpImpl` admits DateTime only for `AddDays` and rejects it for `AddMonths`/`AddYears`. [`DateAddOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/DateAddOperation.st), its backing bean, and `RuntimeMethodNameFactory` preserve source-then-signed-amount order into `RuntimeController`; `BedingungsOperatorHelper` then owns the unit-specific `GregorianCalendar` mutation and completed-period/day differences. The recursive date-operand grammar and checkers admit a generated Add result as the operand of another Add or Difference, and all three template dialects inline that inner call. The outer runtime helper therefore receives the inner `VkDate.getDate()` exact instant and constructs its next calendar from that value; no rendered label or source text intervenes. Direct Date comparison provides an independent observer: `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleicheDATUM` delegates equality and inequality to the exact underlying `java.util.Date` instant. a12-dmkits revision `38b5046bc0c6cc26fcc4982dcb292b7b7822a981` measures a left-hand Date shift against a constructed-Date operand as kernel-authorable when each constant component is a quoted string; a bare numeric component is parsed as an entity name and rejected with `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`. Revision `416065669ad177900e99e1dbec6cb53f3277252e` then locks the retained/derived exact-instant comparison route and its repeated-midnight `false/true/true` day/month/year matrix across dynamic Groovy, static Java, the interpreter, JVM, and Node. `getDatumsDiff` independently ORs `isNichtAngegeben` from both `VkDate` operands, including value-carrying shift results, into both numeric fillability directions. Fresh construction and calendar addition must not be merged, and calendar units must not share an inferred overlap selector. `Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH` carries the source instant's offset into the nominal target, preserves the millisecond field, and re-fits to the actual target offset only when the re-fit preserves the target civil date; travel direction is not the discriminator. Adjacent ±1-day probes do not separate those accounts because their source offset is correlated with direction. `addiereMonate` and `addiereJahre` instead use sign-independent compute-time resolution, normalizing gaps to the post-gap label; the latter also applies both February-28 corrections, and the leap-to-nonleap branch clears the entire clock. `getDifferenzInMonaten` seeds `12 × getDifferenzInJahren`, tests each increasing candidate through a fresh `addiereMonate` copy, and returns the predecessor of the first overshoot; it must not be reduced to one final year/month-coordinate landing because an intermediate gap normalization can already pass the endpoint. For example, `1916-03-30T23:30 → 1916-05-01T00:00` returns zero months when the fresh one-month landing normalizes to `00:30` and already overshoots. The result is also not `12 × completed years`. `getDifferenzInJahren` calls that same year helper before `DifferenceInDays` makes its `365 × years` day jump, after which each day landing carries the offset of its own current instant. Direct OpenJDK 21 controls over the pinned Berlin profile separate long-range overlap, both gap directions, date-preserving fallback, millisecond retention, month/year sign independence, the `369`-day day-mechanism composite, and the `7670`-versus-`7671` long spring-gap year qualifier. Revision `41606566` also measures `AddDays(Now, 1)` millisecond preservation without a peer fix and shows by a 273-case mutation that February-28 clock clearing is not observable through the maintained whole-year consumer, which self-corrects. A short overlap placement likewise self-corrects through the residual day loop and is not evidence for the year resolver. Audit each field mutation independently rather than extrapolating from sign or an adjacent transition.

The kernel accepts a wider legal zone-id domain than the current Lean profile. UTC/GMT and the pinned full historical `Europe/Berlin` profile are established local capabilities; general zone dispatch remains a separate source and representation question.

### Message construction

At kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, [`RuleErrortextChecker`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/service/internal/errortext/RuleErrortextChecker.java) and [`FehlertextUtil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/service/internal/errortext/FehlertextUtil.java) own nonempty text, line/control/encoding checks, even-dollar admission, and parameter checking in that order. [`GrammarOnly_ErrorTextParameterParser.g4`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-service/src/main/antlr4/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/service/internal/errortext/grammar/gen/GrammarOnly_ErrorTextParameterParser.g4) plus [`FullChecks_ErrorTextParameterParser`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/service/internal/errortext/grammar/gen/FullChecks_ErrorTextParameterParser.java) own token grammar, model lookup, and condition-reference legality. The selected Lean capsule deliberately supports only printable ASCII plus one bare nonrepeatable field name; wider legal syntax is explicit insufficiency, not a claimed kernel rejection.

[`FehlertextBuilder`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-rules/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/meta/FehlertextBuilder.java) splits accepted text while preserving empty dollar-delimited segments, normalizes name/value references, and retains `$$` for runtime decoding. The Java, Groovy, and JavaScript [`CodeGenRuleDefinition.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-rules/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/rulesDir/CodeGenRuleDefinition.st) dialects attach messages only in the fired branch. [`MainValidatorController.substFehlertext`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/MainValidatorController.java) performs one split/substitute/join pass: an empty parameter becomes one literal `$`, field name/value parameters resolve once, and inserted bytes are not reparsed.

Field-owned type-format and requiredness messages use distinct routes. [`FieldErrortextChecker`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/service/internal/errortext/FieldErrortextChecker.java) runs the common encoding, line/control, and even-dollar checks before comparing delimited parameters with the locale-fixed words supplied by [`CommonUtils.CONST_FIELD`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-base-utils/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/utils/internal/CommonUtils.java): `field`/`field.value` for US English and `Feld`/`Feld.Wert` for German. The Java runtime helper [`FehlerHandler.transformFehlerText`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/formatdef/error/FehlerHandler.java) performs fixed name replacement and display-value replacement directly, with a null display falling back to the raw supplied value, and contains no visible call to the rule path's empty-parameter decoder. a12-dmkits revision `ed0b0cd9f7110daa354c2404e7b9ca6df4f42fa3` supplies the missing observation: the `en_US` String-pattern producer accepts only lowercase `$field$`/`$field.value$`, rejects `$$` as an empty parameter, and inserts token-looking value bytes once; the measured requiredness producer instead accepts and preserves `$$`. Dynamic Groovy and generated Java agree under the same explicit label-or-name provider profile.

Validation messages are rendered before public result assembly. `FehlerHandler` selects locale resources and `CheckCommand.ersetzeFeldParameter` performs the computation-target field token replacement. Authored `$Field$`/`$Field.value$` template handling is a different syntax and must not be reused for generated target text. Read-only inspection of a12-dmkits [`Message.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/eval/Message.kt) at revision `351bf9f28138783ef7ddc7d8c76db2ab4891a5d8` establishes a current peer gap: its nonempty placeholder regex covers name/value tokens but cannot decode `$$`, so the canonical literal-dollar case still needs an upstream implementation and maintained JVM/Node lock.

Computation produces one formal-message stream. `DocumentComputationResultImpl` partitions it solely by exact error pointer against nonempty computed instances. `PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer` admits concrete coordinates plus wildcard and unknown; `CellAddr` alone cannot represent the full message-pointer domain. Payload text, code, and locale do not affect partition membership.

## a12-dmkits drill route

<a id="a12-dmkits--peer-clean-room-engine--verified-knowledge-a12-rulekit-checkout"></a>

<a id="interpreter-a-peer-clean-room-kmp-evaluator-the-closest-reference"></a>
<a id="adapter--the-kernel-as-oracle-differential-jvm-kernel-linked"></a>
<a id="adapter-the-kernel-as-oracle-differential-jvm-kernel-linked"></a>
<a id="the-doc-set-indexed-by-the-hub"></a>
<a id="corpus-and-the-catalog"></a>
<a id="the-n-drill-down-topology-and-synchronization-provenance"></a>
<a id="what-the-coverage-tells-us"></a>

Use a12-dmkits as maintained knowledge and a kernel-executing test estate, never as the oracle:

- [`docs/SEMANTICS-MAP.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/SEMANTICS-MAP.md) — exhaustive per-clause index;
- [`docs/KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md) — peer canonical prose;
- [`docs/KERNEL-FINDINGS.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/KERNEL-FINDINGS.md) and [`docs/INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md) — evidence-qualified findings and implementation consequences;
- [`docs/RT-SEMANTICS-LEDGER.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/RT-SEMANTICS-LEDGER.md) and [`docs/MVK-LEDGER.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/MVK-LEDGER.md) — kernel-mined runtime/static inventories;
- [`interpreter/`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/) — peer clean-room semantics and common tests;
- [`adapter/src/test/`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/) — kernel-executing laws and differentials;
- [`corpus/`](../../a12-rulekit/corpus/) and catalog semantics facets — retained reusable model/case families where present.

An a12-dmkits interpreter agreement is triangulation. A maintained adapter test that executes the kernel is a Kernel lock. Neither is a project-local retained observation; [`EVIDENCE.md`](EVIDENCE.md) owns that stronger replay boundary.

## Inbound provenance checkpoints

The detailed review chronology is recoverable from Git. These consolidated checkpoints retain exact reviewed routes without an outbound feedback loop. **Every a12-dmkits revision cited here from before 2026-08-10 is receipt history, not a working drill route**, because it no longer resolves in the available checkout while its content is present at HEAD under a different hash. Drill to the named maintained owner instead of the revision, and say which you used. The [ledger receipt-continuity note](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md#receipt-continuity) owns the measurement, the boundary, and the reason no replacement mapping is inferred.

| `22fa19e7229663ee23389be6fbbfed3f26c5fb46` and `4ba31a496faed41107ec95e4207cfc6256872053` | computed-target self-read classification by **reading mode**: the projected class covers an enum category, a semantic index, and the index-**key** position, and pre-empts the plain class within one operation, in either operand order of a two-entry `FirstFilledValue` (whose other entry may be another projection, a plain read of the same field, or a plain read of a different field, all measured), and across precondition/operation slots; the derived-scale gate precedes both classes over the **complete 24-cell operator × position × operand-kind grid** plus twelve suppression rows, measured at a12-dmkits `8094f664` by `theOrderHoldsAcrossEveryOperatorPositionAndOperandKind`, with each expectation derived from the canonical scale rule before the kernel was asked; the earlier six-cell denominator and its unmeasured remainder are retired. Routes: `ComputedTargetProjectedReadLawsTest` and `ComputedTargetScaleLawsTest` over the real-kernel `RuleValidator.check` consistency oracle, asserting each complete emitted code list beside same-model accepted controls (a12-dmkits `KF181`, `KF182`; the corrected `MVK-LEDGER` row). A projection confined to a `Having` filter, incompatible categories, and lists longer than two remain unmeasured |
